# Social Justice Flashcards

> One flashcard per examinable item in the repository Social Justice owners (basic + advanced), plus a concept card per owner file. IDs: SJ-1001 onward by agent block.
> **Verification status:** built from the repository owners with claims checked against Wikipedia and other pages that loaded while building; confirmed items carry a ✅ source tag; unverified details are marked ⚠️. Owner-vs-source conflicts are flagged inside the cards. Time-sensitive facts carry as-of dates from the owner.

---

## SOCIAL JUSTICE — Part 1: Framework, Poverty and Nutrition, Health, Education, Women and Children

> Section note: Prefix `SJ`, block `SJ-1001` onward. Grounded in the repository owners `upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Social-Justice/basic/` and `advanced/` files 01–06. Tags: `(✅ owner NN)` = taken from the owner file; `(✅ Wikipedia: <page>)` = confirmed on a fetched page; `⚠️` = owner-only, memory-only, a conflict, or not re-checked against a dated primary source.

### GROUP — Social Justice, Inclusion and Welfare-State Framework

### Flashcard SJ-1001 — Three dimensions of social justice (distributive, corrective, recognitive)
**Front:** What are the three dimensions of social justice used to evaluate a welfare scheme?
**Definition (learn):** Social justice is pursued along three axes: distributive (who gets what), corrective (remedy for a specific wrong) and recognitive (affirmation of denied identity and dignity). (✅ owner 01)
**Breakdown:**
- Distributive — allocation of resources, opportunities and services; delivered through transfers, reservation, public services, social security.
- Corrective — compensation, rehabilitation, penalty, restoration of a harmed person.
- Recognitive — anti-discrimination law, representation, identity recognition.
- Most vulnerable-group policies need all three at once (owner 01 Section 3 step 4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Framework source is the owner's own analytical structure; no year or figure. (✅ owner 01)
**Links:** SJ-1002, SJ-1003, SJ-1004, SJ-1005, SJ-1016 (Fraser triad), SJ-1006 (capability approach).
**Trap:** Social justice is not only economic redistribution. Redistribution without remedy and dignity can reproduce dependency (owner 01 traps). (✅ owner 01)
**Mains hook:** Use the three-dimension test to judge any scheme for SCs, STs, women or PwD; name which dimension a scheme leaves uncovered. Qualification: redistribution is necessary but not sufficient.
**Back:** *"Who gets it, what is put right, whose dignity is restored — three tests, all needed."*

### Flashcard SJ-1002 — Distributive justice
**Front:** What does distributive justice ask, and how is it implemented?
**Definition (learn):** Fair allocation of resources, opportunities and services; the question is "who receives what?" (✅ owner 01)
**Breakdown:**
- Instruments: transfers (DBT), reservation, public services, social security.
- Contrast with corrective justice (SJ-1003) — distribution is forward-looking allocation; correction is backward-looking remedy.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner table, no year. (✅ owner 01)
**Links:** SJ-1001, SJ-1003, SJ-1010 (universalism vs targeting).
**Trap:** Distributive justice is not the same as equal outcomes; allocation rules can be equal yet fail to reach the disadvantaged (see SJ-1011, inclusion/exclusion error).
**Mains hook:** Distributive measures (PDS, PM-JAY, scholarships) are judged by coverage of the intended group, not only by spending.
**Back:** *"Distribute to the disadvantaged, not just to the eligible list."*

### Flashcard SJ-1003 — Corrective justice
**Front:** What does corrective justice ask, and what are its typical instruments?
**Definition (learn):** Remedying a specific harm; the question is "how is the harm undone?" (✅ owner 01)
**Breakdown:**
- Instruments: compensation, rehabilitation, penalty, restoration.
- Owner example: manual scavenging law combines a ban and penalty (corrective) with rehabilitation (distributive) and dignity restoration (recognitive) (owner 01 Section 5; ⚠️ owner application).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None beyond owner text. (✅ owner 01)
**Links:** SJ-1001, SJ-1002, SJ-1004, SJ-1019.
**Trap:** A ban alone is not corrective justice unless a remedy and rehabilitation follow; prohibition is not eradication.
**Mains hook:** Judge a prohibition law by its enforcement, penalty, and rehabilitation of victims, not by the statute's existence.
**Back:** *"Ban, penalise, rehabilitate — or the wrong persists."*

### Flashcard SJ-1004 — Recognitive justice
**Front:** What does recognitive justice ask, and how does it operate?
**Definition (learn):** Affirmation of denied identities, dignity and voice; the question is "whose personhood is marginalised?" (✅ owner 01)
**Breakdown:**
- Instruments: anti-discrimination law, representation, identity recognition.
- Linked to Nancy Fraser's triad (SJ-1016): misrecognition is itself a distributive barrier.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None beyond owner text. (✅ owner 01)
**Links:** SJ-1001, SJ-1016, SJ-1005.
**Trap:** Recognition cannot substitute for services, assets and employment (owner 01 Core bank).
**Mains hook:** Dignity-based law (transgender, disability, caste) must be paired with material entitlements to change outcomes.
**Back:** *"Recognition opens the door; resources keep it open."*

### Flashcard SJ-1005 — Welfare state (Preamble, DPSP)
**Front:** What is a welfare state, and what gives India its normative foundation for it?
**Definition (learn):** A state that takes responsibility for citizens' minimum living standard through public provision of health, education, housing, social security and poverty relief. (✅ owner 01)
**Breakdown:**
- Normative anchors: the Preamble's "socialist" and the Directive Principles in Part IV (owner cites Articles 38, 39, 41–43, 46–47).
- Not directly justiciable; courts use DPSP as an interpretive aid (owner 01 advanced section 4; doctrine owned by Polity).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner: DPSP Articles 38, 39, 41-43, 46-47. (✅ owner 01)
**Links:** SJ-1006, SJ-1007, SJ-1019, SJ-1013 (residual vs institutional).
**Trap:** DPSP is not enforceable like Fundamental Rights; it gives direction and interpretive guidance, not an individual cause of action.
**Mains hook:** A welfare state must convert DPSP directives into enforceable floors (NFSA, RTE) and accountable services, not announce benevolent schemes.
**Back:** *"Preamble names it, Part IV directs it, courts read it — they do not enforce it alone."*

### Flashcard SJ-1006 — Capability approach: functionings vs capabilities (Sen)
**Front:** Distinguish functionings from capabilities in Amartya Sen's approach.
**Definition (learn):** Functionings are achieved states (being nourished, being literate); capabilities are the real freedoms to achieve those states. (✅ owner 01)
**Breakdown:**
- Shifts evaluation from "what do people have" (income, inputs) to "what can people actually do and be."
- Policy should expand the capability set, not merely deliver inputs (owner 01 advanced section 2).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner, no year. (✅ owner 01)
**Links:** SJ-1007 (Nussbaum), SJ-1008 (Rawls), SJ-1011, SJ-1025 (income vs capability trap).
**Trap:** The same income can produce different capabilities depending on disability, health, social exclusion or location (owner 01 basic traps).
**Mains hook:** Evaluate food, health and education schemes by conversion of inputs into functionings and capabilities, citing dated outcome data (NFHS, ASER).
**Back:** *"Functionings are states achieved; capabilities are freedoms to achieve them."*

### Flashcard SJ-1007 — Nussbaum's central capabilities (ten)
**Front:** Name the ten "central capabilities" in Martha Nussbaum's list.
**Definition (learn):** A threshold list of capabilities a just society must secure for every citizen; a more prescriptive version of Sen's open-ended approach. (✅ owner 01 advanced)
**Breakdown:** owner order:
- life; bodily health; bodily integrity; senses, imagination and thought; emotions; practical reason; affiliation; relation to other species; play; control over one's environment.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Count is ten. (✅ owner 01 advanced section 7)
**Links:** SJ-1006 (Sen), SJ-1025.
**Trap:** Nussbaum's list is fixed and prescriptive; Sen deliberately refuses a fixed list. Do not describe Sen's approach as having ten capabilities.
**Mains hook:** Use the list as a checklist for a welfare audit, noting that a fixed list invites debate about who sets it.
**Back:** *"Life, health, integrity, senses, emotions, reason, affiliation, species, play, control."*

### Flashcard SJ-1008 — Rawls's difference principle
**Front:** What is Rawls's difference principle?
**Definition (learn):** Social and economic inequalities are just only if they are arranged to benefit the least-advantaged members of society. (✅ owner 01 advanced section 7)
**Breakdown:**
- A distributive-justice standard used alongside the capability approach in Mains answers.
- Owner marks this as occasionally referenced; treat it as a standard, not a Social Justice doctrine owned here.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** No year in owner. The owner's Must-Know entry gives the standard statement (greatest benefit to the least advantaged). (✅ owner 01 advanced; ⚠️ concept table tag)
**Links:** SJ-1006, SJ-1009, SJ-1002.
**Trap:** The difference principle concerns inequality that is permitted; it does not demand equal distribution.
**Mains hook:** Test a benefit design by asking whether the least-advantaged gain most from the inequality it tolerates.
**Back:** *"Inequality is just only if the worst-off gain."*

### Flashcard SJ-1009 — Formal vs substantive equality; horizontal vs vertical equity
**Front:** Define horizontal and vertical equity and link them to formal and substantive equality.
**Definition (learn):** Horizontal equity: equals treated equally. Vertical equity: unequals treated unequally in proportion to disadvantage. Formal equality (same rule for all) may entrench disadvantage; substantive equality uses differentiated support. (✅ owner 01 advanced sections 2–3; ⚠️ concept labels)
**Breakdown:**
- Affirmative action operationalises vertical equity.
- Causal chain: formal equality -> substantive equality (differentiated support) -> capability equality (does support expand capability?).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. (✅ owner 01)
**Links:** SJ-1006, SJ-1010, SJ-1021 (2024 GS-I Q18).
**Trap:** Equal inputs are not equal outcomes; formal equality alone is necessary but not sufficient for social justice.
**Mains hook:** "Formal legal equality is a necessary but insufficient condition for social justice" — argue through vertical equity and capability conversion.
**Back:** *"Equals equal, unequals unequal in proportion."*

### Flashcard SJ-1010 — Universalism vs targeting
**Front:** Compare universalism and targeting as welfare design choices.
**Definition (learn):** Universalism gives the same entitlement to everyone; targeting concentrates resources on identified beneficiaries. (⚠️ concept table; ✅ owner 01 cross-check)
**Breakdown:**
- Universalism: lower stigma, lower exclusion error, higher cost, some leakage to non-needy.
- Targeting: efficiency, lower cost, higher exclusion risk and documentation barrier.
- Hybrid designs (universal basic services + targeted top-ups) are the common answer (owner 01 advanced section 6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. (✅ owner 01 advanced)
**Links:** SJ-1011, SJ-1012, SJ-1013, SJ-1024 (hybrid).
**Trap:** Universalism and targeting are not mutually exclusive; many architectures combine them (owner 01 basic traps).
**Mains hook:** Treat it as a design trade-off, not a moral absolute; name the error type it reduces and the one it increases.
**Back:** *"Everyone-easy or the-needy-only: each leaks, each excludes, hybrids hedge."*

### Flashcard SJ-1011 — Inclusion error vs exclusion error
**Front:** Define inclusion error and exclusion error for a targeted scheme.
**Definition (learn):** Inclusion error: a non-eligible person receives the benefit (leakage). Exclusion error: an eligible person does not receive it (under-coverage). (✅ owner 01 advanced)
**Breakdown:**
- Targeting reduces inclusion error and tends to increase exclusion error.
- Universalism reduces exclusion error and increases cost and inclusion error.
- ONORC and deduplication aim to reduce both in NFSA (owner 02 advanced; ⚠️ application).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. (✅ owner 01)
**Links:** SJ-1010, SJ-1030 (NFSA exclusion), SJ-1031 (ONORC).
**Trap:** Students confuse the two; the question asks which error a design is more prone to, not which is "bad."
**Mains hook:** Relate the error type to the measure used (Aadhaar seeding, documentary proof) and to migrant/homeless exclusion.
**Back:** *"Inclusion: the wrong person gets it. Exclusion: the right person does not."*

### Flashcard SJ-1012 — Rights-based vs charity-based welfare
**Front:** What separates a rights-based welfare entitlement from a charity-based benefit?
**Definition (learn):** Rights-based welfare: a citizen holds a statutory or constitutional claim with a remedy where the law provides one. Charity-based: support is state beneficence, withdrawable at discretion. (⚠️ owner 01 concept table)
**Breakdown:**
- Owner example: NFSA converts PDS from discretionary scheme to statutory entitlement (owner 01 section 5; ⚠️).
- A statutory label alone does not guarantee delivery (owner 01).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** NFSA 2013 — see SJ-1028. (✅ owner 01 cross-link)
**Links:** SJ-1005, SJ-1027, SJ-1010, SJ-1074 (RTE as entitlement).
**Trap:** A right on paper can still be a "paper promise" when fiscal constraint is ignored (owner 01 advanced section 6).
**Mains hook:** Judge an entitlement by its enforceability, grievance route and funding, not by its name.
**Back:** *"Claim with a remedy versus favour at discretion."*

### Flashcard SJ-1013 — Residual vs institutional welfare model
**Front:** Distinguish residual and institutional models of the welfare state.
**Definition (learn):** Residual: the state intervenes only when family or market fails (means-tested, stigmatised). Institutional: the state provides universal services as a citizenship right (de-stigmatised, costlier). (⚠️ owner 01 advanced concept table)
**Breakdown:**
- India is a hybrid: universal entitlements (RTE, NFSA) layered on targeted schemes (owner 01 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. (✅ owner 01)
**Links:** SJ-1005, SJ-1010, SJ-1074.
**Trap:** Targeted does not automatically mean residual; the test is whether the entitlement is a right.
**Mains hook:** Place a scheme on the residual–institutional spectrum and argue the trade-off in stigma and cost.
**Back:** *"Residual waits for failure; institutional guarantees the citizen."*

### Flashcard SJ-1014 — Social protection floor (ILO Recommendation 202, 2012)
**Front:** What does the ILO Recommendation 202 (2012) establish, and what are its four guarantees?
**Definition (learn):** A globally endorsed social-protection floor: basic income security through the life cycle and access to essential health care. (✅ owner 01; ✅ owner 01 advanced for four guarantees)
**Breakdown:** four guarantees (owner 01 advanced section 7):
- (a) access to essential health care
- (b) basic income security for children
- (c) basic income security for working-age persons unable to earn
- (d) basic income security for the elderly
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Adopted 2012 by the International Labour Conference. (✅ owner 01)
**Links:** SJ-1015 (R202 vs Convention), SJ-1005.
**Trap:** It is a Recommendation, not a Convention; it creates no automatic domestic legal obligation.
**Mains hook:** Use the floor as a benchmark to test whether India's health and income-security schemes cover all four groups.
**Back:** *"Health, children, working-age unable, elderly — the floor under everyone."*

### Flashcard SJ-1015 — ILO Recommendation vs ILO Convention (binding trap)
**Front:** Why is ILO Recommendation 202 not binding, while a ratified ILO Convention is?
**Definition (learn):** Conventions bind a state only on ratification; Recommendations are non-binding guidance. (✅ owner 01 advanced traps)
**Breakdown:**
- R202 — non-binding global norm adopted by the International Labour Conference.
- Convention — binding on ratifying members (contrast with C138 and C182 in SJ-1074/1075, child labour).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** R202 adopted 2012. (✅ owner 01)
**Links:** SJ-1014, SJ-1139.
**Trap:** "ILO Recommendation 202 is a binding convention" is the owner's listed trap.
**Mains hook:** Distinguish soft standard-setting (R202) from ratified obligations (C138, C182) when evaluating India's commitments.
**Back:** *"Recommend guides; ratify binds."*

### Flashcard SJ-1016 — Fraser's redistribution–recognition–representation triad
**Front:** What three dimensions does Nancy Fraser's triad use to analyse justice?
**Definition (learn):** Redistribution (material), recognition (status and identity) and representation (political voice). Owner names the triad as the Core evidence bank. (✅ owner 01 Section 11A, owner cited)
**Breakdown:**
- Misrecognition operates as a distributive barrier (owner 01 Core bank).
- Recognition cannot substitute for services, assets, employment.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner, no year. ⚠️ (outside owner reading; widely attributed to Fraser's work, not fetched).
**Links:** SJ-1001, SJ-1004, SJ-1017.
**Trap:** Students often list the triad but do not connect it to the Mains question's evidence.
**Mains hook:** Use the triad for a 20-mark comparison with Rawls, Sen and Ambedkar (owner 01 Core 20-mark route).
**Back:** *"Money, esteem, voice — none alone is justice."*

### Flashcard SJ-1017 — Ambedkar's warning: political equality amid social and economic inequality
**Front:** What warning does the owner attribute to Ambedkar on political equality?
**Definition (learn):** Political equality (one person, one vote) does not by itself remove social and economic inequality. (✅ owner 01 Core evidence bank; ⚠️ not re-verified against the primary speech text)
**Breakdown:**
- Owner uses this to justify the move from formal to substantive equality.
- Pairs with DPSP and reservation logic (owner 01 Core).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner gives no date; do not assign one. ⚠️
**Links:** SJ-1009, SJ-1016, SJ-1019.
**Trap:** Do not attribute to Ambedkar a claim the owner does not record; quote the warning only as the owner phrases it.
**Mains hook:** A 15-mark answer on formal equality can open with this as the classical argument.
**Back:** *"Vote equal, life unequal — the Constitution must bridge both."*

### Flashcard SJ-1018 — Minerva Mills (1980) and DPSP as interpretive aid
**Front:** What is the owner's cited doctrinal use of the DPSP in Minerva Mills (1980)?
**Definition (learn):** The DPSP is used as an interpretive aid for social justice rather than as a directly enforceable claim. (✅ owner 01 advanced section 4 cites the case)
**Breakdown:**
- Holding detail belongs to Polity; this card records only the owner's social-justice use.
- Pairs with SJ-1005.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Minerva Mills, 1980 (owner). ⚠️ holding wording not re-verified.
**Links:** SJ-1005, SJ-1019.
**Trap:** Do not state the case holding in Mains beyond "DPSP as interpretive aid" without Polity support.
**Mains hook:** Cite as the judicial route by which DPSP shapes welfare interpretation.
**Back:** *"Directives guide the reading of rights."*

### Flashcard SJ-1019 — Part IV DPSP social-justice directives
**Front:** Which Directive Principles does this owner rely on for social justice?
**Definition (learn):** Articles 38, 39, 41, 42, 43, 46, 47 direct the state toward distributive, livelihood and group-specific goals. (✅ owner 01 basic section 6)
**Breakdown:**
- Art 38 — social order; Art 39 — economic principles (livelihood, equal pay, child welfare); Art 41–43 — work, education, public assistance, living wage; Art 46 — weaker sections; Art 47 — nutrition, public health (owner-cited; article-level content from memory ⚠️ where not owner-stated).
- Art 46 is the owner's example of recognitive and distributive combined (SCs, STs, weaker sections).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner list. (✅ owner 01)
**Links:** SJ-1005, SJ-1018, SJ-1051 (Art 47 health), SJ-1026 (nutrition).
**Trap:** Art 21A (RTE) is a Fundamental Right, not a DPSP; Art 47 is the DPSP link for health and nutrition.
**Mains hook:** Map each Directive to the scheme that implements it and note whether it is justiciable (it is not).
**Back:** *"38 social order, 39 livelihood, 41–43 work-education-assistance, 46 weak, 47 health."*

### Flashcard SJ-1020 — Growth-development paradox PYQ route (2019 GS-II Q6, 2023 GS-I Q16)
**Front:** Which two Mains PYQs route to the growth-vs-human-development theme?
**Definition (learn):** 2019 GS-II Q6 (high growth alongside the lowest human development indicators; examine; 10 marks; 150 words). 2023 GS-I Q16 (why human development lags economic development; 15 marks; 250 words). (✅ owner 01 PYQ block)
**Breakdown:**
- Core route supersedes older Advanced pointers (owner 01 Section 11A).
- Use the capability lens: growth is an input; HDI and MPI measure outcomes.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Years and marks per owner ledger. (✅ owner 01)
**Links:** SJ-1006, SJ-1007, SJ-1025.
**Trap:** HDI averages conceal gender, caste, regional and intra-household inequality (owner Core bank).
**Mains hook:** State a qualified thesis: growth expands resources; human development needs institutions that convert resources into capability.
**Back:** *"Growth buys inputs; institutions make capabilities."*

### Flashcard SJ-1021 — 2024 GS-I Q18: affirmative action benefits not reaching the underprivileged
**Front:** What does the 2024 GS-I Q18 ask, and what answer structure does the owner prescribe?
**Definition (learn):** "Despite comprehensive policies for equity and social justice, underprivileged sections are not yet getting the full benefits of affirmative action envisaged by the Constitution. Comment." (250 words; 15 marks; owner-ledger wording). (✅ owner 01)
**Breakdown:**
- Owner route: identify the barrier; separate entitlement from access; test outcomes, not scheme lists.
- Cross-link: capability approach (SJ-1006) and vertical equity (SJ-1009).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024, GS-I, Q18. (✅ owner 01)
**Links:** SJ-1009, SJ-1011, SJ-1022.
**Trap:** Do not answer with a list of schemes; the question asks the implementation gap.
**Mains hook:** Structure as constitutional promise -> instrument -> identification/enforcement/delivery gap -> dated outcome -> correction.
**Back:** *"Promise, instrument, gap, outcome, fix."*

### Flashcard SJ-1022 — 2025 GS-II Q16: inequality in resource ownership and the paradox of poverty
**Front:** What does the 2025 GS-II Q16 ask, and what loop does the owner give?
**Definition (learn):** "Inequality in the ownership pattern of resources is one of the major causes of poverty. Discuss in the context of 'paradox of poverty'." (250 words; 15 marks; owner-ledger wording). (✅ owner 02)
**Breakdown:**
- Loop: unequal land, assets, credit, digital access -> unequal opportunity and bargaining power -> low capability and income -> weak saving and asset formation -> inherited inequality.
- Test against joblessness, public-service access and implementation failure; separate productive-asset reform from consumption relief.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025, GS-II, Q16. (✅ owner 02)
**Links:** SJ-1021, SJ-1006, SJ-1023 (asset-loop diagram in owner 02 advanced).
**Trap:** Consumption relief is necessary but not transformative; do not treat cash transfers as land reform.
**Mains hook:** Discuss productive-asset reform (land records, tenancy security, credit, skills, digital access) as the structural answer.
**Back:** *"Who owns the asset owns the next year's income."*

### Flashcard SJ-1023 — Paradox-of-poverty loop (owner diagram logic)
**Front:** What is the self-reinforcing asset-capability loop behind the "paradox of poverty"?
**Definition (learn):** Unequal ownership -> unequal opportunity and bargaining power -> low capability and income -> weak saving and investment -> inherited ownership inequality. (✅ owner 02 advanced section 10)
**Breakdown:** The loop closes back at ownership; breaking it requires asset redistribution, not only income support.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner diagram. (✅ owner 02)
**Links:** SJ-1022, SJ-1006.
**Trap:** Monetary poverty can fall while asset inequality persists; a fall in income poverty is not the end of the loop.
**Mains hook:** Use the loop to explain why a 2025-type question needs asset-side remedies.
**Back:** *"Ownership begets bargaining power begets ownership."*

### Flashcard SJ-1024 — Hybrid design: universal basic services plus targeted top-ups
**Front:** Why do Social Justice owners prefer a hybrid welfare architecture?
**Definition (learn):** A hybrid gives universal basic services (health, education) and targeted transfers or affirmative action for the most disadvantaged. (✅ owner 01 basic traps; ⚠️ design rationale)
**Breakdown:**
- Universal layer: lowers stigma and exclusion error.
- Targeted layer: concentrates resources where needed.
- Owner examples: RTE and NFSA universal-style entitlements layered on targeted schemes (owner 01 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. (✅ owner 01)
**Links:** SJ-1010, SJ-1013, SJ-1011.
**Trap:** Universal and targeted are not opposites; the trap is an either-or answer.
**Mains hook:** Recommend a hybrid with a named universal floor and a named targeted top-up, and say how each reduces one error type.
**Back:** *"Universal floor, targeted top-up."*

### Flashcard SJ-1025 — Income is not the capability metric (trap)
**Front:** Why is income an inadequate sole measure of social justice?
**Definition (learn):** The capability approach explicitly rejects income or utility as the sole metric; it asks what people can actually do and be. (✅ owner 01 advanced traps)
**Breakdown:**
- Functionings are easier to observe (literacy, IMR) than capabilities; policy tends to target observable proxies (owner 01 advanced section 6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. (✅ owner 01)
**Links:** SJ-1006, SJ-1007, SJ-1020.
**Trap:** "The capability approach measures only income" is the owner's trap.
**Mains hook:** A 15-mark answer on robustness of capability versus income poverty must name the proxy problem.
**Back:** *"Income is an input; capability is what the input makes possible."*

### GROUP — Poverty, Hunger, Food and Nutrition Security

### Flashcard SJ-1026 — Food security vs nutrition security
**Front:** Distinguish food security from nutrition security.
**Definition (learn):** Food security is reliable access to sufficient, safe and nutritious food; NFSA primarily secures calorie and grain access. Nutrition security adds dietary diversity, micronutrients, care practices and the health and sanitation environment. (✅ owner 02)
**Breakdown:**
- Food security: calorie access via statutory foodgrain entitlement.
- Nutrition security: multi-sectoral (diet diversity, micronutrients, care, WASH).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner table, no year. (✅ owner 02)
**Links:** SJ-1027 (NFSA), SJ-1046 (specific vs sensitive), SJ-1048 (trap), SJ-1041 (vicious cycle).
**Trap:** NFSA grain entitlement is not nutrition security; the owner calls this a recurring exam distinction.
**Mains hook:** "Food security is necessary but not sufficient for nutrition security" — argue with NFSA grain versus diet diversity and WASH.
**Back:** *"Calories are food security; diversity, care and sanitation make nutrition security."*

### Flashcard SJ-1027 — NFSA 2013: entitlements and coverage
**Front:** What foodgrain entitlements does the National Food Security Act, 2013 give?
**Definition (learn):** A statutory entitlement to foodgrains: 5 kg per person per month for priority households and 35 kg per household per month for Antyodaya Anna Yojana (AAY) households. (✅ owner 02)
**Breakdown:**
- Priority households: about 75% of rural and 50% of urban population (owner 02 basic section 3).
- AAY: poorest of the poor.
- Coverage is about 67% of population, not universal (owner 02 advanced section 4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** NFSA 2013 (owner). Ministry: Department of Food and Public Distribution (MoCAFPD) at Union level (owner 02 basic section 4). (✅ owner 02)
**Links:** SJ-1028 (price and free-distribution), SJ-1029 (PMGKAY), SJ-1030 (coverage/exclusion), SJ-1044 (Prelims Q25 route), SJ-1012 (rights-based).
**Trap:** NFSA does not cover everyone; "universal coverage" is the owner's listed trap.
**Mains hook:** Use coverage (67%) and exclusion errors to test whether NFSA is rights-based in practice.
**Back:** *"Five kilograms priority, thirty-five for AAY, roughly two-thirds covered."*

### Flashcard SJ-1028 — NFSA statutory issue price vs free distribution (since 1 Jan 2023)
**Front:** What are the NFSA statutory Central Issue Prices, and what is the current practice?
**Definition (learn):** The Act fixes Central Issue Price ceilings of Rs 3/kg rice, Rs 2/kg wheat and Rs 1/kg coarse grains. Since 1 January 2023, foodgrains under NFSA have been distributed free of cost through the PMGKAY merger. (✅ owner 02)
**Breakdown:**
- Statutory ceiling: rice Rs 3/kg, wheat Rs 2/kg, coarse grains Rs 1/kg.
- Current practice: free distribution to AAY and priority-household beneficiaries.
- Union Cabinet extended the free-foodgrain policy through December 2028, owner-cited (roughly 81 crore beneficiaries, outlay about Rs 11.8 lakh crore; ⚠️ outlay and beneficiary numbers are owner-only).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Free since 1 Jan 2023; extension to Dec 2028 (owner). ⚠️ Outlay figure owner-only.
**Links:** SJ-1027, SJ-1029, SJ-1030.
**Trap:** The "Rs 1–3/kg payable" answer is wrong for current practice; the figures are the statutory ceiling only.
**Mains hook:** A scheme's price on paper can differ from practice; cite the date of the change.
**Back:** *"Three-two-one is the ceiling; free since 2023 is the practice."*

### Flashcard SJ-1029 — PM Garib Kalyan Anna Yojana (PMGKAY) and its NFSA merger
**Front:** What is PMGKAY and how is it related to NFSA?
**Definition (learn):** Pradhan Mantri Garib Kalyan Anna Yojana, merged into NFSA from 1 January 2023 so that NFSA foodgrains are distributed free. (✅ owner 02 basic section 2)
**Breakdown:**
- Merger ended the separate payment structure for NFSA beneficiaries.
- The free distribution route is administered through NFSA (owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Merger effective 1 Jan 2023 (owner). (✅ owner 02)
**Links:** SJ-1027, SJ-1028.
**Trap:** Do not describe PMGKAY as a separate ration card or a separate entitlement from NFSA after the merger.
**Mains hook:** Use the merger to show how a crisis-era scheme was converted into a statutory-linked routine entitlement.
**Back:** *"Garib Kalyan folded into the Act, free from 2023."*

### Flashcard SJ-1030 — NFSA coverage and exclusion errors
**Front:** What exclusion and inclusion problems limit NFSA coverage?
**Definition (learn):** Coverage is state-wise identification (priority households and AAY), not universal. Exclusion errors persist for migrants, the homeless and people lacking documents. (✅ owner 02 advanced section 4)
**Breakdown:**
- Coverage about 67% (owner).
- Ghost ration cards (inclusion) versus eligible persons lacking Aadhaar (exclusion) (owner 01/02 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-only coverage ratio; the 75%/50% rural-urban entitlement shares are owner-stated. (✅ owner 02)
**Links:** SJ-1011 (inclusion/exclusion error), SJ-1031 (ONORC), SJ-1027.
**Trap:** NFSA covers about 67%, not all citizens.
**Mains hook:** Argue that portability (ONORC) reduces migrant exclusion but not documentation exclusion.
**Back:** *"Two-thirds in, the homeless and undocumented out."*

### Flashcard SJ-1031 — One Nation One Ration Card (ONORC)
**Front:** What is ONORC and how does it work?
**Definition (learn):** One Nation One Ration Card enables portability of NFSA entitlements across states using Aadhaar-seeded ration cards and electronic Point of Sale (ePoS) machines. (✅ owner 02)
**Breakdown:**
- Technical backbone: NIC and FCI; inter-state data via the IMPDS portal (owner 02 advanced section 4).
- Requires Aadhaar seeding, ePoS roll-out and inter-state data sharing.
- Owner: operational across all 36 States/UTs, with Assam's pan-India completion in June 2022 (⚠️ date not re-verified).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Pan-India completion June 2022 (⚠️ owner-only). (✅ owner 02)
**Links:** SJ-1030, SJ-1027.
**Trap:** ONORC is not a separate card; it is a portability system for existing NFSA cards.
**Mains hook:** Test ONORC against migrant exclusion caused by Aadhaar and ePoS gaps.
**Back:** *"One card, any shop, anywhere in India."*

### Flashcard SJ-1032 — Poshan 2.0 / Saksham Anganwadi (MWCD)
**Front:** What is Poshan 2.0 and which ministry runs it?
**Definition (learn):** The umbrella nutrition programme of the Ministry of Women and Child Development (MWCD) that merges ICDS and related schemes, delivered through anganwadi centres. (✅ owner 02)
**Breakdown:**
- Delivery: supplementary nutrition, growth monitoring, pre-school education, referral.
- Focus: children under 6, pregnant and lactating women; first 1000 days (SJ-1045).
- Poshan Tracker app records growth-monitoring data (SJ-1047).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** MWCD; first-1000-days emphasis (owner). (✅ owner 02)
**Links:** SJ-1045, SJ-1033, SJ-1038 (THR vs hot meal), SJ-1049 (ministry trap).
**Trap:** Poshan 2.0 is MWCD, not Ministry of Education (owner trap).
**Mains hook:** Use Poshan 2.0 as the first-1000-days lever in the vicious-cycle answer.
**Back:** *"Anganwadi feeds the first thousand days."*

### Flashcard SJ-1033 — PM POSHAN (erstwhile Mid-Day Meal)
**Front:** What is PM POSHAN and who administers it?
**Definition (learn):** The school-nutrition programme providing cooked meals to children in government and aided schools (Classes I–VIII). Administered by the Ministry of Education. (✅ owner 02)
**Breakdown:**
- Centrally sponsored with Centre-state cost sharing; calorie and protein norms, kitchen infrastructure, social audit (owner 02 advanced section 4).
- Objectives: attendance, retention, nutritional status.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Classes I–VIII (owner). (✅ owner 02)
**Links:** SJ-1032, SJ-1049, SJ-1041.
**Trap:** PM POSHAN is Ministry of Education, not MWCD.
**Mains hook:** Use it as school-age nutrition in the 2024 Q7 answer.
**Back:** *"Mid-day meal renamed, run by Education."*

### Flashcard SJ-1034 — Stunting vs wasting
**Front:** Distinguish stunting from wasting.
**Definition (learn):** Stunting is low height-for-age in under-5 children, a chronic indicator. Wasting is low weight-for-height in under-5 children, an acute indicator. (✅ owner 02)
**Breakdown:**
- Stunting reflects cumulative deprivation, especially the first 1000 days.
- Wasting reflects recent food shortage or illness; a crisis indicator.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Under-5 age band (owner). (✅ owner 02)
**Links:** SJ-1036 (NFHS figures), SJ-1037 (SAM/MAM), SJ-1045.
**Trap:** Stunting and wasting are not interchangeable (owner trap).
**Mains hook:** Match the indicator to the policy response: chronic deprivation calls for first-1000-days care; acute wasting calls for treatment and feeding.
**Back:** *"Stunting: height, chronic. Wasting: weight, acute."*

### Flashcard SJ-1035 — Anaemia
**Front:** What is anaemia, and which groups does the owner flag?
**Definition (learn):** Haemoglobin deficiency (iron, folate, B12) affecting oxygen-carrying capacity; prevalent among children, adolescent girls and pregnant women. (✅ owner 02)
**Breakdown:**
- Linked to productivity, maternal and child health.
- Anaemia Mukt Bharat strategy (SJ-1039) targets it.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** NFHS-5 anaemia: 67.1% of children aged 6–59 months (owner, see SJ-1036). (✅ owner 02)
**Links:** SJ-1036, SJ-1039, SJ-1040.
**Trap:** Anaemia is not the same as undernutrition; it has micronutrient and infection causes.
**Mains hook:** Target adolescent girls and pregnant women within the first-1000-days frame.
**Back:** *"Low haemoglobin, high risk for mother and child."*

### Flashcard SJ-1036 — NFHS-5 baseline and NFHS-6 under-five indicators (dated)
**Front:** What are the NFHS-5 baseline and NFHS-6 under-five stunting figures?
**Definition (learn):** NFHS-5 (2019–21): 35.5% under-five stunting, 19.3% wasting, 67.1% anaemia among children aged 6–59 months. NFHS-6 (2023–24): under-five stunting 29.3% (owner-reported). (✅ owner 02 for NFHS-5; ⚠️ NFHS-6 figure owner-only)
**Breakdown:**
- Keep each round separate; do not splice indicators from different rounds.
- NFHS round list (NFHS-5 2019–21, NFHS-6 2023–24 marked latest) confirmed on Wikipedia (✅ Wikipedia: National Family Health Survey).
- NFHS-6 release date 29 May 2026 is owner-stated only and was not re-verified (⚠️).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** NFHS-5 2019–21; NFHS-6 2023–24. Owner also cites 715 districts and about 6.79 lakh households for NFHS-6 (⚠️ owner-only).
**Links:** SJ-1034, SJ-1035, SJ-1118 (NFHS-6 child marriage).
**Trap:** Mixing NFHS-5 and NFHS-6 in one baseline; the owner warns against it.
**Mains hook:** State the round, the indicator and the denominator for every NFHS figure.
**Back:** *"Five was 35.5, six says 29.3 — never splice rounds."*

### Flashcard SJ-1037 — SAM vs MAM (acute malnutrition)
**Front:** Distinguish Severe Acute Malnutrition (SAM) and Moderate Acute Malnutrition (MAM).
**Definition (learn):** SAM: weight-for-height below -3 SD, requires facility-based therapeutic care. MAM: weight-for-height -2 SD to -3 SD, managed through community-based supplementary feeding. (✅ owner 02 advanced section 2)
**Breakdown:**
- Both tracked through ICDS or anganwadi growth monitoring.
- Nutrition Rehabilitation Centres for SAM (owner 02 advanced section 7).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** -3 SD and -2 SD to -3 SD thresholds (owner). (✅ owner 02)
**Links:** SJ-1034, SJ-1032.
**Trap:** A child at -2.5 SD is MAM, not SAM; threshold boundaries matter.
**Mains hook:** Link SAM to clinical referral and MAM to community feeding.
**Back:** *"Minus three is severe, minus two to three is moderate."*

### Flashcard SJ-1038 — Take-Home Ration (THR) vs hot cooked meal (HCM)
**Front:** Compare the Take-Home Ration and the hot cooked meal under Poshan 2.0.
**Definition (learn):** THR: dry ration given to beneficiaries to cook at home (for pregnant and lactating women and children 6 months–3 years). HCM: cooked meal served at anganwadi or school. (✅ owner 02 advanced section 2)
**Breakdown:**
- HCM ensures consumption at point of delivery but needs infrastructure.
- THR is flexible but can be diverted within households.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Age bands per owner. (✅ owner 02)
**Links:** SJ-1032, SJ-1033.
**Trap:** Not interchangeable; THR is for home consumption, HCM is consumed at the centre.
**Mains hook:** Choose THR or HCM by the target group, infrastructure and diversion risk.
**Back:** *"Ration home, meal on site."*

### Flashcard SJ-1039 — Anaemia Mukt Bharat and the 6x6x6 strategy
**Front:** What is the Anaemia Mukt Bharat strategy's 6x6x6 framework?
**Definition (learn):** A multi-cause intervention bank for anaemia reduction; the owner records the 6x6x6 frame as the strategy used in its answer bank. (✅ owner 02 Core 11A)
**Breakdown:**
- Routed Prelims demand: 2023 Prelims GS-I Q52 on Anaemia Mukt Bharat interventions and target groups; key not held locally, so no answer recorded (owner 02 PYQ block).
- Fortification addresses selected micronutrient gaps but cannot replace dietary diversity or clinical treatment (owner 02 Core).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2023 Prelims Q52 routed; key unavailable locally (owner). (✅ owner 02)
**Links:** SJ-1035, SJ-1040, SJ-1044.
**Trap:** Fortification is not a substitute for diet, infection control or clinical care.
**Mains hook:** Map 6x6x6 interventions to the lifecycle (adolescent girls, pregnancy, early childhood).
**Back:** *"Fortify, supplement, but never skip the diet."*

### Flashcard SJ-1040 — Fortified rice under NFSA (boundary case)
**Front:** What does the owner say about fortified rice under NFSA?
**Definition (learn):** Government has mandated fortified rice (iron, folic acid, B12) through PDS as a nutrition-specific enhancement within food security. (⚠️ owner 02 advanced section 5, boundary case)
**Breakdown:**
- Effectiveness depends on consumption and bioavailability (owner).
- Owner notes a phased national rollout; no coverage percentage asserted.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None asserted. ⚠️
**Links:** SJ-1039, SJ-1027.
**Trap:** A mandate is not proof of coverage; ask for the rollout date and consumption evidence.
**Mains hook:** Use fortified rice as the example of food-security delivery that targets nutrition indirectly.
**Back:** *"Mandate is not meal — ask who eats it."*

### Flashcard SJ-1041 — Poverty-malnutrition-human capital vicious cycle
**Front:** Trace the poverty–malnutrition–human-capital vicious cycle.
**Definition (learn):** Poverty limits diverse food and care; food insecurity causes calorie and micronutrient deficits; undernutrition impairs development; low human capital reduces earnings; poverty transmits to the next generation. (✅ owner 02; 2024 GS-II Q7)
**Breakdown:**
- Impaired development: cognitive, physical (stunting, wasting), health (anaemia, immunity).
- Intergenerational trap: stunted child -> lower-earning adult -> malnourished offspring.
- Levers: first-1000-days (Poshan 2.0), PM POSHAN, ONORC, women's anaemia, WASH convergence.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024 GS-II Q7 (owner). (✅ owner 02)
**Links:** SJ-1042, SJ-1045, SJ-1046, SJ-1032, SJ-1033.
**Trap:** A narrow NFSA-only answer misses the nutrition dimension (owner advanced).
**Mains hook:** Structure any poverty–nutrition answer with the cycle, then the convergence levers.
**Back:** *"Poor parents, stunted child, poorer adult — break it early."*

### Flashcard SJ-1042 — 2024 GS-II Q7: breaking the vicious cycle (10 marks)
**Front:** What does the 2024 GS-II Q7 ask, and what answer does the owner prescribe?
**Definition (learn):** "Poverty and malnutrition create a vicious cycle, adversely affecting human capital formation. What steps can be taken to break the cycle?" (150 words; 10 marks). (✅ owner 02)
**Breakdown:** Define cycle; trace intergenerational trap; list levers (Poshan 2.0 first-1000-days, PM POSHAN, ONORC, women's nutrition and anaemia, WASH); conclude with convergence.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024, GS-II, Q7, 10 marks, 150 words. (✅ owner 02)
**Links:** SJ-1041, SJ-1045, SJ-1046.
**Trap:** A single scheme cannot break the cycle; the owner calls the single-scheme approach structurally insufficient.
**Mains hook:** End with a convergence imperative and a measurable indicator (stunting round and year).
**Back:** *"Map the cycle, pull the levers, converge."*

### Flashcard SJ-1043 — 2018 GS-II Q17 and 2019 GS-II Q7: hunger and poverty routes
**Front:** Which earlier Mains PYQs route to food and hunger, and what do they ask?
**Definition (learn):** 2018 GS-II Q17 (15 marks, 250 words): food availability focus against human-development policies as a cause of hunger. 2019 GS-II Q7 (10 marks, 150 words): divergence between poverty reduction and hunger, with shrinking social expenditure. (✅ owner 02 PYQ block)
**Breakdown:**
- Core owns both routes (owner 02 Section 11A).
- Use availability, access, absorption and utilisation as the four-part frame.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Years and marks per owner ledger. (✅ owner 02)
**Links:** SJ-1026, SJ-1041, SJ-1020 (growth vs human development).
**Trap:** Food availability is not household access; calorie adequacy is not diet quality.
**Mains hook:** Separate availability, access, absorption and utilisation (owner 02 Core 15-mark frame).
**Back:** *"Availability, access, absorption, utilisation — the four gates of food."*

### Flashcard SJ-1044 — 2018 Prelims Q25: NFSA beneficiary categories (routed, key not held)
**Front:** What is the statement-level content of the 2018 Prelims Q25 route on NFSA?
**Definition (learn):** The routed demand is the NFSA 2013 beneficiary categories and provisions. The owner records the official key as unavailable locally, so no option is recorded. (✅ owner 02 PYQ block)
**Breakdown:** Cover priority households, AAY, entitlement per person or household, and the statutory CIP ceiling (SJ-1027, SJ-1028).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2018, Prelims GS-I, Q25 (owner). (✅ owner 02)
**Links:** SJ-1027, SJ-1028, SJ-1030.
**Trap:** Do not confuse the statutory price ceiling with current free distribution (SJ-1028).
**Mains hook:** Objective route; the Mains bridge is the NFSA eligibility architecture. Argue that statutory categories still need identification and documentation before the intended household is served.
**Back:** *"Priority, AAY, five and thirty-five."*

### Flashcard SJ-1045 — First 1000 days (conception to age two)
**Front:** What is the first-1000-days window and why does it matter?
**Definition (learn):** The period from conception to age two, when nutritional deprivation causes irreversible stunting and cognitive impairment. (✅ owner 02)
**Breakdown:**
- Interventions here yield the highest return (owner).
- Poshan 2.0 is built around this window (SJ-1032).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Conception to age 2 (owner). (✅ owner 02)
**Links:** SJ-1032, SJ-1034, SJ-1041.
**Trap:** The window is not only infancy; pregnancy is part of it.
**Mains hook:** Argue for early intervention as the highest-return step in any 10-mark answer.
**Back:** *"Conception to two: the window that cannot be reopened."*

### Flashcard SJ-1046 — Nutrition-specific vs nutrition-sensitive interventions
**Front:** Distinguish nutrition-specific from nutrition-sensitive interventions.
**Definition (learn):** Nutrition-specific interventions directly target nutritional outcomes (supplementary feeding, micronutrients, growth monitoring, breastfeeding). Nutrition-sensitive interventions act through other sectors (WASH, agriculture diversification, social protection, women's education). (✅ owner 02 advanced section 2)
**Breakdown:**
- Specific: Poshan 2.0, PM POSHAN, IFA supplementation.
- Sensitive: SBM and JJM (WASH), income support, agricultural diversification.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. (✅ owner 02)
**Links:** SJ-1026, SJ-1032, SJ-1033, SJ-1041.
**Trap:** WASH and income support are not nutrition-specific; they act indirectly.
**Mains hook:** Argue that the 2024 Q7 answer needs both kinds of intervention.
**Back:** *"Direct feeding versus the other sectors that feed the child."*

### Flashcard SJ-1047 — Poshan Tracker (app)
**Front:** What is the Poshan Tracker and what limits its data quality?
**Definition (learn):** A real-time data-entry app used by anganwadi workers for growth monitoring, supplementary nutrition and service coverage. (⚠️ owner 02 advanced section 4)
**Breakdown:**
- Data quality depends on worker capacity and device availability (owner).
- Dashboard data is high-frequency but may suffer reporting bias (owner 02 advanced section 6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None asserted. ⚠️
**Links:** SJ-1032, SJ-1036.
**Trap:** An administrative dashboard figure is not an NFHS outcome.
**Mains hook:** Use Poshan Tracker data as monitoring input, and NFHS as outcome evidence.
**Back:** *"Dashboard counts, survey outcomes — keep them apart."*

### Flashcard SJ-1048 — Trap: "NFSA addresses nutrition security"
**Front:** Why is "NFSA addresses nutrition security comprehensively" a trap?
**Definition (learn):** NFSA primarily addresses calorie and grain access; nutrition security needs dietary diversity, micronutrients, care practices and WASH. (✅ owner 02 basic traps)
**Breakdown:** Correct framing: NFSA is food security; the remainder is multi-sectoral.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. (✅ owner 02)
**Links:** SJ-1026, SJ-1027, SJ-1046.
**Trap:** The answer-option wording "NFSA ensures nutrition" is the examiner trap.
**Mains hook:** Use the same distinction to explain "food security is necessary but not sufficient."
**Back:** *"Grain is food security; diet is nutrition security."*

### Flashcard SJ-1049 — Trap: PM POSHAN vs Poshan 2.0 ministry pair
**Front:** Which ministry administers PM POSHAN and which administers Poshan 2.0?
**Definition (learn):** PM POSHAN is administered by the Ministry of Education; Poshan 2.0 (ICDS) by MWCD. (✅ owner 02)
**Breakdown:**
- Two schemes, two ministries, two age groups (school-age vs under-6 and pregnant women).
- Both sit in the nutrition architecture; neither is an NFSA scheme.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Ministries per owner. (✅ owner 02)
**Links:** SJ-1032, SJ-1033.
**Trap:** Owner trap lists both directions; the pairing is the test.
**Mains hook:** Use the two-ministry split to show convergence needs coordination.
**Back:** *"Education feeds schoolchildren; women and child feeds the under-six."*

### GROUP — Health Systems, Public Health and Universal Health Coverage

### Flashcard SJ-1050 — Universal Health Coverage (WHO three-dimension cube)
**Front:** What are the three dimensions of Universal Health Coverage?
**Definition (learn):** UHC means all people have access to needed promotive, preventive, curative and rehabilitative services of sufficient quality without financial hardship. (✅ owner 03)
**Breakdown:**
- Population: who is covered (breadth).
- Services: what is covered (depth; promotive, preventive, curative, rehabilitative).
- Financial protection: out-of-pocket cost and catastrophic spending (height).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** WHO framework (owner-cited). (✅ owner 03)
**Links:** SJ-1051 (NHP 2017), SJ-1054 (PM-JAY), SJ-1053 (HWC/AAM), SJ-1056 (OOPE).
**Trap:** UHC is not free hospitalisation alone; insurance cover is not service availability (owner 03 Semantic ownership).
**Mains hook:** Structure any health answer on the cube and show which dimension a scheme leaves weak.
**Back:** *"Who, what, what it costs — the UHC cube."*

### Flashcard SJ-1051 — National Health Policy 2017
**Front:** What are the core targets of the National Health Policy 2017?
**Definition (learn):** India's guiding health policy document, with goals including raising public health expenditure to 2.5% of GDP, strengthening primary care, achieving UHC and reducing OOPE. (✅ owner 03)
**Breakdown:**
- A policy, not a statute (owner 03 advanced; trap SJ-1067).
- Vision for a primary-care-led model.
- Implementation depends on state action and budget (owner 03 advanced section 4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2017; 2.5% of GDP public health expenditure target (owner). (✅ owner 03)
**Links:** SJ-1050, SJ-1052, SJ-1067.
**Trap:** NHP 2017 is a policy; an aspiration is not proof the target has been reached (owner advanced section 3).
**Mains hook:** Use the 2.5% target to test whether public spending is rising toward the stated aim.
**Back:** *"Two and a half of GDP, primary care first."*

### Flashcard SJ-1052 — National Health Mission (NRHM and NUHM)
**Front:** What is the National Health Mission and what does it cover?
**Definition (learn):** The umbrella programme covering NRHM (rural) and NUHM (urban); supports public-health infrastructure, human resources, free drugs and diagnostics and flexible financing for states. (✅ owner 03)
**Breakdown:**
- Centrally sponsored with Centre-state cost sharing.
- Supports ASHA and ANM, Janani Suraksha Yojana, RBSK, mobile medical units (owner 03 advanced section 4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner, no launch year given in owner. ⚠️ (launch year not re-verified).
**Links:** SJ-1051, SJ-1053, SJ-1063 (JSY), SJ-1065 (trap).
**Trap:** NHM is not rural-only; NUHM covers urban (owner 03 basic traps).
**Mains hook:** NHM is the delivery spine under Ayushman Bharat's primary tier.
**Back:** *"Rural NRHM plus urban NUHM — one mission."*

### Flashcard SJ-1053 — Health and Wellness Centres / Ayushman Arogya Mandirs (HWC/AAM)
**Front:** What do Health and Wellness Centres deliver, and what is the target?
**Definition (learn):** Upgraded sub-centres and PHCs delivering Comprehensive Primary Health Care (CPHC) through 12 service packages beyond reproductive and child health. (✅ owner 03)
**Breakdown:**
- Packages include NCDs, mental health, ENT, ophthalmology, oral health, geriatric and palliative care, emergency care (owner 03 advanced section 2).
- Target: 1.5 lakh HWCs (owner 03 advanced section 4).
- Staffed by Mid-Level Health Provider (MLHP) or Community Health Officer (CHO) (owner 03 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1.5 lakh target (owner); operational counts must come from the dashboard with access date (owner 03 advanced section 9). ⚠️ count not asserted.
**Links:** SJ-1050, SJ-1064 (CHO/MLHP), SJ-1066 (trap vs PM-JAY).
**Trap:** HWC/AAM is not reproductive-child health only (owner advanced trap).
**Mains hook:** HWC/AAM is the grassroots answer to marketisation in the 2024 Q17 frame.
**Back:** *"Twelve packages, one and a half lakh centres, first contact."*

### Flashcard SJ-1054 — PM-JAY (Pradhan Mantri Jan Arogya Yojana)
**Front:** What does PM-JAY provide and who implements it?
**Definition (learn):** Cashless secondary and tertiary hospitalisation cover up to Rs 5 lakh per family per year, for core eligible households identified by SECC deprivation criteria. Implemented by the National Health Authority. (✅ owner 03)
**Breakdown:**
- Empanels public and private hospitals; NHA maintains beneficiary database and processes claims.
- State Health Agencies implement at state level and may add top-ups (owner 03 basic section 4).
- Tax-funded, not contribution-funded (owner 03 advanced trap; see SJ-1065).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Rs 5 lakh per family per year (owner). (✅ owner 03)
**Links:** SJ-1050, SJ-1055 (70+), SJ-1058, SJ-1065.
**Trap:** PM-JAY is not universal across all citizens by default (owner trap).
**Mains hook:** PM-JAY is demand-side financial protection, not provider supply; pair it with HWC/AAM.
**Back:** *"Five lakh cashless, SECC-identified, NHA runs it."*

### Flashcard SJ-1055 — PM-JAY 70+ enrolment route
**Front:** Who can enrol under the PM-JAY 70+ route?
**Definition (learn):** All citizens aged 70 years and above can enrol regardless of income or SECC status; a separate income-independent route distinct from core eligibility. (✅ owner 03; ⚠️ the 2024 expansion date is owner-only)
**Breakdown:**
- Age-specific, not universal coverage for all ages (owner trap).
- Check current NHA guidance for separate cards and interaction with other cover (owner 03 basic section 5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Age 70 and above (owner). ⚠️ 2024 year is owner-stated.
**Links:** SJ-1054, SJ-1063.
**Trap:** Do not describe the 70+ expansion as universal for all ages.
**Mains hook:** Separate the core route from the age-based route when counting beneficiaries.
**Back:** *"Seventy and above, no income test."*

### Flashcard SJ-1056 — Out-of-Pocket Expenditure and NHA series (dated)
**Front:** What OOPE shares does the owner cite from National Health Accounts, and for which years?
**Definition (learn):** OOPE is household health spending paid at point of service; high OOPE causes catastrophic expenditure. Owner cites 47.1% (2019-20), 44.4% (2020-21) and 39.4% (2021-22). (⚠️ owner 03; figures not verified: a PIB source fetch returned 403; memory suggests a slightly different 2020-21 value, which should be re-checked)
**Breakdown:**
- Each figure is an accounting year, not the publication year (owner 03 current anchor).
- Declining share is progress, but the remaining share still signals incomplete protection.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 47.1 (2019-20); 44.4 (2020-21); 39.4 (2021-22). ⚠️
**Links:** SJ-1050, SJ-1073 (NHA vs National Health Accounts trap), SJ-1058.
**Trap:** Do not use publication year as accounting year.
**Mains hook:** Cite the accounting year with each OOPE share and note that aggregates hide household variation.
**Back:** *"Accounting year, not release year."*

### Flashcard SJ-1057 — Public health system tiering
**Front:** Order the tiers of India's public health delivery system.
**Definition (learn):** Sub-centre -> Primary Health Centre (PHC) -> Community Health Centre (CHC) -> Sub-District or District Hospital. (✅ owner 03)
**Breakdown:**
- Primary care at SC and PHC, secondary at CHC and district hospital, tertiary at medical colleges (owner 03 advanced concept table).
- HWC/AAM upgrades SC and PHC (SJ-1053).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. (✅ owner 03)
**Links:** SJ-1053, SJ-1052.
**Trap:** The tiering order is the examiner test; do not invert CHC and PHC.
**Mains hook:** The first contact sits at SC/PHC; referral sits above.
**Back:** *"Sub-centre, PHC, CHC, district."*

### Flashcard SJ-1058 — Clinical Establishments Act, 2010
**Front:** What does the Clinical Establishments Act, 2010 do and what is its status?
**Definition (learn):** A central Act providing for registration and regulation of clinical establishments (hospitals, clinics), with price and quality transparency. States must adopt or enact equivalent legislation for enforcement. (⚠️ owner 03 advanced, concept table)
**Breakdown:**
- State adoption is uneven; a federal regulatory gap (owner 03 advanced section 4).
- Regulatory tool against marketisation (2024 Q17).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2010 (owner). ⚠️ Adoption status by state not asserted.
**Links:** SJ-1059, SJ-1060, SJ-1072.
**Trap:** The Act is not uniformly enforced across India (owner advanced trap).
**Mains hook:** Argue that regulation without state adoption leaves private markets unchecked.
**Back:** *"Central law, state adoption decides enforcement."*

### Flashcard SJ-1059 — Standard Treatment Guidelines and price capping
**Front:** What are Standard Treatment Guidelines (STGs) and what price-control tool does the owner name?
**Definition (learn):** STGs are evidence-based diagnosis and treatment protocols that promote rational care and cost control. Owner names NPPA price capping for devices, as an example. (⚠️ owner 03 advanced concept table; NPPA example is owner-stated)
**Breakdown:**
- Adoption varies across public and empanelled private providers.
- Pair with clinical establishment rules (SJ-1058).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None asserted. ⚠️
**Links:** SJ-1058, SJ-1060.
**Trap:** An STG is guidance; it does not replace enforcement.
**Mains hook:** Price caps plus STGs reduce unnecessary procedures under marketisation.
**Back:** *"Protocols for care, caps for price."*

### Flashcard SJ-1060 — Marketisation of healthcare and the 2024 GS-II Q17
**Front:** What does the 2024 GS-II Q17 ask and what route does the owner give?
**Definition (learn):** "In a crucial domain like the public healthcare system the Indian State should play a vital role to contain the adverse impact of marketisation of the system. Suggest some measures through which the State can enhance the reach of public healthcare at the grassroots level." (250 words; 15 marks). (✅ owner 03)
**Breakdown:**
- Define marketisation and its harm: high cost, unnecessary procedures, catastrophic OOPE, unequal access.
- Constitutional and policy basis: DPSP Article 47, NHP 2017.
- Measures: HWC/AAM expansion, NHM support, PM-JAY, regulation, PPP with accountability.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024, GS-II, Q17, 15 marks. (✅ owner 03)
**Links:** SJ-1053, SJ-1054, SJ-1058, SJ-1059, SJ-1019 (Art 47), SJ-1071.
**Trap:** A purely market-driven answer misses the state's role as guarantor.
**Mains hook:** End with the state as primary guarantor, not residual provider.
**Back:** *"Contain harm, expand public, insure, regulate."*

### Flashcard SJ-1061 — Health PYQ routes 2018 GS-II Q7, 2020 GS-II Q6, 2021 GS-II Q6
**Front:** Which earlier Mains health PYQs route to this owner, and what do they ask?
**Definition (learn):** 2018 GS-II Q7 (10 marks): community-level healthcare intervention and Health for All. 2020 GS-II Q6 (10 marks): health policies for geriatric and maternal care (cross-cutting with senior-citizens topic). 2021 GS-II Q6 (10 marks): primary health structure as a precondition for sustainable development. (✅ owner 03 PYQ block)
**Breakdown:**
- Community health maps to ASHAs, ANMs, VHSNCs, Ayushman Arogya Mandirs.
- Financing chain: revenue -> risk pooling -> strategic purchasing -> provision.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Years and marks per owner. (✅ owner 03)
**Links:** SJ-1052, SJ-1053, SJ-1050.
**Trap:** Insurance without public primary care leaves prevention and outpatient OOPE unresolved.
**Mains hook:** Make primary care the base of the UHC pyramid in any 10-mark answer.
**Back:** *"Community first, insurance second."*

### Flashcard SJ-1062 — Pradhan Mantri Surakshit Matritva Abhiyan (routed, key present)
**Front:** What is the routed 2024 Prelims Q99 demand on maternal health?
**Definition (learn):** The routed demand is the Pradhan Mantri Surakshit Matritva Abhiyan (maternal health). The owner records the official Set-A key is available locally but does not record the answer. (⚠️ owner 03 PYQ block)
**Breakdown:** Scheme name and the maternal-health objective; check its year and ministry against an official source before use (⚠️ not verified here).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024, Prelims GS-I, Q99 (owner). ⚠️ launch date not re-checked.
**Links:** SJ-1063, SJ-1052.
**Trap:** Do not state the answer; the owner records none.
**Mains hook:** Read the maternal-health abhiyan as an NHM-linked delivery route; judge it by maternal and neonatal outcomes, not by facility-delivery counts alone.
**Back:** *"Safe motherhood abhiyan — check the official key."*

### Flashcard SJ-1063 — Janani Suraksha Yojana (routed to 2023 Prelims Q51)
**Front:** What is the routed 2023 Prelims Q51 demand on maternal and neonatal mortality?
**Definition (learn):** The route covers the Janani Suraksha Yojana and institutional delivery in maternal and neonatal mortality reduction. Owner records the key as unavailable locally. (✅ owner 03 PYQ block; JSY sits under NHM per owner 03 advanced section 4)
**Breakdown:**
- JSY is a cash incentive for institutional delivery under NHM (owner).
- Institutional delivery is a means, not an outcome by itself.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2023, Prelims GS-I, Q51. (✅ owner 03)
**Links:** SJ-1052, SJ-1062.
**Trap:** Institutional delivery rate is not the maternal-mortality outcome.
**Mains hook:** Institutional delivery is an input; argue for tracking maternal and neonatal mortality alongside facility-delivery rates.
**Back:** *"Institutional birth is input, mortality is outcome."*

### Flashcard SJ-1064 — MLHP and Community Health Officer staffing
**Front:** Who staffs the HWCs under the owner's design?
**Definition (learn):** Health and Wellness Centres are staffed by a Mid-Level Health Provider (MLHP) or a Community Health Officer (CHO). (✅ owner 03 advanced section 4)
**Breakdown:**
- HR bottleneck limits service at HWCs and CHCs (owner 03 advanced section 6).
- ASHA and ANM are the community workforce under NHM (SJ-1052).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. (✅ owner 03)
**Links:** SJ-1053, SJ-1052.
**Trap:** Do not conflate ASHA (community link worker) with the HWC clinical staff.
**Mains hook:** Workforce vacancies are the binding constraint on HWC delivery.
**Back:** *"MLHP or CHO staffs the centre; ASHA links the village."*

### Flashcard SJ-1065 — Trap: PM-JAY (tax-funded) vs ESIS (contribution-based)
**Front:** Why is "PM-JAY is a social-health-insurance scheme with employee contributions" wrong?
**Definition (learn):** PM-JAY is tax-funded. ESIS is the contribution-based social health insurance scheme (owner 03 advanced trap). (✅ owner 03)
**Breakdown:**
- Tax-funded: NHM and PM-JAY.
- Contribution-based SHI: ESIS (owner-cited example).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. (✅ owner 03)
**Links:** SJ-1054, SJ-1052.
**Trap:** Contribution-linked cover is SHI; tax funding is not.
**Mains hook:** India's architecture is mixed and fragmented; no single comprehensive national pool (owner 03 advanced).
**Back:** *"Tax funds PM-JAY; contributions fund ESIS."*

### Flashcard SJ-1066 — Trap: HWC/AAM vs PM-JAY (two Ayushman pillars)
**Front:** Why are HWC/AAM and PM-JAY not the same programme?
**Definition (learn):** HWC/AAM is comprehensive primary care (preventive, promotive, basic curative). PM-JAY is hospitalisation insurance for secondary and tertiary care. (✅ owner 03)
**Breakdown:**
- Two pillars of Ayushman Bharat, two funding and delivery routes.
- Primary care sits at HWC; hospital cover at empanelled hospitals.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. (✅ owner 03)
**Links:** SJ-1053, SJ-1054.
**Trap:** The owner's Prelims pairing trap confuses the two.
**Mains hook:** Pair both pillars to explain UHC, with the cube (SJ-1050).
**Back:** *"Primary care at the centre, hospital cover at the empanelled door."*

### Flashcard SJ-1067 — Trap: National Health Policy is a statute
**Front:** Why is NHP 2017 not an enacted statute?
**Definition (learn):** NHP 2017 is a policy document; statutory entitlements come from separate Acts such as the Clinical Establishments Act, 2010 (owner 03 advanced trap). (✅ owner 03)
**Breakdown:** Policy sets vision; statute creates enforceable duty; implementation depends on state budget.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. (✅ owner 03)
**Links:** SJ-1051, SJ-1058.
**Trap:** A policy target is not a legal right.
**Mains hook:** Distinguish policy aspiration from enforceable entitlement.
**Back:** *"Policy aims, statute binds."*

### Flashcard SJ-1068 — Mental Healthcare Act 2017 and Tele-MANAS (owner-cited)
**Front:** What do the Mental Healthcare Act and Tele-MANAS add to the health architecture, per the owner?
**Definition (learn):** The owner cites the Mental Healthcare Act, 2017 as a rights framework and Tele-MANAS as an access expansion. Neither replaces district professionals and continuity of care. (⚠️ owner 03 Core 11A; Act year is owner-stated and matches general knowledge but was not fetched)
**Breakdown:** Rights framework plus remote access; workforce still needed.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Act 2017 (owner). ⚠️
**Links:** SJ-1053 (mental health package), SJ-1064.
**Trap:** A helpline is not a psychiatric service network.
**Mains hook:** Rights statute plus tele-access still needs district capacity.
**Back:** *"Rights on paper, professionals on the ground."*

### Flashcard SJ-1069 — PM-ABHIM (owner-cited, unverified)
**Front:** What does the owner say about PM-ABHIM?
**Definition (learn):** The owner cites PM-ABHIM as strengthening health systems alongside NHM free drugs and diagnostics. (⚠️ owner 03 Core 11A; no launch date or scale given in owner)
**Breakdown:** System-strengthening mission complementing NHM; check the official scheme page before citing a year.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None asserted. ⚠️
**Links:** SJ-1052, SJ-1053.
**Trap:** Do not attach a date or outlay from memory.
**Mains hook:** Use PM-ABHIM only as a system-capacity example.
**Back:** *"Health mission, check the year."*

### Flashcard SJ-1070 — Kerala model (illustrative boundary case)
**Front:** What does the Kerala model illustrate in the owner's health analysis?
**Definition (learn):** Strong public-health infrastructure, high literacy and decentralised governance yield low OOPE and good outcomes; the owner uses it as a supply-side case. (⚠️ owner 03 advanced section 5)
**Breakdown:** Illustrative, not a measured causal claim; state the data year if used.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None asserted. ⚠️
**Links:** SJ-1057, SJ-1056.
**Trap:** A state example does not prove causation; cite the indicator and year.
**Mains hook:** Use Kerala to argue supply-side strengthening, qualified by literacy and governance factors.
**Back:** *"Public supply, literacy and local governance together."*

### Flashcard SJ-1071 — Insurance-led vs provision-led UHC (advanced trade-off)
**Front:** What trade-off does the owner set between insurance-led and provision-led UHC?
**Definition (learn):** PM-JAY relies on empanelled private providers, which may not expand supply in under-served areas; provision (HWC, NHM) is needed to reach remote and marginalised populations. (⚠️ owner 03 advanced section 6)
**Breakdown:**
- Moral hazard and supplier-induced demand if regulation and STGs are weak.
- Cream-skimming and claim inflation by empanelled hospitals are the boundary risks (owner 03 advanced section 5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. ⚠️
**Links:** SJ-1054, SJ-1053, SJ-1059.
**Trap:** Insurance does not create service supply.
**Mains hook:** Argue that UHC needs both: provision where it is absent and insurance where it is missing.
**Back:** *"Insurance pays; provision must exist to be paid for."*

### Flashcard SJ-1072 — Trap: Clinical Establishments Act uniformly enforced
**Front:** Why is "the Clinical Establishments Act is uniformly enforced" a trap?
**Definition (learn):** State adoption varies; some states have not adopted or notified equivalent rules (owner 03 advanced trap). (⚠️ owner-only)
**Breakdown:** Federal regulatory gap.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None asserted. ⚠️
**Links:** SJ-1058.
**Trap:** The trap tests state adoption, not the central Act's existence.
**Mains hook:** Use federal adoption gaps to show why regulation is patchy.
**Back:** *"Enacted centrally, enforced by states."*

### Flashcard SJ-1073 — Trap: National Health Accounts vs National Health Authority
**Front:** What is the difference between the National Health Accounts and the National Health Authority?
**Definition (learn):** National Health Accounts produces official estimates of health expenditure (public, private, OOPE). The National Health Authority implements PM-JAY. (✅ owner 03)
**Breakdown:** Accounts measure spend; the Authority runs insurance.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. (✅ owner 03)
**Links:** SJ-1056, SJ-1054.
**Trap:** Do not confuse the estimates body with the scheme implementer.
**Mains hook:** Accounts show where money goes; claims data show what protection reaches a household. Argue that both must be read together.
**Back:** *"Accounts count money; the Authority pays claims."*

### GROUP — Education and Human Resource Development

### Flashcard SJ-1074 — Right to Education Act, 2009
**Front:** What does the Right of Children to Free and Compulsory Education Act, 2009 guarantee?
**Definition (learn):** A statutory framework for free and compulsory elementary education for children aged 6–14. (✅ owner 04)
**Breakdown:**
- Free schooling in the public system; entry-level obligation for weaker and disadvantaged children in private unaided non-minority schools (SJ-1076).
- Ministry of Education is the nodal ministry (owner 04).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Ages 6–14; 2009 (owner). (✅ owner 04)
**Links:** SJ-1075 (Art 21A), SJ-1076 (25%), SJ-1077 (2019 amendment), SJ-1078 (NEP 2020).
**Trap:** RTE 2009 covers 6–14, not 3–18 (owner trap); NEP's ECCE and secondary are policy, not statutory right.
**Mains hook:** Access is guaranteed by RTE; learning needs NIPUN and quality inputs.
**Back:** *"Six to fourteen, free and compulsory."*

### Flashcard SJ-1075 — Article 21A (86th Amendment, 2002)
**Front:** Which amendment inserted Article 21A, and what does it make a fundamental right?
**Definition (learn):** The 86th Amendment, 2002, inserted Article 21A, making free and compulsory education a fundamental right for children aged 6–14. (✅ owner 04)
**Breakdown:**
- The constitutional basis for RTE 2009.
- Polity owns doctrinal detail; Social Justice applies the entitlement lens (owner 04).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 86th Amendment, 2002 (owner). (✅ owner 04)
**Links:** SJ-1074, SJ-1019 (DPSP contrast).
**Trap:** Art 21A is a Fundamental Right; the DPSP education directive (Art 41) is not enforceable like it.
**Mains hook:** A constitutional right to education converts access into an enforceable claim, but not into learning.
**Back:** *"Eighty-sixth Amendment, six to fourteen, a right."*

### Flashcard SJ-1076 — RTE 25% entry-level reservation in private unaided schools
**Front:** What is the RTE 25% obligation for private unaided schools?
**Definition (learn):** Private unaided non-minority schools must admit children from weaker and disadvantaged groups up to 25% of entry-level seats, subject to the Act and judicial interpretation. (✅ owner 04)
**Breakdown:**
- Owner notes implementation challenges: delayed reimbursements, documentation barriers, uneven enforcement (⚠️ owner 04).
- Access versus inclusion test (owner 04 advanced section 5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 25% (owner). (✅ owner 04)
**Links:** SJ-1074, SJ-1011.
**Trap:** The 25% applies at entry level in private unaided non-minority schools, not in government schools.
**Mains hook:** Argue that a seat quota without reimbursement and documentation fails at the access point.
**Back:** *"Quarter of entry seats, private unaided, weaker groups."*

### Flashcard SJ-1077 — RTE Amendment 2019: detention at Grades 5 and 8
**Front:** What did the 2019 amendment to RTE change on detention?
**Definition (learn):** The no-detention policy till Grade 8 was relaxed; states may reintroduce detention at Grades 5 and 8. (✅ owner 04 advanced section 2)
**Breakdown:**
- Owner records a motivation-versus-exclusion dilemma.
- Owner's Core note: state discretion restored for regular examinations in Classes V and VIII, with possible detention after instruction and re-examination (owner 04 Core 11A).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2019 amendment; Grades 5 and 8 (owner). (✅ owner 04)
**Links:** SJ-1074, SJ-1097.
**Trap:** The amendment did not abolish the no-detention policy; it gave state discretion.
**Mains hook:** Discuss retention as motivation and risk of dropout for disadvantaged children.
**Back:** *"Five and eight: states may detain again."*

### Flashcard SJ-1078 — NEP 2020: 5+3+3+4 structure
**Front:** What are the stages and ages of the NEP 2020 structure?
**Definition (learn):** Foundational (ages 3–8; ECCE plus Grades 1–2), Preparatory (ages 8–11; Grades 3–5), Middle (ages 11–14; Grades 6–8), Secondary (ages 14–18; Grades 9–12). (✅ owner 04 advanced)
**Breakdown:**
- Replaces the earlier 10+2 structure conceptually.
- Mother-tongue instruction till at least Grade 5 (owner 04 advanced section 4).
- Multidisciplinary higher education, vocational integration, Academic Bank of Credits.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 5+3+3+4 (owner). (✅ owner 04)
**Links:** SJ-1079 (trap), SJ-1081 (NIPUN), SJ-1085 (ABC), SJ-1087 (vocational).
**Trap:** The 5+3+3+4 counts stages, not statutory ages.
**Mains hook:** The structure is a policy frame; its quality depends on FLN (SJ-1081).
**Back:** *"Five, three, three, four — three to eighteen."*

### Flashcard SJ-1079 — Trap: NEP 2020 makes ECCE a statutory right
**Front:** Why is "NEP 2020 makes ECCE a statutory right" wrong?
**Definition (learn):** NEP 2020 extends focus to ECCE as policy; RTE 2009 is the statutory basis and covers ages 6–14 (owner 04 advanced traps). (✅ owner 04)
**Breakdown:** Policy versus statute distinction; ECCE is policy.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. (✅ owner 04)
**Links:** SJ-1074, SJ-1078.
**Trap:** NEP 2020 is a policy document, not an Act.
**Mains hook:** Use the policy-statute split when a question asks what is enforceable.
**Back:** *"NEP policy, RTE statute."*

### Flashcard SJ-1080 — Samagra Shiksha
**Front:** What is Samagra Shiksha, and what does it merge?
**Definition (learn):** An integrated centrally sponsored scheme of the Ministry of Education merging SSA (elementary), RMSA (secondary) and teacher education. (✅ owner 04)
**Breakdown:**
- Covers infrastructure, teacher training, ICT, girls' hostels, inclusive education.
- Centre-state cost sharing (owner 04 basic section 4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner, no launch year. ⚠️
**Links:** SJ-1074, SJ-1081, SJ-1078.
**Trap:** Samagra Shiksha does not replace RTE; it is the scheme implementing RTE and NEP (owner 04 advanced trap).
**Mains hook:** Scheme capacity and statutory duty must be distinguished.
**Back:** *"SSA, RMSA, teacher training — one umbrella."*

### Flashcard SJ-1081 — NIPUN Bharat (foundational literacy and numeracy)
**Front:** What is NIPUN Bharat and what target does it set?
**Definition (learn):** National Initiative for Proficiency in Reading with Understanding and Numeracy; a mission under NEP 2020 targeting foundational literacy and numeracy by the end of Grade 3, with national target year 2026-27. (✅ owner 04)
**Breakdown:**
- Launched 2021 (owner 04 advanced section 4).
- States submit implementation plans with grade-wise learning outcomes (Vidya Pravesh).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Target 2026-27; launched 2021 (owner). (✅ owner 04)
**Links:** SJ-1082 (FLN), SJ-1083 (ASER), SJ-1078.
**Trap:** NIPUN Bharat is a learning mission, not a scholarship (owner trap).
**Mains hook:** Argue that FLN by Grade 3 is the policy response to near-universal enrolment with weak learning.
**Back:** *"Read and count by Grade three."*

### Flashcard SJ-1082 — Foundational literacy and numeracy (FLN) and learning poverty
**Front:** Define FLN and the World Bank's learning-poverty concept.
**Definition (learn):** FLN is basic reading, writing and arithmetic expected by Grade 3. Learning poverty (World Bank) is the share of 10-year-olds unable to read and understand a simple text. (⚠️ owner 04 concept tables; learning-poverty definition owner-stated)
**Breakdown:**
- FLN is the NEP and NIPUN priority.
- Learning poverty adds a global benchmark.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None asserted. ⚠️
**Links:** SJ-1081, SJ-1083.
**Trap:** Enrolment is not learning; literacy is not education quality (owner Core).
**Mains hook:** "High enrolment does not guarantee foundational literacy and numeracy" — cite FLN data with the round.
**Back:** *"Grade three reading, ten-year-old text: the learning gap."*

### Flashcard SJ-1083 — ASER 2024: 23.4% of Standard III children read a Standard II text (owner-cited)
**Front:** What does ASER 2024 report on Standard III reading in government schools?
**Definition (learn):** ASER 2024, a non-government rural household survey, found recovery from the 2022 learning shock but continuing FLN deficit: 23.4% of government-school Standard III children could read a Standard II text. (⚠️ figure owner-stated; not fetched, the ASER page returned 404)
**Breakdown:**
- Rural household survey, not school administrative data.
- Do not generalise to urban India (owner 04 current anchor).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ASER 2024 (owner). ⚠️ 23.4% not verified.
**Links:** SJ-1082, SJ-1081, SJ-1090 (digital education), SJ-1084 (AISHE).
**Trap:** ASER is not UDISE+ or AISHE data.
**Mains hook:** Cite ASER with the round, school type, class and competency together.
**Back:** *"Twenty-three point four read a Standard II text."*

### Flashcard SJ-1084 — AISHE 2021-22: GER 28.4 (owner-cited)
**Front:** What does the owner cite for higher-education GER from AISHE?
**Definition (learn):** AISHE 2021-22 reported an all-India GER of 28.4 for ages 18–23, up from 27.3 in 2020-21; female GER 28.5. (⚠️ owner-stated; not fetched)
**Breakdown:**
- GER measures access, not completion or quality (concept table).
- Regional, gender and social-group disparities persist (owner 04 advanced section 3).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** AISHE 2021-22: 28.4 (owner). ⚠️
**Links:** SJ-1082, SJ-1097.
**Trap:** GER includes students of any age; NER is the age-matched measure (⚠️ general knowledge, not owner-stated).
**Mains hook:** Use AISHE for access, with the year; add SC/ST disparities as an equity qualification.
**Back:** *"Access rising, equity lagging."*

### Flashcard SJ-1085 — Academic Bank of Credits (ABC)
