**Front:** What does the Academic Bank of Credits do under NEP 2020?
**Definition (learn):** A reform letting students accumulate credits from multiple institutions and redeem them for a degree. (✅ owner 04 advanced concept table)
**Breakdown:**
- Supports multiple entry-exit and lifelong learning.
- Contrast with a single-institution degree route.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** NEP 2020 (owner). (✅ owner 04)
**Links:** SJ-1078, SJ-1088.
**Trap:** ABC is a credit-accumulation reform; it is not a separate regulator.
**Mains hook:** Credit portability supports access flexibility, not by itself employability.
**Back:** *"Credits saved across institutions, redeemed as a degree."*

### Flashcard SJ-1086 — NCVET and NAPS (skills ecosystem)
**Front:** What do NCVET and NAPS do in the skills ecosystem?
**Definition (learn):** NCVET (National Council for Vocational Education and Training) regulates skill qualifications, awarding bodies and assessment agencies, replacing NCVT and NSDA (owner). NAPS (National Apprenticeship Promotion Scheme) gives stipend support to employers hiring apprentices. (✅ owner 04)
**Breakdown:**
- NCVET under the Ministry of Skill Development and Entrepreneurship.
- NAPS bridges classroom and workplace learning.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** NCVET replaces NCVT and NSDA (owner). (✅ owner 04)
**Links:** SJ-1087, SJ-1095.
**Trap:** NCVET is the regulator, not the training provider.
**Mains hook:** Qualification regulation and apprenticeship money together address employability.
**Back:** *"NCVET regulates, NAPS pays the apprentice's stipend."*

### Flashcard SJ-1087 — Skill India Mission, PM-KAUSHAL and the NSQF levels
**Front:** Which programmes make up the skill-training delivery side, and what does NSQF measure?
**Definition (learn):** Skill India Mission and PM-KAUSHAL centres deliver skill training; ITIs and polytechnics add institutional capacity. NSQF is a competency hierarchy of 10 levels (owner 04 advanced). (⚠️ NSQF level count is owner-stated; not fetched)
**Breakdown:**
- Recognition, certification and mobility across formal and non-formal training.
- Placement and wage outcomes are contested metrics (owner 04 advanced section 6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** NSQF levels 1–10 (owner). ⚠️
**Links:** SJ-1086, SJ-1095.
**Trap:** Training output is not placement; check the wage outcome.
**Mains hook:** Skill training must align with industry demand to convert into employment.
**Back:** *"Train, certify, match, then pay."*

### Flashcard SJ-1088 — HECI and the Viksit Bharat Shiksha Adhishthan Bill, 2025 (status dated)
**Front:** What is HECI and what is the owner's dated status of the enabling bill?
**Definition (learn):** HECI (Higher Education Commission of India) was proposed under NEP 2020 as a single regulator to replace UGC, AICTE and NCTE. The owner says the enabling bill (Viksit Bharat Shiksha Adhishthan Bill, 2025) was approved by Cabinet in December 2025 and introduced in Parliament, and as of mid-2026 has not been enacted. (⚠️ owner-only status)
**Breakdown:**
- UGC, AICTE and NCTE remain operative until enactment and phased implementation.
- Owner's dated status must be re-checked against Parliament records before use.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Cabinet approval December 2025 (owner). ⚠️ not re-verified.
**Links:** SJ-1085, SJ-1089.
**Trap:** HECI has not replaced UGC or AICTE (owner trap); say "proposed" unless the Act is enacted.
**Mains hook:** Single regulator reform: argue autonomy and accountability trade-offs, not a settled outcome.
**Back:** *"One regulator proposed, three still operate."*

### Flashcard SJ-1089 — Public Examinations (Prevention of Unfair Means) Act, 2024 (routing trap)
**Front:** What do the owner's Basic and Advanced files say about the 2024 GS-II Q11 route, and why is the routing contested?
**Definition (learn):** The 2024 GS-II Q11 (15 marks, 250 words) asks the aims of the Public Examinations (Prevention of Unfair Means) Act, 2024, and whether university and state-board examinations fall within it. The Act targets notified public examinations (owner 04 basic section 5). (✅ owner 04 basic; the owner's PYQ ledger routes this to Governance, not to this owner)
**Breakdown:**
- Do not assume all university or state-board exams are covered.
- The basic file treats it as an education-integrity link; the PYQ ledger routes it to Governance.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024, GS-II, Q11, 15 marks (owner). (✅ owner 04)
**Links:** SJ-1088, SJ-1074.
**Trap:** Do not claim universal coverage of all exams under the Act.
**Mains hook:** Link exam fairness to equal access and institutional trust; check the Act's notified authorities first.
**Back:** *"Notified exams only — check the list before the link."*

### Flashcard SJ-1090 — 2020 GS-I Q20: digital education (DIKSHA and SWAYAM)
**Front:** What does the 2020 GS-I Q20 route ask about digital education?
**Definition (learn):** "Digital initiatives and the functioning of the education system" (15 marks, 250 words). Core owns this route. (✅ owner 04 PYQ block)
**Breakdown:**
- DIKSHA supports school content and teachers; SWAYAM hosts online courses (owner 04 Core).
- Access qualifiers: devices, connectivity, language, disability, study space.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2020, GS-I, Q20, 15 marks. (✅ owner 04)
**Links:** SJ-1082, SJ-1091.
**Trap:** Digital provision is not digital access.
**Mains hook:** Digital initiatives must be judged by who can actually use them.
**Back:** *"Content on screens, access in homes."*

### Flashcard SJ-1091 — 2020 GS-II Q18: NEP 2020 and SDG 4
**Front:** What does the 2020 GS-II Q18 ask about NEP 2020 and SDG 4?
**Definition (learn):** "National Education Policy 2020 and Sustainable Development Goal 4" — critically examine (15 marks, 250 words). (✅ owner 04 PYQ block)
**Breakdown:**
- SDG 4 is inclusive quality education and lifelong learning.
- Test NEP reforms against finance, teachers and ECCE delivery (owner 04 Core).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2020, GS-II, Q18, 15 marks. (✅ owner 04)
**Links:** SJ-1078, SJ-1081, SJ-1088.
**Trap:** Policy alignment with SDG 4 is not delivery.
**Mains hook:** Qualify the NEP-SDG link with finance and teacher capacity.
**Back:** *"Policy aligns, finance and teachers decide."*

### Flashcard SJ-1092 — 2021 GS-II Q7: earn while you learn and vocational skilling
**Front:** What does the 2021 GS-II Q7 ask?
**Definition (learn):** "Earn while you learn and meaningful vocational education and skilling" (10 marks, 150 words). (✅ owner 04 PYQ block)
**Breakdown:**
- Link apprenticeship (NAPS) and vocational integration (NEP).
- Stigma and matching remain constraints (owner 04 Core 11A).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2021, GS-II, Q7, 10 marks. (✅ owner 04)
**Links:** SJ-1086, SJ-1087.
**Trap:** Earn-while-learn is not the same as employment guarantee.
**Mains hook:** Vocational stream stigma is the attitude barrier to qualify.
**Back:** *"Earn, learn, and shed the stigma."*

### Flashcard SJ-1093 — 2022 GS-II Q18: RTE 2009 and awareness about schooling
**Front:** What does the 2022 GS-II Q18 ask?
**Definition (learn):** "Right to Education Act 2009 and awareness about schooling" — analyse (15 marks, 250 words). (✅ owner 04 PYQ block)
**Breakdown:** Awareness of entitlement is the access precondition; use the 25% quota and enrolment chain (SJ-1076, SJ-1097).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2022, GS-II, Q18, 15 marks. (✅ owner 04)
**Links:** SJ-1074, SJ-1076, SJ-1097.
**Trap:** Knowledge of a right does not guarantee enforcement.
**Mains hook:** Awareness plus grievance route makes RTE claims usable.
**Back:** *"A right nobody knows is a right nobody claims."*

### Flashcard SJ-1094 — 2023 GS-II Q6: inadequate attention to HRD in India
**Front:** What does the 2023 GS-II Q6 ask?
**Definition (learn):** Inadequate attention to Human Resource Development in India — suggest measures (10 marks, 150 words). (✅ owner 04 PYQ block)
**Breakdown:** HRD equals schooling, skills and health converting into productive capability (owner 04 Core).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2023, GS-II, Q6, 10 marks. (✅ owner 04)
**Links:** SJ-1006, SJ-1097, SJ-1060.
**Trap:** HRD is not reducible to employability.
**Mains hook:** Three-pillar HRD: health, education, skills.
**Back:** *"Health, schooling, skills — HRD needs all three."*

### Flashcard SJ-1095 — 2023 GS-II Q18: skill development and the skill–employment link
**Front:** What does the 2023 GS-II Q18 ask?
**Definition (learn):** Skill development programmes and links between skill education and employment — analyse the linkages (15 marks, 250 words). Shared with Economy; both routes terminate in Core (owner 04 PYQ block). (✅ owner 04)
**Breakdown:** Map training, certification, apprenticeship and placement; test the placement gap.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2023, GS-II, Q18, 15 marks (owner). (✅ owner 04)
**Links:** SJ-1086, SJ-1087.
**Trap:** Training completion is not employment.
**Mains hook:** Qualify the linkage with placement and wage outcomes.
**Back:** *"Trained is not employed."*

### Flashcard SJ-1096 — 2018 Prelims Q21: RTE teacher qualification (routed, key not held)
**Front:** What is the routed 2018 Prelims Q21 demand?
**Definition (learn):** Teacher qualification eligibility provisions under the RTE Act. Official key unavailable locally; no option recorded. (✅ owner 04 PYQ block)
**Breakdown:** Cover eligibility statements for teachers under the entitlement statute.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2018, Prelims GS-I, Q21 (owner). (✅ owner 04)
**Links:** SJ-1074, SJ-1077.
**Trap:** Statement-level detail; do not infer the answer.
**Mains hook:** Teacher eligibility under the entitlement statute links to schooling quality; argue that qualified teachers, not enrolment, turn RTE access into learning.
**Back:** *"Teacher eligibility is a statutory detail."*

### Flashcard SJ-1097 — Education conversion chain: enrolment to employment
**Front:** Trace the education conversion chain and name the measure at each link.
**Definition (learn):** Enrolment -> attendance -> transition -> completion -> competency -> higher education or skill -> employment. UDISE+, ASER and AISHE measure different links. (✅ owner 04 Core 11A)
**Breakdown:**
- Enrolment: UDISE+ administrative data.
- Learning: ASER (rural household sample).
- Higher education access: AISHE GER.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. (✅ owner 04)
**Links:** SJ-1083, SJ-1084, SJ-1082, SJ-1095.
**Trap:** Enrolment is not attendance or learning; literacy is not quality.
**Mains hook:** Use the chain to show where the leak is: access, learning, or transition.
**Back:** *"Enrol, attend, learn, transition, work — measure each link."*

### GROUP — Women and Gender Justice

### Flashcard SJ-1098 — Gender equality, gender equity and women's empowerment
**Front:** Distinguish gender equality, gender equity and women's empowerment.
**Definition (learn):** Equality: same legal treatment and opportunities. Equity: differentiated treatment that offsets structural disadvantage. Empowerment: expansion of women's agency, capability and voice, the outcome when protective and affirmative instruments work. (✅ owner 05)
**Breakdown:**
- Equality is the constitutional baseline (Art 14–16 in owner 05 advanced).
- Equity is the instrument layer (protective law plus affirmative schemes).
- Empowerment is the measured outcome (NFHS indicators, workforce, representation).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024, GS-I PYQ (10 marks) owner route. (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** SJ-1009 (formal vs substantive), SJ-1099, SJ-1107, SJ-1113.
**Trap:** Treating equality, equity and empowerment as synonyms misses the causal chain UPSC expects.
**Mains hook:** Define the three terms, then argue that programme design must account for unequal starting conditions.
**Back:** *"Same rules, fair treatment, expanded agency."*

### Flashcard SJ-1099 — Protection of Women from Domestic Violence Act, 2005: civil reliefs
**Front:** What civil reliefs does the DV Act, 2005 provide?
**Definition (learn):** Protection orders (restraining violence), residence orders (right to reside in the shared household) and monetary relief. The Act's principal reliefs are civil and protective. (✅ owner 05)
**Breakdown:**
- Aggrieved woman may approach a Protection Officer or Magistrate.
- Residence order is independent of her ownership interest (owner 05 advanced section 2).
- Owner records that residence rights are contested where the property belongs to in-laws.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2005 (owner). (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** SJ-1100, SJ-1101, SJ-1102.
**Trap:** The DV Act is not purely a penal statute (owner trap).
**Mains hook:** Discuss protection and housing security as different functions of the Act.
**Back:** *"Protect, house, pay — civil reliefs first."*

### Flashcard SJ-1100 — Protection Officer and the protection/residence distinction
**Front:** What role does the Protection Officer play, and how do protection and residence orders differ?
**Definition (learn):** A Protection Officer, notified under the DV Act, helps aggrieved women get orders. A protection order restrains the respondent from violence or contact; a residence order secures the right to stay in the shared household. (✅ owner 05)
**Breakdown:**
- Protection order: safety function.
- Residence order: housing-security function.
- Enforcement depends on Magistrate orders and Protection Officer capacity (owner 05 advanced section 4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** SJ-1099, SJ-1101.
**Trap:** The two orders serve different functions; do not merge them.
**Mains hook:** Protection Officer vacancies are a named enforcement gap.
**Back:** *"Order keeps you safe, residence keeps you housed."*

### Flashcard SJ-1101 — DV Act Section 31: breach of protection order is criminal
**Front:** What does Section 31 of the DV Act make an offence?
**Definition (learn):** Breach of a protection order is a criminal offence under Section 31. (✅ owner 05 basic and advanced)
**Breakdown:**
- Cruelty may separately attract IPC/BNS prosecution (owner).
- The Act's reliefs are primarily civil; the breach is the criminal hook.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Section 31 (owner). (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** SJ-1099, SJ-1100.
**Trap:** The owner's trap: the DV Act is not wholly civil nor a general penal statute.
**Mains hook:** The criminal sanction attaches to breach, which is why enforcement capacity matters.
**Back:** *"Breach of the order, not the marriage, is the crime."*

### Flashcard SJ-1102 — DV Act: "relationship in the nature of marriage" (live-in partners)
**Front:** Does the DV Act cover live-in partners?
**Definition (learn):** Yes; the Act explicitly covers relationships in the nature of marriage, extending protection beyond legal marriage. Factual determination rests with the Magistrate (owner 05 advanced section 5). (✅ owner 05)
**Breakdown:**
- Other domestic relationships also covered (sisters, mothers) (owner).
- Scope expansion, not automatic protection.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** SJ-1099.
**Trap:** The DV Act does not apply only to legally married women (owner trap).
**Mains hook:** Scope of "domestic relationship" as an access-to-justice issue.
**Back:** *"Marriage in fact, not only in law."*

### Flashcard SJ-1103 — POSH Act, 2013: Internal Committee (10 or more employees)
**Front:** When must an employer constitute an Internal Committee under the POSH Act?
**Definition (learn):** Every establishment with ten or more employees must constitute an Internal Committee to receive and inquire into sexual harassment complaints. (✅ owner 05)
**Breakdown:**
- IC is the primary mechanism (owner 05 advanced section 2).
- Owner records that the external-member requirement is often unmet.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2013 Act; threshold ten (owner). (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** SJ-1104, SJ-1105, SJ-1106.
**Trap:** The POSH Act is not limited to the organised sector (owner trap).
**Mains hook:** IC compliance is the measurable test of the POSH law.
**Back:** *"Ten employees, one internal committee."*

### Flashcard SJ-1104 — POSH Act: Local Committee (district)
**Front:** What is the Local Committee and whom does it cover?
**Definition (learn):** A district-level committee constituted by the District Officer to receive complaints from workplaces with fewer than ten employees and from women in the unorganised sector. (✅ owner 05)
**Breakdown:**
- Safety-net extension (owner 05 advanced section 2).
- Capacity varies across districts (owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** SJ-1103, SJ-1105.
**Trap:** Local Committee is not a substitute for IC in larger workplaces.
**Mains hook:** Domestic workers may use the LC route because a dwelling is within "workplace" (owner advanced).
**Back:** *"Small workplace or unorganised: the district committee."*

### Flashcard SJ-1105 — POSH Act: 90-day inquiry and domestic-worker coverage
**Front:** Within how many days must the POSH inquiry be completed, and how is a dwelling treated?
**Definition (learn):** Inquiry is completed within 90 days (owner 05 basic section 3). A dwelling place or house is included within "workplace", so domestic workers can use the LC route (owner 05 advanced section 4).
**Breakdown:**
- Failure to comply attracts penalties; repeat offenders can lose licence (owner).
- Enforcement in homes is a persistent implementation gap (owner 05 advanced section 5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 90 days (owner). (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** SJ-1103, SJ-1104.
**Trap:** Domestic workers are not outside the POSH Act (owner).
**Mains hook:** Use domestic work as the boundary case for enforcement.
**Back:** *"Ninety days to inquire, homes included."*

### Flashcard SJ-1106 — Vishaka and the workplace-harassment route (owner-cited)
**Front:** What does the owner cite Vishaka for?
**Definition (learn):** The owner uses Vishaka to link equality and dignity to workplace protection, the precursor to the 2013 POSH Act. (⚠️ owner-cited; case year and holding not fetched)
**Breakdown:** Use the holding narrowly, as the owner instructs.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None asserted. ⚠️
**Links:** SJ-1103, SJ-1098.
**Trap:** Do not overstate the holding beyond the owner's wording.
**Mains hook:** Judicial guidance preceded and shaped the statutory route.
**Back:** *"Court guidance, then the Act."*

### Flashcard SJ-1107 — Mission Shakti (2021): Sambal and Samarthya
**Front:** What are the two sub-schemes of Mission Shakti?
**Definition (learn):** Mission Shakti is an MWCD umbrella scheme (2021) with two tracks: Sambal (safety and security, including One Stop Centres, Women Helpline, Beti Bachao Beti Padhao, Nari Adalat) and Samarthya (empowerment). (✅ owner 05; sub-component list ⚠️ owner-stated)
**Breakdown:**
- Integrates erstwhile standalone schemes.
- Current component mapping should be checked against MWCD guidelines (owner 05 basic section 3).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2021 (owner). (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** SJ-1098, SJ-1108, SJ-1121.
**Trap:** Do not attach a component to the wrong sub-scheme.
**Mains hook:** Mission Shakti is the umbrella test of protection plus empowerment.
**Back:** *"Sambal protects, Samarthya empowers."*

### Flashcard SJ-1108 — Maternity Benefit Act, 1961 (amended 2017): 26 weeks
**Front:** What does the Maternity Benefit Act (amended 2017) guarantee?
**Definition (learn):** 26 weeks of paid maternity leave for the first two surviving children in covered establishments with ten or more employees. (✅ owner 05)
**Breakdown:**
- Labour-law employer obligation (owner 05 advanced concept table).
- Organised-sector focus; check the covered establishment threshold.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1961 Act; 2017 amendment; 26 weeks (owner). (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** SJ-1109, SJ-1110.
**Trap:** The Act is not the same as PMMVY (SJ-1110).
**Mains hook:** Employer-funded leave versus state cash transfer: different instruments.
**Back:** *"Twenty-six weeks, employer pays, two children."*

### Flashcard SJ-1109 — PMMVY (conditional maternity-benefit DBT) — instalment structure conflict
**Front:** What is PMMVY and what does the owner state about its instalments?
**Definition (learn):** Pradhan Mantri Matru Vandana Yojana is a conditional DBT for eligible pregnant and lactating women. Owner states Rs 5,000 for the first child and Rs 6,000 for a second child if it is a girl, subject to health, birth and immunisation conditions. (⚠️ owner 05; instalment structure conflicts with memory, see Breakdown)
**Breakdown:**
- Owner (basic and advanced) says Rs 5,000 in two instalments for the first child; Rs 6,000 in one instalment for a second girl child.
- Memory suggests the first-child Rs 5,000 is paid in three instalments under PMMVY; the fetched MWCD page returned a certificate error, so the instalment count is NOT verified. Re-check the MWCD guideline before answering.
- Not a substitute for employer-funded leave.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Rs 5,000 and Rs 6,000 amounts (owner). ⚠️ instalment count.
**Links:** SJ-1108, SJ-1110.
**Trap:** Do not merge PMMVY with the MB Act's employer obligation.
**Mains hook:** Conditional DBT supports pregnancy wage loss, not a labour right.
**Back:** *"Five thousand first, six for a second girl — check the instalments."*

### Flashcard SJ-1110 — Trap: Maternity Benefit Act vs PMMVY
**Front:** Why are the Maternity Benefit Act and PMMVY not the same?
**Definition (learn):** The Act creates an employer obligation in covered establishments; PMMVY is a welfare cash transfer with exclusions tied to regular government or PSU employment (owner 05). (✅ owner 05)
**Breakdown:** Labour right versus welfare transfer.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** SJ-1108, SJ-1109.
**Trap:** An eligible PMMVY beneficiary still has no employer leave right.
**Mains hook:** Distinguish labour-law right from welfare transfer in precise answers.
**Back:** *"Employer leave versus state cash."*

### Flashcard SJ-1111 — National Commission for Women Act, 1990: powers
**Front:** What powers does the NCW have under the 1990 Act?
**Definition (learn):** NCW investigates complaints of deprivation of women's rights, takes suo motu cognisance, reviews legislation, advises on policy and inspects institutions affecting women. It recommends; it cannot enforce orders like a court. (✅ owner 05 advanced section 4)
**Breakdown:**
- Statutory body under the NCW Act, 1990.
- Follow-up depends on executive or judicial action.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1990 Act (owner). (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** SJ-1112, SJ-1120 (2020 Q15 route).
**Trap:** NCW can recommend; it cannot enforce.
**Mains hook:** Recognitive oversight is strong on voice and weak on enforcement.
**Back:** *"Investigate, recommend, advise — not enforce."*

### Flashcard SJ-1112 — Statutory vs constitutional body (NCW, NCSC, NCST)
**Front:** Why is NCW a statutory body while NCSC and NCST are constitutional bodies?
**Definition (learn):** NCW is under the NCW Act, 1990 and can be modified by ordinary legislation. NCSC (Article 338) and NCST (Article 338A) have constitutional mandates. (✅ owner 05; articles per owner)
**Breakdown:**
- Constitutional bodies have constitutionally fixed mandates.
- NCPCR is also statutory (SJ-1133).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Art 338, 338A (owner). (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** SJ-1111, SJ-1133.
**Trap:** NCW is not a constitutional body (owner trap).
**Mains hook:** Design choice: statutory bodies are easier to modify, which matters for independence.
**Back:** *"NCW by statute; NCSC and NCST by Article."*

### Flashcard SJ-1113 — Gender Budget Statement: Part A and Part B
**Front:** What do Part A and Part B of the Gender Budget Statement cover?
**Definition (learn):** Part A lists schemes with 100% women-specific allocation; Part B lists schemes with 30–99% pro-women allocation. It tracks allocation, not outcomes. (✅ owner 05)
**Breakdown:**
- Accountability tool, not a separate budget.
- Reform proposal: a third part tracking outcomes (owner 05 advanced section 4, ⚠️ suggestion).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Thresholds 100% and 30–99% (owner). (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** SJ-1114, SJ-1098.
**Trap:** Allocation is an input; empowerment is an outcome.
**Mains hook:** Gender budgeting tracks money; link it to NFHS outcomes for a complete answer.
**Back:** *"Part A full, Part B partial, outcomes missing."*

### Flashcard SJ-1114 — Trap: gender budget allocation vs outcome
**Front:** Why is "Gender Budget tracks outcomes" a trap?
**Definition (learn):** The Statement tracks budgetary allocation, not whether it translates into empowerment (owner 05 advanced concept table). (✅ owner 05)
**Breakdown:** Input (allocation) versus impact (NFHS-measured indicators).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** SJ-1113.
**Trap:** Rising allocation without outcome change is possible if delivery is weak.
**Mains hook:** A Mains answer must distinguish input from impact.
**Back:** *"Money counted is not change achieved."*

### Flashcard SJ-1115 — Shayara Bano and Joseph Shine (owner-cited)
**Front:** What do Shayara Bano and Joseph Shine stand for in the owner's case bank?
**Definition (learn):** Shayara Bano scrutinised discriminatory personal-law practice; Joseph Shine rejected a paternalistic adultery offence. Owner says use holdings narrowly. (⚠️ owner-cited; holdings not fetched)
**Breakdown:**
- Both are gender-justice case anchors in the owner's bank.
- Check holdings and years before stating them.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None asserted. ⚠️
**Links:** SJ-1098, SJ-1106.
**Trap:** Do not attribute to them a holding the owner does not record.
**Mains hook:** Use as judicial route for equality within personal law and sexual autonomy.
**Back:** *"Personal law tested, paternalism struck."*

### Flashcard SJ-1116 — Articles 243D/243T and the 106th Amendment (one-third reservation)
**Front:** What reservations do Articles 243D and 243T provide, and what does the 106th Amendment add?
**Definition (learn):** Articles 243D and 243T reserve local seats for women. The 106th Amendment creates one-third reservation for women in Lok Sabha and Assemblies, operational after the Article 334A census-delimitation sequence. (✅ owner 05 advanced section 3; ⚠️ article-level detail owner-stated)
**Breakdown:**
- Panchayat reservation of one-third (73rd Amendment, owner).
- Operational timing depends on delimitation (owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 106th Amendment (owner). (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** SJ-1119, SJ-1121.
**Trap:** Numerical presence does not defeat proxy control or party gatekeeping (owner Core).
**Mains hook:** Representation is not substantive power; test proxy control.
**Back:** *"One third local, one third Parliament once delimited."*

### Flashcard SJ-1117 — NFHS-5 women's account and phone access (owner-cited)
**Front:** What are the NFHS-5 access figures for women the owner cites?
**Definition (learn):** 78.6% of women aged 15–49 reported a bank or savings account they themselves used; 54.0% reported a mobile phone they themselves used. (⚠️ owner-stated; NFHS-5 figures not fetched)
**Breakdown:** Dated access baseline, not proof of control over assets or decisions.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** NFHS-5 (owner). ⚠️
**Links:** SJ-1036, SJ-1098.
**Trap:** Access is not control.
**Mains hook:** Use access indicators with a caution on autonomy.
**Back:** *"Account owned, decision not necessarily."*

### Flashcard SJ-1118 — NFHS-6 child marriage: 20.1% (owner-cited)
**Front:** What does the owner cite as NFHS-6 child-marriage prevalence?
**Definition (learn):** 20.1% of women aged 20–24 married before age 18, down from 23.3% in NFHS-5. (⚠️ owner-stated; not verified)
**Breakdown:** Prevalence indicator; not proof every marriage was detected.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** NFHS-6 (owner); round 2023–24 (Wikipedia confirms NFHS-6 listed). ⚠️ figure.
**Links:** SJ-1036, SJ-1131.
**Trap:** Do not splice NFHS-5 and NFHS-6.
**Mains hook:** Link prevalence decline to schooling, safety and household economics.
**Back:** *"Twenty-three point three to twenty point one."*

### Flashcard SJ-1119 — 2019 GS-II Q13 and 2021 GS-II Q2 routes
**Front:** What do the 2019 GS-II Q13 and 2021 GS-II Q2 ask?
**Definition (learn):** 2019 GS-II Q13 (15 marks): reservation of seats for women in local self-government and patriarchy. 2021 GS-II Q2 (10 marks): greater representation of women in the higher judiciary. (✅ owner 05 PYQ block)
**Breakdown:** Local-government and judicial representation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Years, marks per owner. (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** SJ-1116, SJ-1120.
**Trap:** Representation is not substantive power.
**Mains hook:** Use Articles 243D/T and judicial appointments with evidence.
**Back:** *"Seats for women, benches for women — and what they do."*

### Flashcard SJ-1120 — 2020 GS-II Q15, 2021 GS-II Q17, 2023 GS-II Q12 and Q14 routes
**Front:** What do these four PYQs ask?
**Definition (learn):** 2020 GS-II Q15: constitutionalisation of the NCW (15 marks). 2021 GS-II Q17: patriarchal attitudes and interventions beyond education and schemes (15 marks). 2023 GS-II Q12: constitutional perspectives on gender justice with provisions and case law (15 marks). 2023 GS-II Q14: civil society groups and women's representation in state legislatures (15 marks). (✅ owner 05 PYQ block)
**Breakdown:** Commission design; norms; constitutional doctrine; civil-society representation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Years and marks per owner. (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** SJ-1111, SJ-1112, SJ-1115, SJ-1116.
**Trap:** A commission's statutory design is different from constitutional status (SJ-1112).
**Mains hook:** Each route needs evidence and a counterpoint.
**Back:** *"Commission, norms, constitution, civil society."*

### Flashcard SJ-1121 — SHG social capital and the 2025 GS-II Q6 route (Governance cross-link)
**Front:** What does the 2025 GS-II Q6 ask, and what does the owner say?
**Definition (learn):** "Women's social capital complements in advancing empowerment and gender equity. Explain." (150 words; 10 marks). Owner routes social-capital theory to Governance (bonding, bridging, linking capital) and applies it to Mission Shakti's economic component. (✅ owner 05)
**Breakdown:**
- SHG chain: membership -> credit -> income -> assets.
- SHG federation leadership and Panchayat reservation convert economic participation into agency.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025, GS-II, Q6, 10 marks (owner). (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** SJ-1107, SJ-1098.
**Trap:** The PYQ tests the SHG pathway, not the full protection-empowerment architecture (owner 05 advanced).
**Mains hook:** Use SHGs as one mechanism alongside protective law, maternity support and gender budgeting.
**Back:** *"Groups build credit, credit builds agency."*

### GROUP — Children and Child Protection

### Flashcard SJ-1122 — POCSO Act, 2012: child below 18, gender-neutral
**Front:** Who is a "child" under POCSO, and is the Act gender-specific?
**Definition (learn):** Any person below 18 years; the Act is gender-neutral and protects all children regardless of sex. (✅ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- Dedicated statute for sexual offences against children.
- Graded offences: penetrative, aggravated penetrative, sexual harassment, use of child for pornography (owner 06 basic section 3).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2012; age below 18 (owner). (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** SJ-1123, SJ-1136, SJ-1144.
**Trap:** POCSO does not apply only to girls (owner trap).
**Mains hook:** POCSO is the default law for child sexual abuse, online or offline.
**Back:** *"Under eighteen, any gender, protected."*

### Flashcard SJ-1123 — POCSO: mandatory reporting, Special Courts, in-camera trial
**Front:** What procedural safeguards does POCSO provide?
**Definition (learn):** Mandatory reporting by anyone with knowledge of an offence, Special Courts for speedy trial, in-camera proceedings and child-friendly procedure (interpreter, special educator, support person). (✅ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- Child-friendly procedure is part of the statute (owner).
- Reporting obligation is universal, not limited to professionals.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None beyond owner. (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** SJ-1122, SJ-1136.
**Trap:** Mandatory reporting applies to any person with knowledge, not only to designated officers.
**Mains hook:** Child-friendly procedure and speed are the protection test.
**Back:** *"Report it, try it fast, in camera, with support."*

### Flashcard SJ-1124 — Juvenile Justice Act, 2015: two categories of children
**Front:** What are the two categories of children under the JJ Act, 2015?
**Definition (learn):** Children in conflict with law (alleged offenders) and children in need of care and protection. (✅ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- Conflict with law -> Juvenile Justice Board (JJB), rehabilitative focus.
- Care and protection -> Child Welfare Committee (CWC).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2015 Act (owner). (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** SJ-1125, SJ-1126, SJ-1127.
**Trap:** The owner says the bifurcation is a common examiner test; do not merge the two categories.
**Mains hook:** Distinguish the two routes before any juvenile-justice answer.
**Back:** *"Conflict goes to the Board, need goes to the Committee."*

### Flashcard SJ-1125 — Juvenile Justice Board composition
**Front:** Who sits on a Juvenile Justice Board?
**Definition (learn):** A Metropolitan or Judicial Magistrate plus two social workers; deals with children in conflict with law and disposes of cases after inquiry. (✅ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- Preliminary assessment for heinous offences (SJ-1127).
- Rehabilitative orientation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Composition (owner). (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** SJ-1124, SJ-1127.
**Trap:** The JJB is not the same body as the CWC.
**Mains hook:** JJB capacity and psychological evaluation infrastructure limit safeguards (owner 06 advanced).
**Back:** *"One magistrate, two social workers."*

### Flashcard SJ-1126 — Child Welfare Committee: powers and composition
**Front:** What powers does a Child Welfare Committee hold?
**Definition (learn):** A Committee of a chairperson and four members with the powers of a Bench of Magistrates, dealing with children in need of care and protection. Orders may include foster care, sponsorship, adoption or institutional care. (✅ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- Rehabilitation and care orders.
- Separate from JJB.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Chairperson plus four members (owner). (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** SJ-1124, SJ-1132.
**Trap:** A CWC is not a court for offences; it acts for children in need of care.
**Mains hook:** Care decisions need child-sensitive process and district oversight (owner 06 Core 11A).
**Back:** *"Chair and four, magistrate powers, care orders."*

### Flashcard SJ-1127 — JJ Act Section 15 preliminary assessment and Section 18(3) transfer (16–18, heinous)
**Front:** What must happen before a 16–18-year-old accused of a heinous offence is transferred to a Children's Court?
**Definition (learn):** The JJB first makes the Section 15 preliminary assessment of capacity, understanding and circumstances; only then may it transfer under Section 18(3). The Children's Court then applies the Act's safeguards. Adult treatment is not automatic. (✅ owner 06 basic and advanced)
**Breakdown:**
- Owner records the section numbers in its advanced file.
- Rehabilitation-deterrence tension (owner 06 advanced section 6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Section 15 and 18(3) (owner). (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** SJ-1128, SJ-1125, SJ-1144.
**Trap:** "All juveniles are tried by JJBs without exception" is the owner's trap; transfer is possible in limited cases.
**Mains hook:** Critically examine the transfer provision: safeguard versus dilution of rehabilitation.
**Back:** *"Assess first, transfer rarely, safeguards always."*

### Flashcard SJ-1128 — Heinous offence (JJ Act): seven-year minimum
**Front:** How does the JJ Act define a heinous offence?
**Definition (learn):** An offence for which the minimum punishment under the IPC or BNS is imprisonment of seven years or more. (✅ owner 06 advanced section 2)
**Breakdown:**
- Threshold is the minimum punishment, not the maximum.
- Triggers the 16–18 preliminary-assessment route (SJ-1127).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Seven years (owner). ⚠️ section number of definition not owner-stated.
**Links:** SJ-1127.
**Trap:** The threshold is the minimum punishment; do not use maximum.
**Mains hook:** Argue whether the seven-year minimum is the right trigger for the 16–18 assessment, given the rehabilitative principle.
**Back:** *"Minimum seven years, not maximum."*

### Flashcard SJ-1129 — Child Labour (Prohibition and Regulation) Amendment Act, 2016
**Front:** What does the 2016 Amendment prohibit?
**Definition (learn):** Complete prohibition of employment of children below 14 in any occupation; adolescents aged 14–18 are prohibited from hazardous occupations and processes listed in the Schedule. (✅ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- Family-enterprise and child-artist exceptions carry statutory conditions (owner 06 basic section 3).
- Not a general licence for child work.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2016 Amendment; under 14 complete ban; 14–18 hazardous ban (owner). (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** SJ-1130, SJ-1139 (ILO C138), SJ-1140 (Census).
**Trap:** Below 18 is not uniformly banned; only hazardous work is banned for 14–18 (owner trap).
**Mains hook: 10-mark option:** test the Amendment's enforcement against hidden and hazardous work.
**Back:** *"Under fourteen, none; fourteen to eighteen, not hazardous."*

### Flashcard SJ-1130 — Child labour exemptions (family enterprise and child artist)
**Front:** What are the narrow exceptions in the 2016 Amendment?
**Definition (learn):** The family-enterprise and child-artist exceptions, which carry statutory conditions. (✅ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- Exceptions are conditional, not a general licence.
- Owner notes apprenticeship exemptions with restrictions (owner 06 Core 4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None beyond owner. (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** SJ-1129.
**Trap:** Family work is not automatically lawful.
**Mains hook:** Exceptions are where enforcement often breaks.
**Back:** *"Exception with conditions, not a licence."*

### Flashcard SJ-1131 — Prohibition of Child Marriage Act, 2006
**Front:** What does PCMA 2006 provide on marriage age and validity?
**Definition (learn):** Child marriage (boy below 21, girl below 18) is generally voidable at the option of the party who was a child at marriage; specified forced, trafficked or abducted-child situations are void. Child Marriage Prohibition Officers (CMPOs) prevent solemnisation and seek injunctions. (✅ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- Voidable, not automatically void, is the key distinction.
- CMPO role is preventive.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2006 Act; ages 21 (boy) and 18 (girl) (owner). (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** SJ-1118 (NFHS-6), SJ-1122.
**Trap:** Voidable is not void; the option belongs to the child-party.
**Mains hook:** Punishment alone can drive unions underground; schooling and safety matter (owner 06 Core).
**Back:** *"Twenty-one for boys, eighteen for girls, voidable by the child."*

### Flashcard SJ-1132 — Mission Vatsalya and Child Helpline 1098
**Front:** What does Mission Vatsalya cover, and what helpline does it run?
**Definition (learn):** MWCD's umbrella child-protection scheme covering institutional care (Child Care Institutions under the JJ Act), Child Helpline 1098, sponsorship and foster care, and JJ Act implementation support. Restructured from the erstwhile Integrated Child Protection Scheme (ICPS). (✅ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- Institutional and non-institutional care.
- Helpline 1098.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Restructured from ICPS (owner). (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** SJ-1126, SJ-1133.
**Trap:** Mission Vatsalya is not NCPCR; one is the scheme, the other a statutory commission.
**Mains hook:** Scheme delivery must link to CWC and NCPCR oversight.
**Back:** *"Vatsalya runs the care, 1098 answers the call."*

### Flashcard SJ-1133 — NCPCR: Commissions for Protection of Child Rights Act, 2005
**Front:** What is NCPCR's composition and statutory basis?
**Definition (learn):** The National Commission for Protection of Child Rights, a statutory body under the Commissions for Protection of Child Rights Act, 2005. Chairperson plus six Members, at least two women. (✅ owner 06 and owner 06 advanced)
**Breakdown:**
- Examines safeguards, inquires into complaints, recommends measures, inspects institutions.
- Statutory, not constitutional (owner trap).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2005 Act; Chairperson plus six Members (owner). (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** SJ-1112, SJ-1134, SJ-1135.
**Trap:** NCPCR is not a constitutional body.
**Mains hook:** NCPCR is a coordinating and recommending body, not an enforcer.
**Back:** *"Chair and six, two women, statutory oversight."*

### Flashcard SJ-1134 — NCPCR vs State Commissions (SCPCR)
**Front:** How do NCPCR and State Commissions for Protection of Child Rights relate?
**Definition (learn):** NCPCR is the national Commission; State Commissions (SCPCRs) perform parallel functions at state level. Coordination between them is essential for monitoring. (⚠️ owner 06 advanced concept table; SCPCR existence and parallel role are owner-stated)
**Breakdown:**
- Coordination is an advisory and monitoring mechanism.
- No single body can monitor all states alone (owner 06 advanced section 6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None asserted. ⚠️
**Links:** SJ-1133, SJ-1135.
**Trap:** Do not describe SCPCR as a subordinate of NCPCR without a source.
**Mains hook:** NCPCR's reach depends on State Commissions; argue that coordination, not a single national body, is what makes monitoring work.
**Back:** *"One national, many state, one coordinated watch."*

### Flashcard SJ-1135 — NCPCR's limited powers (advisory, no enforcement)
**Front:** Why can NCPCR not enforce orders against platforms?
**Definition (learn):** NCPCR can examine, inquire, recommend and advise; it cannot enforce orders like a court. Enforcement against platforms runs through IT Rules and MeitY or other authorities (owner 06 advanced section 5). (✅ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- Platform sanction is MeitY's domain, not NCPCR's.
- Trap: "NCPCR can order intermediaries to remove CSAM" is wrong (owner trap).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** SJ-1137, SJ-1133, SJ-1141.
**Trap:** Recommendatory power is not a removal order.
**Mains hook:** NCPCR's leverage is coordination and publicity, not sanctions.
**Back:** *"Recommends, flags, follows up; does not order takedowns."*

### Flashcard SJ-1136 — POCSO e-Box and the National Cybercrime Reporting Portal
**Front:** What reporting channels does the owner name for online child sexual abuse?
**Definition (learn):** NCPCR's POCSO e-Box enables online reporting of child sexual abuse; the National Cybercrime Reporting Portal has a dedicated child-abuse category routed to state police cyber cells. (✅ owner 06 advanced section 3)
**Breakdown:**
- e-Box is NCPCR's channel.
- Cybercrime Portal is police-facing.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None asserted. (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** SJ-1122, SJ-1141, SJ-1137.
**Trap:** Reporting a case is not the same as NCPCR removing content.
**Mains hook:** Reporting channels exist; coordination and follow-up are the gap.
**Back:** *"e-Box for NCPCR, portal for police."*

### Flashcard SJ-1137 — IT Rules intermediary obligations (no universal deadline)
**Front:** What do the IT Rules require of intermediaries on child sexual content?
**Definition (learn):** Intermediaries must publish user rules barring child sexual content and comply with notice, order, takedown and cooperation duties. Significant intermediaries have additional due-diligence measures. (✅ owner 06 advanced)
**Breakdown:**
- Owner warns not to claim one universal proactive-detection duty or deadline.
- Cite the applicable intermediary category and rule.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None asserted. ⚠️ rule numbers not verified.
**Links:** SJ-1135, SJ-1141, SJ-1136.
**Trap:** Do not invent a universal removal deadline.
**Mains hook:** Platform duty depends on category; encryption is the trade-off.
**Back:** *"Rules by category, not one deadline for all."*

### Flashcard SJ-1138 — DPDP Act child-data obligations: scheduled 13 May 2027 (owner-stated)
**Front:** When do the DPDP child-data duties start, per the owner?
**Definition (learn):** The DPDP Act, 2023 has child-data safeguards; the 2025 Rules set phased commencement. The owner states substantive child-data duties, including verifiable parental consent, are scheduled for 13 May 2027. (⚠️ owner-stated; date not verified)
**Breakdown:**
- Not current enforcement as at the owner's 21 July 2026 check.
- Use POCSO and IT Rules for immediate harm.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 13 May 2027 (owner). ⚠️
**Links:** SJ-1137, SJ-1141.
**Trap:** Do not describe DPDP child-data duties as currently enforceable.
**Mains hook:** A notified future framework, not a present enforcement tool.
**Back:** *"Parental consent from 2027, POCSO now."*

### Flashcard SJ-1139 — ILO Conventions 138 and 182
**Front:** What do ILO Conventions 138 and 182 require?
**Definition (learn):** C138 (Minimum Age Convention) requires a national minimum age for employment and progressive abolition of child labour. C182 (Worst Forms of Child Labour) requires urgent elimination of slavery-like practices, trafficking, forced recruitment, commercial sexual exploitation, illicit activities and hazardous work. (✅ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- Both are Conventions, binding on ratifying states; contrast with Recommendation 202 (SJ-1015).
- India-specific ratification status is not stated in the owner.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Two conventions (owner). (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** SJ-1015, SJ-1129, SJ-1142.
**Trap:** A Recommendation is not a Convention (SJ-1015).
**Mains hook:** Use C182's worst-forms list to test whether India's hazardous-work ban covers the forms the Convention names.
**Back:** *"138 sets the minimum age, 182 bans the worst forms."*

### Flashcard SJ-1140 — Census 2011: about 10.1 million child workers aged 5–14 (dated)
**Front:** What dated figure does the owner give for child labour?
**Definition (learn):** Census 2011 counted about 10.1 million workers aged 5–14, main plus marginal. (✅ owner 06; figure matches general knowledge but was not fetched ⚠️)
**Breakdown:**
- Does not capture hidden, unpaid or hazardous forms.
- A decade-old measure; use with its year.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Census 2011; 10.1 million (owner). ⚠️ not fetched.
**Links:** SJ-1129, SJ-1139.
**Trap:** Census figures are not current child labour counts.
**Mains hook:** Cite the census year; argue that hidden labour is undercounted.
**Back:** *"Two thousand eleven: ten million children at work."*

### Flashcard SJ-1141 — 2025 GS-II Q18: NCPCR and the digital-era challenge
**Front:** What does the 2025 GS-II Q18 ask, and what route does the owner give?
**Definition (learn):** "The National Commission for Protection of Child Rights has to address the challenges faced by children in the digital era. Examine the existing policies and suggest measures the Commission can initiate to tackle the issue." (250 words; 15 marks). (✅ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- Challenges: online sexual exploitation, CSAM, cyberbullying, data privacy, screen-time.
- Existing policies: POCSO, IT Rules, e-Box, Cybercrime Portal.
- NCPCR measures: monitoring cell, platform reviews, cyber-safety curriculum, age verification advocacy, data-protection coordination.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025, GS-II, Q18, 15 marks (owner). (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** SJ-1135, SJ-1136, SJ-1137, SJ-1138.
**Trap:** "Awareness" alone is generic; the answer needs policy-linked measures (owner 06 advanced).
**Mains hook:** Map challenge -> policy -> gap -> NCPCR measure.
**Back:** *"Challenge, policy, gap, NCPCR action."*

### Flashcard SJ-1142 — 2018 Prelims Q30: ILO Conventions 138 and 182 (routed, key not held)
**Front:** What is the routed 2018 Prelims Q30 demand?
**Definition (learn):** The subject matter of ILO Conventions 138 and 182. Owner records the key as unavailable locally; no option recorded. (✅ owner 06 PYQ block)
**Breakdown:** Cover minimum age (138) and worst forms (182).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2018, Prelims GS-I, Q30 (owner). (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** SJ-1139, SJ-1015.
**Trap:** Do not confuse the two conventions' subject matter.
**Mains hook:** Map C138 minimum-age and C182 worst-forms duties onto the Child Labour Amendment, noting that ratified Conventions bind while Recommendations do not.
**Back:** *"Minimum age and worst forms."*

### Flashcard SJ-1143 — JJ Amendment 2021 (owner-cited, unverified)
**Front:** What does the owner say the 2021 JJ Amendment did?
**Definition (learn):** The owner says it strengthened District Magistrate oversight of adoption and child-care institutions and reclassified specified offences. (⚠️ owner-only; not verified)
**Breakdown:** Accountability must preserve child-sensitive decision-making (owner 06 Core 11A).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2021 (owner). ⚠️
**Links:** SJ-1124, SJ-1126.
**Trap:** Do not attribute the reclassification details beyond the owner's wording.
**Mains hook:** Oversight and child-sensitivity are in tension.
**Back:** *"Magistrates oversee, children stay central."*

### Flashcard SJ-1144 — Trap: "POCSO only for female children" and "all juveniles go to JJB"
**Front:** Name the two child-protection traps in the owner's trap list.
**Definition (learn):** POCSO is gender-neutral (SJ-1122). All juveniles are not tried only by JJBs; a 16–18-year-old accused of a heinous offence may be transferred after the Section 15 assessment (SJ-1127). (✅ owner 06)
**Breakdown:** Two different traps; both test statute scope.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** SJ-1122, SJ-1127.
**Trap:** Both are examiner distractors.
**Mains hook:** Use these two traps to test whether an answer on juvenile justice or POCSO knows the statutory scope before offering a view.
**Back:** *"Any gender under POCSO; transfer is not automatic."*

### Flashcard SJ-1145 — Trap: NCPCR as a constitutional body
**Front:** Why is "NCPCR is a constitutional body" wrong?
**Definition (learn):** NCPCR is statutory under the 2005 Act; constitutional bodies include NCSC (Article 338) and NCST (Article 338A) (owner 06 basic traps). (✅ owner 06)
**Breakdown:** Statutory versus constitutional status changes the amendment route.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Art 338, 338A (owner). (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** SJ-1112, SJ-1133.
**Trap:** Same trap as NCW (SJ-1112).
**Mains hook:** Statutory status decides how easily Parliament can change the body's mandate, which bears on its independence.
**Back:** *"NCPCR by statute, not Article."*

## SOCIAL JUSTICE — Part 2: SC, ST, OBC/EWS, Minorities, Disability, Elderly, Transgender and Marginalised Communities

Source tags: `(✅ owner NN)` = repository owner (basic and/or advanced); `(✅ Wikipedia: <page>)` = fetched page; `(✅ PRS)` = PRS bill tracker fetched; `⚠️` = unverified, memory-only, or fetch failed. Owner-vs-source conflicts are stated inside the card.

### GROUP — Scheduled Castes: Rights, Atrocities and Welfare
Owners: `basic/07_Scheduled-Castes-Rights-Atrocities-and-Welfare.md` and `advanced/07_Scheduled-Castes-Rights-Atrocities-and-Welfare.md`.

### Flashcard SJ-2001 — SC protection architecture: legal remedy, welfare delivery, targeting
**Front:** What are the instruments of Scheduled Caste protection, and what failure does each address?
**Definition (learn):** SC protection in India runs through three linked channels: legal remedy against atrocities, welfare delivery through schemes and credit, and, since August 2024, evidence-based targeting within the SC category.
**Breakdown:**
- Legal remedy: SC and ST (Prevention of Atrocities) Act, 1989, amended 2015 and 2018.
- Welfare delivery: PM-AJAY (umbrella, MoSJE) and NSFDC (concessional credit).
- Institutional remedy: National Commission for Scheduled Castes (Article 338).
- Targeting: *State of Punjab v. Davinder Singh* (1 Aug 2024) permits data-supported sub-classification.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** SCs were 16.6% of population in Census 2011 (✅ owner 07). Core architecture (✅ owner 07).
**Links:** SJ-2002, SJ-2011, SJ-2012, SJ-2013, SJ-2015
**Trap:** Treating welfare schemes as legal protection. The PoA Act punishes and gives relief; PM-AJAY and NSFDC deliver project and credit support. Neither substitutes for the other.
**Mains hook:** Protection without credible local enforcement, and welfare without within-SC targeting, both leave gaps. ⚠️ Evaluative close.
**Back:** *"Law shields, schemes lift, data aims."*

### Flashcard SJ-2002 — Scheduled Castes and Scheduled Tribes (Prevention of Atrocities) Act, 1989
**Front:** When was the PoA Act passed and notified, and what is its basic design?
**Definition (learn):** A special penal law giving enhanced punishment for listed caste-based atrocities against SCs and STs, with Special Courts and victim relief.
**Breakdown:**
- Passed by Parliament on 11 September 1989; notified 30 January 1990 (✅ Wikipedia: SC/ST PoA Act, 1989).
- Core machinery: FIR for listed offences; investigation by DSP or above (✅ owner 07); trial in Special Courts; victim relief per the Schedule (✅ owner 07).
- Relief rules: Atrocities Rules, 1995, as amended (✅ owner 07 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Passed 11 Sep 1989; notified 30 Jan 1990; amended 2015 (assent 31 Dec 2015, in force 26 Jan 2016) and 2018 (Section 18A, effective 20 Aug 2018) (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2003, SJ-2004, SJ-2005, SJ-2008
**Trap:** Exclusive Special Courts were added by the 2015 Amendment, not by the 1989 text (✅ Wikipedia). Do not describe the 1989 Act as already having that architecture.
**Mains hook:** The Act is the legal first line; its reach depends on State notification of courts, prosecutor capacity and investigation quality.
**Back:** *"Eighty-nine enacted, ninety notified, fifteen widened, eighteen re-armed."*

### Flashcard SJ-2003 — Atrocity offences and victim relief under the PoA Act
**Front:** Who is the offender and what kinds of acts count as an atrocity under the PoA Act?
**Definition (learn):** Listed offences in the Act's Schedule committed against an SC or ST person by a non-SC/ST person, which attract enhanced punishment.
**Breakdown:**
- Examples in the owner's list: assault, wrongful dispossession, forced labour, sexual exploitation, public humiliation (✅ owner 07).
- 2015 examples: garlanding with footwear, tonsuring of the head, forcing manual scavenging (✅ Wikipedia: SC/ST PoA Act, 1989).
- Before 2015 the Act had 19 punishable offences; the 2015 Amendment roughly doubled recognised crimes (✅ Wikipedia).
- Victim relief: monetary compensation, shelter, travel expenses per the Schedule (✅ owner 07).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Offender category is non-SC/ST; victim category is SC/ST (✅ owner 07).
**Links:** SJ-2002, SJ-2005, SJ-2006, SJ-2023
**Trap:** Assuming every caste-tinged offence is an atrocity. Only offences in the Schedule, with the statutory victim-offender relation, qualify. Non-physical boycotts are a boundary case (⚠️ owner 07 advanced, illustrative).
**Mains hook:** Use the 2015 list expansion to show that law can be broad while registration and conviction stay weak. Cite data with a denominator (see SJ-2023).
**Back:** *"Listed harm, outside doer, caste victim, relief owed."*

### Flashcard SJ-2004 — Special Courts and Special Public Prosecutors under the PoA Act
**Front:** What are Special and Exclusive Special Courts under the PoA Act, and who is notified to them?
**Definition (learn):** Courts designated to try PoA offences, with Special Public Prosecutors, created in their exclusive form by the 2015 Amendment and notified by the State.
**Breakdown:**
- Special Public Prosecutors conduct the prosecution (✅ owner 07 advanced).
- Exclusive Special Court provision added in 2015 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Notification depends on statutory conditions and State action; owner 07 says notification follows the Chief Justice's concurrence (✅ owner 07 basic).
- 2015 aim: trial completion, as far as possible, within two months of the charge sheet (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** No automatic one-court-per-district rule (✅ owner 07).
**Links:** SJ-2005, SJ-2006, SJ-2023
**Trap:** "Every district has an Exclusive Special Court." The owner rejects this. Verify State notification for the specific State.
**Mains hook:** Court capacity, pendency and witness vulnerability decide outcomes more than the statute text. ⚠️ Analytical inference.
**Back:** *"Special court, special prosecutor, notified not automatic."*

### Flashcard SJ-2005 — The 2015 Amendment to the PoA Act
**Front:** What did the 2015 Amendment change in the PoA Act?
**Definition (learn):** An amendment that expanded atrocity offences, strengthened victim and witness rights, and created the Special and Exclusive Special Court architecture.
**Breakdown:**
- Passed with presidential assent 31 Dec 2015; in force 26 Jan 2016; Act No. 1 of 2016 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Offence list expanded; Section 3 renumbered (✅ Wikipedia).
- New Section 14A (time-bound trial) and Section 15A (victim and witness rights) (✅ Wikipedia).
- Schedule of IPC offences added (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Assent 31 Dec 2015; in force 26 Jan 2016 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2002, SJ-2004, SJ-2006, SJ-2008
**Trap:** Confusing the 2015 Amendment (expansion) with the 2018 Amendment (procedural safeguards reversed).
**Mains hook:** Shows Parliament widening protection; the next question is whether the widened list is enforced.
**Back:** *"Fifteen widened the list, sixteen put it in force."*

### Flashcard SJ-2006 — Sections 14A and 15A of the PoA Act (victims, witnesses, time-bound trial)
**Front:** What do Sections 14A and 15A of the PoA Act provide?
**Definition (learn):** Section 14A provides for time-bound trials; Section 15A sets out rights of victims and witnesses. Both were inserted by the 2015 Amendment.
**Breakdown:**
- Section 14A: trial completion, as far as possible, within two months of the charge sheet (✅ Wikipedia).
- Section 15A: victim and witness rights, including protection and participation (✅ Wikipedia; detailed content ⚠️ not fetched).
- Witness vulnerability is cited by the owner as a driver of low conviction (✅ owner 07 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Inserted 2015 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2004, SJ-2005, SJ-2023
**Trap:** "Time-bound" in Section 14A is an "as far as possible" direction, not an absolute deadline (✅ Wikipedia wording).
**Mains hook:** Protective provisions on paper need witness-protection capacity to produce convictions. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Fourteen-A clocks the trial, fifteen-A guards the witness."*

### Flashcard SJ-2007 — Subhash Kashinath Mahajan (20 March 2018)
**Front:** What did the Supreme Court's March 2018 ruling in Subhash Kashinath Mahajan do to the PoA Act?
**Definition (learn):** A Supreme Court ruling that diluted the Act's stringent arrest and anticipatory-bail provisions and barred immediate arrest, which provoked the 2018 Amendment.
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 20 March 2018 (✅ Wikipedia: SC/ST PoA Act, 1989).
- Effect: banned immediate arrest and diluted the Act's provisions (✅ Wikipedia).
- Response: Parliament inserted Section 18A (SJ-2008).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 20 Mar 2018 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2008, SJ-2009, SJ-2010
**Trap:** The 2018 Amendment did not implement the Court's direction; it overrode it (SJ-2010).
**Mains hook:** A textbook case of legislative override of a judicial ruling, relevant to the separation-of-powers theme (⚠️ owner 07 Mains angles).
**Back:** *"March eighteen, the Court eased arrest; August made it stern again."*

### Flashcard SJ-2008 — The 2018 Amendment and Section 18A
**Front:** What does Section 18A of the PoA Act, inserted in 2018, provide?
**Definition (learn):** A statutory insertion that removes the preliminary-inquiry and arrest-approval requirements and restores the bar on anticipatory bail for PoA offences.
**Breakdown:**
- No preliminary inquiry before FIR registration (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 07).
- No approval needed before arrest; investigating officer may arrest (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 07).
- Anticipatory bail is ruled out for the statutory offences (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 07).
- Effective 20 August 2018 when Parliament passed it (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Effective 20 Aug 2018 (✅ Wikipedia). Upheld 10 Feb 2020 (SJ-2009).
**Links:** SJ-2007, SJ-2009, SJ-2010
**Trap:** Section 18A is not a general repeal of all bail safeguards; the owner notes the narrow *Prathvi Raj Chauhan* exception (SJ-2009).
**Mains hook:** Section 18A protects victims' procedural access but raises due-process questions for the accused; a balanced answer names both.
**Back:** *"No inquiry, no approval, no anticipatory bail."*

### Flashcard SJ-2009 — Prathvi Raj Chauhan (10 February 2020)
**Front:** What did the Supreme Court decide in February 2020 on the 2018 Amendment?
**Definition (learn):** The Court upheld the validity of Section 18A while recognising that constitutional courts may still intervene in rare cases where no prima facie case is disclosed.
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 10 Feb 2020 (✅ Wikipedia: SC/ST PoA Act, 1989).
- Case name *Prathvi Raj Chauhan*: ✅ owner 07 (name not confirmed by fetched page).
- Exception: rare constitutional-court protection where no prima facie case exists (✅ owner 07).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Upheld 10 Feb 2020.
**Links:** SJ-2007, SJ-2008, SJ-2010
**Trap:** Treating the 2020 ruling as striking down Section 18A, or as removing the prima-facie safeguard entirely. Neither is correct.
**Mains hook:** Shows judicial balancing between victim protection and the accused's safeguards.
**Back:** *"Twenty upheld the shield, kept a door for no-case complaints."*

### Flashcard SJ-2010 — Trap: the 2018 PoA Amendment "implemented" a Supreme Court direction
**Front:** Did the 2018 PoA Amendment implement a Supreme Court direction?
**Definition (learn):** No. The Amendment overrode the 2018 Supreme Court ruling in *Subhash Kashinath Mahajan*, restoring the stringent provisions.
**Breakdown:**
- Ruling: *Subhash Kashinath Mahajan*, 20 Mar 2018 (SJ-2007).
- Amendment: Section 18A, 2018 (SJ-2008).
- Owner correction: Parliament reinstated the original stringent provisions (✅ owner 07 traps).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Ruling Mar 2018; override Aug 2018 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2007, SJ-2008, SJ-2009
**Trap:** The phrase "passed to implement" is the distractor; the correct phrase is "passed to override".
**Mains hook:** Use as an example of Parliament responding to judicial reasoning.
**Back:** *"Implement is the decoy; override is the answer."*

### Flashcard SJ-2011 — PM-AJAY (Pradhan Mantri Anusuchit Jaati Abhyudaya Yojana)
**Front:** What is PM-AJAY, and what are its main components?
**Definition (learn):** An umbrella MoSJE framework for SC development that works through approved village, district and state projects and hostel support, not automatic individual entitlements.
**Breakdown:**
- Adarsh Gram development for SC-dominated villages (✅ owner 07).
- Grants for approved district and state socio-economic projects (✅ owner 07).
- Hostel support in higher educational institutions (✅ owner 07).
- Owner says it merges erstwhile standalone schemes (✅ owner 07).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Launch year not stated in owner; ⚠️ not fetched.
**Links:** SJ-2001, SJ-2012, SJ-2014
**Trap:** Treating every MoSJE SC scheme as a PM-AJAY component, or PM-AJAY as an automatic scholarship or loan (SJ-2014).
**Mains hook:** Targeted SC development through projects needs proposal capacity and state uptake; state the delivery route and reporting date when citing coverage.
**Back:** *"Village, district, hostel: projects, not entitlements."*

### Flashcard SJ-2012 — NSFDC (National Scheduled Castes Finance and Development Corporation)
**Front:** What is NSFDC and how does it deliver credit?
**Definition (learn):** A Government of India company under MoSJE that provides concessional credit, micro-credit and skill-development finance to SC beneficiaries through state channelising agencies.
**Breakdown:**
- Status: PSU under MoSJE (✅ owner 07 advanced).
- Delivery: state channelising agencies (✅ owner 07).
- Complements PM-AJAY's livelihood support (✅ owner 07 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Year of establishment not stated in owner; ⚠️ not fetched.
**Links:** SJ-2001, SJ-2011, SJ-2014
**Trap:** Confusing NSFDC (credit company) with NCSC (constitutional rights body) or with PM-AJAY (project umbrella).
**Mains hook:** Credit access widens mobility only when linked to skills, markets and non-discrimination. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Credit company, state channels, SC borrowers."*

### Flashcard SJ-2013 — National Commission for Scheduled Castes (NCSC), Article 338
**Front:** What is the status of the NCSC, and what does it do?
**Definition (learn):** A constitutional body under Article 338 that investigates SC-rights violations, advises on safeguards and reviews their implementation.
**Breakdown:**
- Status: constitutional body under Article 338 (✅ owner 07).
- Functions: investigate violations, advise on policy and safeguards (✅ owner 07 advanced).
- Composition and detailed mandate: cross-linked to Polity owner (✅ owner 07).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Article 338 (✅ owner 07). Constitutional amendment year ⚠️ not stated in owner.
**Links:** SJ-2014 (trap), SJ-2001, SJ-2024
**Trap:** NCSC is a constitutional body; the National Commission for Women is statutory under the NCW Act, 1990 (✅ owner 07 traps).
**Mains hook:** Constitutional status gives institutional security; enforcement still depends on State response. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Three-thirty-eight is constitutional, not statutory."*

### Flashcard SJ-2014 — Trap: PM-AJAY is an automatic individual entitlement
**Front:** Is PM-AJAY an automatic scholarship or loan entitlement for every SC individual?
**Definition (learn):** No. PM-AJAY is a project framework that funds approved Adarsh Gram development, district and state socio-economic projects and hostel support.
**Breakdown:**
- Individual benefits flow through other schemes or NSFDC credit with their own eligibility (✅ owner 07 traps).
- Approval comes through State and district proposals (✅ owner 07).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 07 trap entry (✅ owner 07).
**Links:** SJ-2011, SJ-2012, SJ-2013
**Trap:** The distractor is "automatic individual entitlement". The correct answer is "umbrella project framework".
**Mains hook:** Distinguish entitlement, project and credit routes in any welfare-delivery answer.
**Back:** *"Projects flow through proposals, not personal claims."*

### Flashcard SJ-2015 — *State of Punjab v. Davinder Singh* (1 August 2024)
**Front:** What did the Supreme Court decide in *State of Punjab v. Davinder Singh* on 1 August 2024?
**Definition (learn):** A seven-judge Constitution Bench ruling by a 6:1 majority that states may sub-classify Scheduled Castes for reservation when supported by quantifiable empirical data.
**Breakdown:**
- Bench: seven judges, 6:1 majority (✅ Wikipedia: State of Punjab v. Davinder Singh).
- Citation: 2024 INSC 562 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Overruled *E.V. Chinnaiah* (SJ-2016).
- Holding: states may sub-classify for reservation in public employment and education, grounded in Articles 15 and 16 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Owner focus: sub-classification within SCs for reservation and welfare (✅ owner 07).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Judgment 1 Aug 2024; 6:1; overruled *E.V. Chinnaiah* (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2016, SJ-2017, SJ-2018, SJ-2019, SJ-2021
**Trap:** The Court permits sub-classification; it does not mandate a sub-quota (SJ-2021).
**Mains hook:** Targeting within SCs is now constitutionally possible; its legitimacy rests on empirical data and political-unity risks. ⚠️ Evaluative close.
**Back:** *"Six to one: divide with data, never by decree."*

### Flashcard SJ-2016 — *E.V. Chinnaiah v. State of Andhra Pradesh* (2004)
**Front:** Which 2004 decision did *Davinder Singh* overrule, and what did it hold?
**Definition (learn):** A five-judge bench decision that treated Scheduled Castes as one homogeneous group that could not be sub-divided for reservation.
**Breakdown:**
- Year and bench: 2004, five judges (✅ Wikipedia: State of Punjab v. Davinder Singh).
- Holding: SCs homogeneous; no internal sub-division (✅ Wikipedia).
- Overruled on 1 Aug 2024 (SJ-2015).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2004; five-judge bench; overruled 2024 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2015, SJ-2017
**Trap:** Thinking *Chinnaiah* is still good law on SC homogeneity. It is overruled.
**Mains hook:** Shows how a judicial reading of "homogeneous SCs" changed with data on within-SC inequality. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Two-thousand-four said one block; twenty-twenty-four said divide with data."*

### Flashcard SJ-2017 — Davinder Singh: legal basis and the evidence requirement
**Front:** On what constitutional basis, and under what evidential condition, may states sub-classify SCs after *Davinder Singh*?
**Definition (learn):** States may sub-classify SCs and STs for reservation under Articles 15 and 16, but only on quantifiable and empirical data showing relative backwardness and inadequate representation.
**Breakdown:**
- Basis: Articles 15 and 16 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Evidence: quantifiable and empirical data on backwardness and representation in public service (✅ Wikipedia).
- Article 341: the President identifies which castes are on the SC list; states apportion benefits within it (✅ Wikipedia).
- Owner: the judgment does not allocate a sub-quota itself (✅ owner 07).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Evidence test: quantifiable and empirical data (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2015, SJ-2018, SJ-2021
**Trap:** Wikipedia says the holding covers SCs and STs; the owner's focus is SCs only. Flag: ⚠️ conflict in scope; the card keeps both. Check the judgment text before a precise answer.
**Mains hook:** A sub-classification without data is arbitrary and legally vulnerable; state the evidence condition in any answer (✅ owner 07 advanced).
**Back:** *"Articles fifteen and sixteen, backed by numbers, not hunches."*

### Flashcard SJ-2018 — Davinder Singh: Justice Trivedi's dissent and the Presidential List
**Front:** Who was the lone dissenter in *Davinder Singh*, and what did the dissent argue?
**Definition (learn):** Justice Bela M. Trivedi delivered the sole dissent, arguing that sub-classification amounts to tinkering with the Presidential List.
**Breakdown:**
- Dissent: Justice Bela M. Trivedi (✅ Wikipedia).
- Argument: sub-classification tinkers with the Presidential List (✅ Wikipedia).
- Majority position: Presidential List is not altered; Article 341 limits the President's power to identifying castes (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Vote 6:1 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2015, SJ-2017
**Trap:** The dissent is not the holding. Do not treat the Presidential-List objection as the law.
**Mains hook:** The debate pits constitutional list-making against state-level apportionment; present both sides.
**Back:** *"One dissent, one list, six for apportionment."*

### Flashcard SJ-2019 — Davinder Singh: creamy-layer remarks (source conflict)
**Front:** Did *Davinder Singh* make a binding creamy-layer rule for SCs and STs?
**Definition (learn):** No binding rule has been established. The majority's creamy-layer remarks are observations; sources differ on how prominent they were.
**Breakdown:**
- Wikipedia: the majority endorsed applying the creamy-layer exclusion to SCs and STs as an "observation" that could lead to future legislative or judicial action; bindingness not stated (✅ Wikipedia).
- Owner: creamy-layer comments appeared in separate opinions, not the binding holding (✅ owner 07).
- No general SC/ST creamy-layer exclusion has been legislated (✅ owner 07).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Observation status; no legislated SC/ST creamy-layer (✅ owner 07).
**Links:** SJ-2015, SJ-2022, SJ-2056
**Trap:** ⚠️ Conflict: owner says "separate opinions"; Wikipedia says "majority". Do not state which without the judgment text. Either way, it is not an enacted rule.
**Mains hook:** Creamy-layer logic for SCs and STs is contested; it is not the same as the OBC rule (SJ-2056).
**Back:** *"Remarks are not rules; check the bench before quoting."*

### Flashcard SJ-2020 — Punjab's 1975 notification (background to sub-classification)
**Front:** What earlier Punjab measure illustrates the within-SC division that *Davinder Singh* addressed?
**Definition (learn):** A 1975 Punjab notification gave 50% of reserved SC seats to the Valmiki and Mazhabi Sikh communities.
**Breakdown:**
- Measure: 1975 Punjab notification (✅ Wikipedia).
- Effect: 50% of reserved SC seats to Valmiki and Mazhabi Sikh (✅ Wikipedia).
- Relevance: an early state-level attempt at internal apportionment, later litigated (✅ Wikipedia context).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1975; 50% share (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2015, SJ-2017
**Trap:** Thinking Punjab's notification is the same as the 2024 judgment. It is the background case, not the holding.
**Mains hook:** Shows that within-SC apportionment is an older administrative problem, now given constitutional room.
**Back:** *"Seventy-five split the seats; twenty-four legitimised the split."*

### Flashcard SJ-2021 — Trap: *Davinder Singh* mandates a sub-quota or a creamy-layer rule
**Front:** Does *Davinder Singh* itself allot a sub-quota or create a creamy-layer rule for SCs?
**Definition (learn):** No. It permits data-supported sub-classification; implementation must be by lawful State action on evidence.
**Breakdown:**
- Permission, not mandate (✅ owner 07 traps; ✅ Wikipedia).
- It does not alter the Presidential List (✅ owner 07; ✅ Wikipedia majority position).
- Creamy-layer remarks are observations (SJ-2019).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1 Aug 2024 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2015, SJ-2017, SJ-2019
**Trap:** The phrase "permits" vs "mandates" is the trap pair.
**Mains hook:** Any welfare use must be evidence-based and lawfully designed (✅ owner 07).
**Back:** *"Permits are not mandates; data must do the dividing."*

### Flashcard SJ-2022 — Comparison: sub-classification vs creamy layer vs reservation
**Front:** How do sub-classification, creamy layer and reservation differ?
**Definition (learn):** Reservation allocates places to a group; sub-classification divides a reserved group into internal sub-groups; creamy layer excludes advanced members from the benefit.
**Breakdown:**
- Reservation: group-level entitlement under Articles 15(4), 16(4), 15(6) or 16(6) depending on group.
- Sub-classification: internal apportionment within SCs, permitted by *Davinder Singh* (SJ-2015).
- Creamy layer: exclusion rule; applies to OBCs by Indra Sawhney (SJ-2056); not legislated for SCs/STs (SJ-2019).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Sub-classification: 2024; creamy layer for OBCs: 1992 (✅ owner 07 and 09).
**Links:** SJ-2015, SJ-2019, SJ-2056
**Trap:** Calling sub-classification a creamy-layer rule. They are different devices.
**Mains hook:** A targeted SC policy must choose the device and justify it with data.
**Back:** *"Reserve the group, divide the group, exclude the advanced."*

### Flashcard SJ-2023 — NCRB atrocity data and the denominator trap
**Front:** Why must NCRB atrocity counts be read with a denominator and a caution about reporting?
**Definition (learn):** NCRB's Crime in India data counts registered PoA cases. Rising registration may reflect rising victimisation, better reporting or both.
**Breakdown:**
- NCRB publishes registered atrocity cases annually (✅ owner 07 advanced).
- Registration is not conviction; pendency, investigation and witness vulnerability matter (✅ owner 07).
- Under-reporting and fear of retaliation depress registration (✅ owner 07 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** No specific year count given here; cite with year and denominator (✅ owner 07).
**Links:** SJ-2002, SJ-2004, SJ-2006
**Trap:** Reading a rise in FIRs as a rise in prevalence, or a low conviction rate as proof of innocence.
**Mains hook:** Use registration, pendency and conviction together; the data are an incomplete sign of enforcement. ⚠️ Analytical.
**Back:** *"Filed is not proven, proven is not prevalence."*

### Flashcard SJ-2024 — SC scale: Census 2011 share of population
**Front:** What share of India's population did Scheduled Castes form in Census 2011?
**Definition (learn):** Scheduled Castes formed 16.6% of India's population in Census 2011.
**Breakdown:**
- Census 2011 share: 16.6% (✅ owner 07).
- Use: scale anchor for SC policy; not a count of atrocity victims (✅ owner 07).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 16.6%, Census 2011 (✅ owner 07).
**Links:** SJ-2001, SJ-2023
**Trap:** Using the 2011 share as a current population figure without noting its census year.
**Mains hook:** Scale anchors need year and source; the 2027 Census may update them (⚠️ owner 09 current anchor).
**Back:** *"Sixteen point six, Census two-zero-one-one."*

### Flashcard SJ-2025 — Advanced: the within-SC targeting trade-off (opportunity versus fragmentation)
**Front:** What are the opportunity and risk sides of within-SC sub-classification?
**Definition (learn):** Sub-classification can direct benefits to the most disadvantaged sub-groups, but it can also fragment SC political unity if states use it for electoral ends.
**Breakdown:**
- Opportunity: addresses within-SC inequality; prevents advanced sub-castes capturing benefits (✅ owner 07 advanced).
- Risk: fragmentation of collective bargaining; political exploitation (⚠️ owner 07 advanced analysis).
- Requirement: backwardness data for each sub-group; otherwise arbitrary and vulnerable (✅ owner 07 advanced).
- Permission, not mandate: states may use it or not (⚠️ owner 07 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024 ruling; owner's analysis (✅ owner 07; ✅ Wikipedia for permission).
**Links:** SJ-2015, SJ-2017, SJ-2021, SJ-2022
**Trap:** Presenting the trade-off as settled. It is analysis, not a verdict.
**Mains hook:** Evidence-based sub-classification, safeguards against misuse, and transparent criteria form a reasoned close. ⚠️ Owner-proposed recommendation.
**Back:** *"Target the most behind, but keep the group whole."*

### GROUP — Scheduled Tribes, PVTGs and Tribal Welfare
Owners: `basic/08_Scheduled-Tribes-PVTGs-and-Tribal-Welfare.md` and `advanced/08_Scheduled-Tribes-PVTGs-and-Tribal-Welfare.md`.

### Flashcard SJ-2026 — Tribal development: the displacement-rehabilitation two-axis frame
**Front:** What two axes does the 2025 GS-I question use to frame tribal development, and what is the expected "yes, but" answer?
**Definition (learn):** The question asks whether tribal development centres on displacement (mining, dams, protected areas, land alienation) and rehabilitation (FRA, PM-JANMAN, Van Dhan, EMRS).
**Breakdown:**
- Displacement drivers: mining, dams and infrastructure, protected-area notification, land alienation (✅ owner 08).
- Rehabilitation instruments: FRA 2006, PM-JANMAN, Van Dhan/TRIFED, EMRS (✅ owner 08).
- Expected opinion: the two-axis frame is largely right but may omit a third dimension: livelihood/market integration or cultural autonomy (✅ owner 08; ⚠️ opinion).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 GS-I, 250 words (✅ owner 08 PYQ application).
**Links:** SJ-2044, SJ-2048, SJ-2030, SJ-2037
**Trap:** Giving only a two-axis description without the third-dimension opinion.
**Mains hook:** Map both axes, then propose the third dimension and close with a "yes, but" verdict.
**Back:** *"Displace, rehabilitate, then add the third axis."*
