### Flashcard SJ-2027 — Forest Rights Act, 2006: enactment and commencement
**Front:** When was the Forest Rights Act passed, assented and brought into force?
**Definition (learn):** The Scheduled Tribes and Other Traditional Forest Dwellers (Recognition of Forest Rights) Act, 2006 recognises forest rights of forest-dwelling communities.
**Breakdown:**
- Passed 18 Dec 2006; assent 29 Dec 2006; Act No. 2 of 2007 (✅ Wikipedia: Forest Rights Act, 2006).
- Commenced 31 Dec 2007; Rules notified 1 Jan 2008 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Nodal ministry: Ministry of Tribal Affairs (✅ Wikipedia; see SJ-2034).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Passed 18 Dec 2006; in force 31 Dec 2007 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2028, SJ-2030, SJ-2034
**Trap:** Confusing the passage date with the commencement date. The Act was in force only from 31 Dec 2007.
**Mains hook:** A statute enacted in 2006 but commenced in 2007 shows the lag between law and recognition. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Passed oh-six, in force oh-seven."*

### Flashcard SJ-2028 — FRA individual forest rights: the 4-hectare limit
**Front:** What are individual forest rights under the FRA, and what limit applies?
**Definition (learn):** Rights to hold and live in forest land for habitation or self-cultivation, limited to the land actually under occupation and cultivation on 13 December 2005.
**Breakdown:**
- Maximum: 4 hectares (✅ Wikipedia; owner 08 says ceiling per nuclear family ✅ owner 08).
- Reference date: land cultivated as of 13 Dec 2005; no new lands granted (✅ Wikipedia).
- Title is heritable but not transferable or alienable (✅ owner 08).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 4 ha; 13 Dec 2005 cut-off (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2027, SJ-2029, SJ-2031, SJ-2046
**Trap:** "FRA grants only individual titles" is wrong; the Act also recognises community and community forest resource rights (SJ-2046).
**Mains hook:** Tenure security without income support is incomplete (SJ-2044). ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Four hectares, cut-off thirteen December oh-five."*

### Flashcard SJ-2029 — FRA community rights and the category-count conflict
**Front:** What community rights does the FRA recognise, and how many rights categories are there?
**Definition (learn):** Community rights include ownership of minor forest produce (MFP), community grazing, access to biodiversity and traditional fishing or water rights.
**Breakdown:**
- MFP ownership, grazing, access to biodiversity, traditional and customary rights (✅ owner 08).
- Owner's scheme: three categories — individual, community, community forest resource (✅ owner 08).
- Fetched page's grouping: four groups — title, use, relief and development, forest management (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner's three categories; Wikipedia's four groupings.
**Links:** SJ-2028, SJ-2030, SJ-2046
**Trap:** ⚠️ Conflict: a "how many categories" question may use either scheme. Use the owner's three-category frame for the standard answer and cite the statute section for any precise count.
**Mains hook:** MFP ownership gives tribal households a cash base only if the Gram Sabha controls the market (linked to PESA, SJ-2035).
**Back:** *"Leaves, grazing, and a shared right to the wild."*

### Flashcard SJ-2030 — Community forest resource rights (the Gram Sabha's most empowering right)
**Front:** What are community forest resource rights under the FRA?
**Definition (learn):** The right of a Gram Sabha to protect, regenerate, conserve and manage community forest resources that were traditionally protected.
**Breakdown:**
- Owner calls this the most empowering but least operationalised category (✅ owner 08).
- Forest management rights fall under Sections 3(1) and 5 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Gram Sabha as the initial authority for recognition (SJ-2031).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Sections 3(1) and 5 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2029, SJ-2031, SJ-2044
**Trap:** Treating community forest resource rights as a general state forest-management power. They belong to the Gram Sabha.
**Mains hook:** The under-used right is the test of genuine decentralisation in forest governance. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Gram Sabha guards, regenerates, and manages."*

### Flashcard SJ-2031 — FRA recognition process: Gram Sabha, SDLC and DLC
**Front:** Who recognises and adjudicates forest-rights claims, step by step?
**Definition (learn):** The Gram Sabha recommends, a Sub-Divisional Level Committee screens and approves, and a District Level Committee decides at district level.
**Breakdown:**
- Gram Sabha: initial resolution recommending claims (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 08).
- SDLC (taluka level): screening and approval (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 08 names SDLC/DLC).
- DLC: district-level approval and appeals (✅ Wikipedia).
- Composition: three government officials (Forest, Revenue, Tribal Welfare) and three elected members of the local body (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Committees: SDLC then DLC (✅ owner 08; ✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2030, SJ-2033, SJ-2034
**Trap:** The DLC is the final adjudicating body, not the Gram Sabha (✅ owner 08).
**Mains hook:** Rejection rates and evidence burdens show where recognition fails (SJ-2033).
**Back:** *"Sabha recommends, sub-division screens, district decides."*

### Flashcard SJ-2032 — FRA Section 4(2): relocation from critical wildlife habitat
**Front:** Under what conditions can tribals be relocated from a critical wildlife habitat under FRA Section 4(2)?
**Definition (learn):** Relocation is possible only after cumulative safeguards: scientific necessity with no alternative, public consultation, community consent, and compensation with a secure livelihood.
**Breakdown:**
- Scientific necessity, no alternative available, via public consultation (✅ Wikipedia).
- Community consent (✅ Wikipedia; owner's "free informed consent" ✅ owner 08 advanced).
- Compensation and secure livelihood/resettlement package (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 08 advanced).
- Owner adds completed rights recognition and coexistence assessment (✅ owner 08 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Section 4(2) (✅ owner 08; ✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2028, SJ-2043, SJ-2044
**Trap:** Section 4(2) is not a general relocation power for wildlife protection. It applies only under strict conditions.
**Mains hook:** Conservation and tribal rights meet here; the test is consent plus livelihood, not notification alone. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Need, no alternative, consent, then a livelihood."*

### Flashcard SJ-2033 — FRA rejection is not eviction (the 2019 stay)
**Front:** Does rejection of an FRA claim automatically permit eviction?
**Definition (learn):** No. Rejection ends the recognition process but does not erase due-process duties. The 2019 Supreme Court eviction direction for rejected claims was stayed.
**Breakdown:**
- FRA provides a process; rejection does not justify automatic eviction (✅ owner 08 advanced).
- 2019 eviction direction stayed (✅ owner 08 advanced; stay date ⚠️ not fetched).
- Rejection rates are high in some States (⚠️ owner 08 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2019 interim episode (✅ owner 08).
**Links:** SJ-2031, SJ-2032
**Trap:** Treating the 2019 direction as a standing eviction rule. It was stayed.
**Mains hook:** High rejection rates indicate procedural and evidence barriers, not a settled eviction policy. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Rejected is not removed."*

### Flashcard SJ-2034 — FRA nodal ministry and the 2021 Prelims demand
**Front:** Which ministry is the nodal ministry for the FRA, and how was it tested?
**Definition (learn):** The Ministry of Tribal Affairs frames the Rules and publishes monthly FRA implementation reports.
**Breakdown:**
- Nodal ministry: Ministry of Tribal Affairs (✅ Wikipedia; page does not use "nodal").
- 2021 Prelims GS-I Q84 asked for the nodal ministry (✅ owner 08 PYQ integration, routed demand; official key not held locally).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2021 Prelims GS-I, Q84 (✅ owner 08 ledger).
**Links:** SJ-2027, SJ-2031
**Trap:** Confusing MoTA with MoSJE (Social Justice). FRA sits with MoTA.
**Mains hook:** Nodal ministry knowledge supports answers on FRA delivery; the ministry alone does not guarantee implementation. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Tribal affairs, not social justice, holds the forest right."*

### Flashcard SJ-2035 — PESA, 1996: the welfare and livelihood angle
**Front:** What welfare protections does the PESA give Gram Sabhas in Scheduled Areas?
**Definition (learn):** The Panchayats (Extension to Scheduled Areas) Act, 1996 gives Gram Sabhas consultation, minor forest produce, money-lending and market powers in Scheduled Areas.
**Breakdown:**
- Mandatory consultation before land acquisition and resettlement (✅ owner 08).
- Ownership of minor forest produce (✅ owner 08).
- Control over money-lending regulation (✅ owner 08).
- Control over village markets (✅ owner 08).
- Institutional structure is Polity's domain (✅ owner 08).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PESA 1996 (✅ owner 08 basic).
**Links:** SJ-2029, SJ-2046, SJ-2047
**Trap:** Applying PESA to all rural areas. It is limited to Scheduled Areas under the Fifth Schedule (SJ-2047).
**Mains hook:** Gram Sabha power makes welfare meaningful only if consent and money-lending controls are enforced. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Consult, own the produce, control the lender and market."*

### Flashcard SJ-2036 — PVTG: definition, criteria and the 2019 Prelims demand
**Front:** What criteria identify a Particularly Vulnerable Tribal Group, and how many PVTGs are identified?
**Definition (learn):** A sub-category of STs with four identifying criteria: pre-agricultural technology, low literacy, declining or stagnant population, and economic backwardness.
**Breakdown:**
- Criteria: pre-agricultural technology level; low literacy; declining or stagnant population; economic backwardness (✅ owner 08).
- Identified PVTGs: 75 (✅ owner 08; ⚠️ count not fetched, see SJ-2037).
- 2019 Prelims GS-I Q51 asked about PVTG criteria (✅ owner 08 ledger; official key not held locally).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 75 PVTG communities (✅ owner 08).
**Links:** SJ-2037, SJ-2046
**Trap:** Not all STs are PVTGs; PVTG is a narrower vulnerability category.
**Mains hook:** PVTG targeting is a justice tool; it works only if habitat, services and rights converge. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Tech, literacy, numbers, poverty: the four-part PVTG test."*

### Flashcard SJ-2037 — PM-JANMAN: PVTG-focused convergence mission
**Front:** What is PM-JANMAN, when was it launched and whom does it target?
**Definition (learn):** Pradhan Mantri Janjati Adivasi Nyaya Maha Abhiyan, a PVTG-focused convergence mission run by the Ministry of Tribal Affairs.
**Breakdown:**
- Launch: 15 November 2023 (✅ owner 08; fetch failed, ⚠️).
- Target: 75 PVTG communities across 18 States/UTs (✅ owner 08; ⚠️ not fetched).
- Components: housing via PMAY-G convergence, roads, electricity and water, mobile medical units and Anganwadi outreach, EMRS and hostels for education (✅ owner 08).
- Owner's status warning: do not convert dashboard counts into proof of completed service (✅ owner 08 current anchor).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 15 Nov 2023; 75 PVTGs; 18 States/UTs (✅ owner 08; ⚠️ not independently fetched).
**Links:** SJ-2036, SJ-2040, SJ-2048
**Trap:** Treating PM-JANMAN as a universal ST scheme. It is PVTG-specific (SJ-2046).
**Mains hook:** Convergence works only if component reporting is disaggregated. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Seventy-five groups, eighteen states, one convergence drive."* (⚠️ count memory-based until verified.)

### Flashcard SJ-2038 — Van Dhan Yojana and Van Dhan Vikas Kendras
**Front:** What is the Van Dhan Yojana and what are its village units?
**Definition (learn):** A TRIFED-led scheme that sets up Van Dhan Vikas Kendras (VDVKs) at village level for minor forest produce value addition, aggregation and marketing.
**Breakdown:**
- Units: VDVKs at village level (✅ owner 08).
- Functions: MFP aggregation, processing, packaging and market linkage (✅ owner 08).
- Coverage is not universal; benefits accrue unevenly (✅ owner 08 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Launch year not in owner; ⚠️ not fetched.
**Links:** SJ-2039, SJ-2044
**Trap:** VDVKs are not universal and do not replace land title.
**Mains hook:** Livelihood integration (the third axis) needs VDVK-style market linkage. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Village kendra turns the forest into a market."*

### Flashcard SJ-2039 — TRIFED and MSP for minor forest produce
**Front:** What role does TRIFED play in tribal produce marketing?
**Definition (learn):** TRIFED is the national agency for tribal produce marketing; it implements Van Dhan and the MSP-for-MFP support.
**Breakdown:**
- National-level tribal marketing agency (✅ owner 08 advanced).
- Implements Van Dhan Yojana (✅ owner 08).
- MSP-for-MFP price support and marketing support (✅ owner 08).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-only; no year given.
**Links:** SJ-2038, SJ-2044
**Trap:** MSP-for-MFP gives price support; it is not an income guarantee for all tribal households.
**Mains hook:** Price support helps only where market access and storage exist. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"TRIFED sells, the MSP steadies the price."*

### Flashcard SJ-2040 — Eklavya Model Residential Schools (EMRS)
**Front:** What are EMRS and what are their qualifying norms?
**Definition (learn):** Tribal residential schools run under the Ministry of Tribal Affairs, providing quality education from Class VI to XII.
**Breakdown:**
- One school per qualifying block (✅ owner 08).
- Qualifying norm: at least 50% ST population and at least 20,000 ST persons, or as per MoTA norms (⚠️ owner 08 basic; not fetched).
- Schools described as CBSE-affiliated (✅ owner 08).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Classes VI to XII; 50% ST and 20,000 ST norm (⚠️ owner-only norm).
**Links:** SJ-2037, SJ-2041
**Trap:** EMRS is a residential school route, not a scholarship scheme.
**Mains hook:** Education bridges the tribal gap only if completion and post-school outcomes are tracked. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Six to twelve, residential, one per block."*

### Flashcard SJ-2041 — National Commission for Scheduled Tribes (NCST), Article 338A
**Front:** What is NCST's constitutional basis and what does it do?
**Definition (learn):** A constitutional body under Article 338A that investigates ST-rights violations and advises on safeguards.
**Breakdown:**
- Constitutional status under Article 338A (✅ owner 08).
- Functions: investigate and advise (✅ owner 08).
- Composition and mandate: cross-linked to Polity (✅ owner 08).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Article 338A (✅ owner 08).
**Links:** SJ-2013, SJ-2036
**Trap:** NCST is constitutional, like NCSC (Art 338), while NCM is statutory (Minorities owner).
**Mains hook:** Constitutional status helps; implementation still depends on State action. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Three-thirty-eight-A watches the tribes."*

### Flashcard SJ-2042 — Land alienation and State land-transfer laws in Fifth Schedule areas
**Front:** How is transfer of tribal land to non-tribals regulated, and by which State laws?
**Definition (learn):** Land alienation of tribal land to non-tribals is restricted by State tenancy and land-transfer laws in Fifth Schedule areas.
**Breakdown:**
- Examples: Chotanagpur Tenancy Act (Jharkhand); AP Scheduled Areas Land Transfer Regulation (✅ owner 08 advanced).
- Enforcement is the weak link (✅ owner 08 advanced).
- Informal and illegal transfers continue despite prohibition (✅ owner 08 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-only examples; enactment years not stated.
**Links:** SJ-2026, SJ-2035, SJ-2043
**Trap:** A legal prohibition is not the same as protection on the ground. Enforcement determines outcome.
**Mains hook:** Land alienation is a displacement driver as well as a rights issue (SJ-2026). ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Law forbids, enforcement decides."*

### Flashcard SJ-2043 — Samatha and Orissa Mining (Niyamgiri): case anchors
**Front:** Which two cases anchor the Scheduled Area and Gram Sabha answers in tribal welfare?
**Definition (learn):** *Samatha* anchors transfer and mining restrictions in Scheduled Areas; *Orissa Mining Corporation (Niyamgiri)* shows Gram Sabha determination of community and religious forest rights.
**Breakdown:**
- *Samatha*: transfer and mining in Scheduled Areas (✅ owner 08 basic).
- *Orissa Mining Corporation / Niyamgiri*: Gram Sabha determination of rights (✅ owner 08 basic).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Case years not stated in owner; ⚠️ not fetched.
**Links:** SJ-2030, SJ-2042, SJ-2044
**Trap:** Citing *Niyamgiri* as a general FRA holding on all forest rights. It shows a Gram Sabha determination in its facts.
**Mains hook:** Courts have used Gram Sabha consent to shape mining decisions. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Samatha on transfer, Niyamgiri on the Sabha's voice."*

### Flashcard SJ-2044 — LARR 2013 and displacement compensation
**Front:** What displacement architecture does the Land Acquisition, Rehabilitation and Resettlement Act, 2013 provide?
**Definition (learn):** LARR provides impact assessment, compensation and rehabilitation for land acquisition, but cash cannot replace commons, cultural landscapes or autonomy (owner's point).
**Breakdown:**
- Impact, compensation and rehabilitation architecture (✅ owner 08 advanced; Act year ✅ owner 08 advanced).
- Limitation: cash cannot replace commons or identity (✅ owner 08 advanced).
- Cross-linked to Section 4(2) of FRA for protected areas (SJ-2032).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** LARR, 2013 (✅ owner 08 advanced).
**Links:** SJ-2032, SJ-2042, SJ-2045
**Trap:** LARR is not the FRA. Land acquisition compensation and forest-rights recognition are separate routes.
**Mains hook:** Displacement compensation without commons and autonomy repeats old losses. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Cash compensates land, not culture."*

### Flashcard SJ-2045 — Advanced: the third axis (livelihood integration or cultural autonomy)
**Front:** What third dimension should a strong answer add to the displacement-rehabilitation frame?
**Definition (learn):** Livelihood and market integration, or cultural autonomy, depending on context. Either addresses what land title alone does not.
**Breakdown:**
- Livelihood/market integration: title does not ensure income (✅ owner 08 advanced analysis).
- Cultural autonomy: material rehabilitation does not restore language or governance (✅ owner 08 advanced).
- Recommended: add the third axis explicitly to improve policy coherence (⚠️ owner 08 advanced recommended position).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner's analytical extension of the 2025 PYQ (⚠️ inference).
**Links:** SJ-2026, SJ-2038, SJ-2048
**Trap:** Presenting the third axis as a verified official dimension. It is the owner's analytical proposal.
**Mains hook:** The "yes, but" close: two axes are a start; add a third axis for coherence. ⚠️ Analytical.
**Back:** *"Livelihood or identity: the axis title cannot buy."*

### Flashcard SJ-2046 — Trap pair: All STs are PVTGs; FRA grants only individual titles
**Front:** Are all Scheduled Tribes PVTGs, and does the FRA grant only individual titles?
**Definition (learn):** No to both. PVTGs are a vulnerable sub-category; the FRA recognises individual, community and community forest resource rights.
**Breakdown:**
- PVTG is a sub-category of STs with four criteria (SJ-2036).
- FRA: individual, community and community forest resource rights (✅ owner 08).
- PM-JANMAN targets PVTGs, not all STs (SJ-2037).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 75 PVTGs (✅ owner 08).
**Links:** SJ-2028, SJ-2029, SJ-2030, SJ-2036
**Trap:** The two distractors are "all STs are PVTGs" and "only individual titles".
**Mains hook:** Keep the categories distinct when targeting tribes.
**Back:** *"Not every tribe is vulnerable; not every right is a title."*

### Flashcard SJ-2047 — Trap: PESA applies to all rural areas
**Front:** Does PESA apply to all rural areas?
**Definition (learn):** No. PESA applies only to Scheduled Areas under the Fifth Schedule.
**Breakdown:**
- Territory: Scheduled Areas under the Fifth Schedule (✅ owner 08 traps).
- Constitutional and institutional structure is cross-linked to Polity (✅ owner 08).
- Welfare functions (MFP, consultation, money-lending) apply within those areas (SJ-2035).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PESA 1996 (✅ owner 08).
**Links:** SJ-2035, SJ-2046
**Trap:** "All rural areas" is the distractor; the answer is "Scheduled Areas only".
**Mains hook:** Territorial scope matters for any PESA-based answer.
**Back:** *"PESA: Scheduled Areas, not every village."*

### Flashcard SJ-2048 — 2025 GS-I PYQ: the answer route
**Front:** How should a 250-word answer on the 2025 tribal-development question be structured?
**Definition (learn):** Map displacement drivers, map rehabilitation instruments, give a reasoned opinion that adds a third dimension, then recommend.
**Breakdown:**
- (a) Displacement: mining, dams, protected areas, land alienation (✅ owner 08).
- (b) Rehabilitation: FRA, PM-JANMAN, Van Dhan, EMRS (✅ owner 08).
- (c) Opinion: largely accurate, incomplete (✅ owner 08).
- (d) Recommend: holistic model integrating economic opportunity and identity protection (✅ owner 08).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 GS-I; 250 words (✅ owner 08).
**Links:** SJ-2026, SJ-2045, SJ-2037
**Trap:** Stopping at description without the opinion step.
**Mains hook:** Structured "yes, but" answers score on the third-dimension argument.
**Back:** *"Displace, rehabilitate, opine, recommend."*

### Flashcard SJ-2049 — Xaxa Committee and the diagnosis of tribal alienation
**Front:** What diagnosis does the owner attribute to the Xaxa Committee?
**Definition (learn):** The Xaxa Committee diagnosed alienation, displacement and weak tribal self-government as core tribal problems.
**Breakdown:**
- Diagnosis: alienation, displacement and weak self-government (✅ owner 08 basic).
- Use: a named evidence source for tribal policy answers (✅ owner 08).
- Year not stated in owner; ⚠️ not fetched.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Committee year ⚠️ unverified.
**Links:** SJ-2042, SJ-2035, SJ-2044
**Trap:** Quoting Xaxa for a statistic without checking the report year and scope.
**Mains hook:** Named committee evidence sharpens answers on alienation and self-government. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Alienation, displacement, weak self-rule: the Xaxa trio."*

### GROUP — OBC, EWS and Social Mobility
Owners: `basic/09_OBC-EWS-and-Social-Mobility.md` and `advanced/09_OBC-EWS-and-Social-Mobility.md`.

### Flashcard SJ-2050 — Backward-class architecture: OBC, EWS and the mobility test
**Front:** How do OBC reservation, EWS reservation and creamy-layer filtering fit together?
**Definition (learn):** OBC reservation addresses social and educational backwardness; EWS reservation addresses economic disadvantage among non-SC/ST/OBC citizens; the creamy layer keeps the advanced out of the OBC benefit; success is measured by intergenerational mobility.
**Breakdown:**
- Identification: Central List (Art 342A) and State lists (105th Amendment) (✅ owner 09).
- Benefit: OBC 27% in central services and institutions; EWS up to 10% (✅ owner 09).
- Filter: creamy layer for OBCs only (✅ owner 09).
- Outcome test: intergenerational mobility, not coverage (✅ owner 09).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 27% OBC; 10% EWS (✅ owner 09).
**Links:** SJ-2051, SJ-2055, SJ-2058, SJ-2067
**Trap:** Treating OBC and EWS as one category with a different label.
**Mains hook:** Reservation is a route, not the end; judge it by second-generation progress. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Backwardness opens, creamy filters, mobility proves."*

### Flashcard SJ-2051 — OBC: definition and constitutional basis (Articles 15(4) and 16(4))
**Front:** How are OBCs defined and what constitutional provisions entitle them to reservation?
**Definition (learn):** Socially and educationally backward classes other than SCs and STs, identified on social and educational backwardness rather than income alone.
**Breakdown:**
- Constitutional basis: Articles 15(4) and 16(4) (✅ owner 09).
- Identification criterion: social and educational backwardness (✅ owner 09).
- Not synonymous with poverty (✅ owner 09 advanced distinction).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Articles 15(4), 16(4) (✅ owner 09).
**Links:** SJ-2052, SJ-2053, SJ-2061
**Trap:** "OBC means poor." Economic criteria belong to EWS, not to the OBC definition.
**Mains hook:** Distinguish social-educational backwardness (OBC) from economic backwardness (EWS). ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Social and educational, not merely poor."*

### Flashcard SJ-2052 — Article 342A and the Central OBC List
**Front:** Who specifies and who varies the Central OBC List under Article 342A?
**Definition (learn):** The President specifies the Central List; Parliament alone may include or exclude a community. NCBC gives advice in the process.
**Breakdown:**
- President specifies the list (✅ owner 09).
- Parliament varies it (✅ owner 09).
- NCBC advises; it does not add communities itself (✅ owner 09; see SJ-2068).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Article 342A (✅ owner 09).
**Links:** SJ-2051, SJ-2053, SJ-2068
**Trap:** Thinking the NCBC or the President can add communities alone. Parliament varies the list.
**Mains hook:** Central list competence is a constitutional design choice, not a commission power. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"President names, Parliament changes, commission advises."*

### Flashcard SJ-2053 — 105th Amendment (2021) and State OBC lists
**Front:** What did the 105th Constitutional Amendment Act, 2021 restore to States?
**Definition (learn):** The power for States to prepare their own OBC/SEBC lists for State-level reservation.
**Breakdown:**
- Owner: restored state power after the Supreme Court's 2021 Maratha reservation ruling read the 102nd Amendment as Parliament-only (✅ owner 09; the Maratha ruling is ⚠️ not fetched).
- The President's list binds only central purposes (✅ owner 09 advanced).
- Owner-level statement; no fetch of the Act text (⚠️).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 105th Amendment, 2021 (✅ owner 09).
**Links:** SJ-2052, SJ-2069
**Trap:** The 102nd Amendment did not itself give states the power (SJ-2069).
**Mains hook:** Federal list-making needs clear division between central and State purposes. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"One-oh-five returned the State pen."*

### Flashcard SJ-2054 — NCBC: constitutional status (Article 338B) and statutory origin
**Front:** When did NCBC become constitutional, and what was it before?
**Definition (learn):** The National Commission for Backward Classes was a statutory body under the NCBC Act, 1993 and became constitutional through the 102nd Amendment under Article 338B.
**Breakdown:**
- Statutory origin: NCBC Act, 1993; formed 14 Aug 1993 (✅ Wikipedia: National Commission for Backward Classes).
- Constitutional status: 102nd Amendment, Article 338B; date 11 Aug 2018 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Composition under Article 338B(2): Chairperson, Vice-Chairperson and three other members (✅ Wikipedia).
- NCBC considers inclusion and exclusion requests and advises the Centre (✅ Wikipedia; owner 09).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1993 statutory; 11 Aug 2018 constitutional (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2052, SJ-2068, SJ-2013
**Trap:** Saying NCBC is statutory now. It is constitutional under Article 338B.
**Mains hook:** Constitutional status strengthens NCBC but not its power to amend lists. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Ninety-three statute, two-thousand-eighteen constitution."*

### Flashcard SJ-2055 — Creamy layer in OBC reservation: the ₹8 lakh test
**Front:** What is the OBC creamy-layer rule, and what is its income ceiling?
**Definition (learn):** An exclusion rule that removes advanced OBC families from reservation benefit, based on parental status and, where applicable, an income and wealth test.
**Breakdown:**
- Ceiling: ₹8 lakh, last revised September 2017 (✅ owner 09).
- Test uses parental service or status categories, plus income and wealth test with exclusions (✅ owner 09).
- Applies over three consecutive years (✅ owner 09 advanced).
- Salary and agricultural income are not simply clubbed (✅ owner 09).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ₹8 lakh; Sept 2017 (✅ owner 09).
**Links:** SJ-2056, SJ-2057, SJ-2062
**Trap:** Reducing the test to "family income above ₹8 lakh". The status test matters too.
**Mains hook:** Creamy-layer protects intra-group equity; its threshold lags inflation (⚠️ owner 09 limitation).
**Back:** *"Eight lakh, parents' status, three years, not salary alone."*

### Flashcard SJ-2056 — Trap: creamy layer applies to SC/ST reservation
**Front:** Does the creamy-layer exclusion apply to SC or ST reservation?
**Definition (learn):** No. Creamy-layer exclusion applies to OBC reservation; SC/ST reservation has no creamy-layer exclusion under owner-stated doctrine.
**Breakdown:**
- Creamy layer: OBC only (✅ owner 09; Indra Sawhney, ✅ owner 09).
- SC/ST: no creamy-layer exclusion under the owner's doctrine (✅ owner 09).
- Davinder Singh creamy-layer remarks are observations, not an enacted rule (SJ-2019).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner doctrine (✅ owner 09).
**Links:** SJ-2055, SJ-2019, SJ-2057
**Trap:** The distractor is "creamy layer applies to all reservations".
**Mains hook:** Doctrine differs by category; a single rule does not travel across all reservations.
**Back:** *"Creamy filter for OBCs, not for SCs or STs."*

### Flashcard SJ-2057 — *Indra Sawhney v. Union of India* (1992): OBC reservation and creamy layer
**Front:** What did *Indra Sawhney* (1992) establish for OBC reservation?
**Definition (learn):** The Supreme Court upheld OBC reservation within the ordinary limits and required creamy-layer exclusion for OBCs.
**Breakdown:**
- OBC reservation upheld up to 27% (✅ owner 09 advanced).
- Creamy-layer exclusion mandated for OBCs (✅ owner 09 advanced).
- Ordinary 50% ceiling reasoning belongs to Polity (✅ owner 09 cross-link).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1992 (✅ owner 09).
**Links:** SJ-2055, SJ-2056, SJ-2058
**Trap:** Treating Indra Sawhney as an EWS case. It concerns OBCs.
**Mains hook:** Creamy layer is the judicial tool for intra-OBC equity. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Ninety-two gave OBCs their filter."*

### Flashcard SJ-2058 — 103rd Amendment (2019): Articles 15(6) and 16(6) for EWS
**Front:** Which articles and which amendment created the EWS reservation?
**Definition (learn):** The 103rd Constitutional Amendment Act, 2019 inserted Articles 15(6) and 16(6), permitting up to 10% reservation for economically weaker non-SC/ST/OBC citizens.
**Breakdown:**
- Articles 15(6) (education) and 16(6) (public employment) (✅ Wikipedia: Janhit Abhiyan).
- Cap: up to 10% (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 09).
- Scope: economically weaker citizens not SC/ST/OBC (✅ owner 09).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 103rd Amendment, 2019; 10% (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2059, SJ-2060, SJ-2061
**Trap:** EWS is not for economically poor OBCs; they keep the OBC route.
**Mains hook:** Economic criterion for a new category is a second axis of affirmative action. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Fifteen-six and sixteen-six, ten percent for the non-reserved poor."*

### Flashcard SJ-2059 — EWS criteria: ₹8 lakh income and the asset limits
**Front:** What income and asset limits define EWS eligibility?
**Definition (learn):** Gross annual family income below ₹8 lakh, plus asset limits, under a DoPT notification dated 31 January 2019.
**Breakdown:**
- Income: gross annual family income below ₹8 lakh (✅ owner 09; ✅ owner 09 advanced).
- Asset limits: no 5 acres or more of agricultural land; no residential flat of 1,000 sq ft or more; no residential plot of 100 sq yards or more in a notified municipality, or 200 sq yards or more elsewhere (✅ owner 09 advanced).
- Notification date: 31 Jan 2019 (✅ owner 09 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ₹8 lakh; 5 acres; 1,000 sq ft; 100/200 sq yd (✅ owner 09; ⚠️ not fetched).
**Links:** SJ-2055, SJ-2058, SJ-2061
**Trap:** The EWS ₹8 lakh is numerically the same as the OBC creamy-layer ceiling but legally different (SJ-2062).
**Mains hook:** Income-based identification is harder to verify than caste-based identification. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Eight lakh income, asset walls, notified in January nineteen."*

### Flashcard SJ-2060 — *Janhit Abhiyan v. Union of India* (7 November 2022): EWS upheld 3:2
**Front:** What did the Supreme Court decide in *Janhit Abhiyan* on EWS reservation?
**Definition (learn):** The Court upheld the 103rd Amendment's EWS reservation by a 3:2 majority.
**Breakdown:**
- Judgment date: 7 November 2022 (✅ Wikipedia: Janhit Abhiyan v. Union of India).
- Majority: Justices Dinesh Maheshwari, Bela M. Trivedi and J.B. Pardiwala (✅ Wikipedia).
- Minority: CJI Uday Umesh Lalit and Justice Shripathi Ravindra Bhat (✅ Wikipedia).
- Upheld: 103rd Amendment; Articles 15(6), 16(6) (✅ Wikipedia).
- Dissent: EWS excludes SC/ST/OBC, and economic-only criteria undercut representation (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 7 Nov 2022; 3:2 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2058, SJ-2071
**Trap:** The majority said the amendment did not violate the basic structure; the minority did. Do not reverse.
**Mains hook:** Constitutional validity of economic criteria in reservation remains contested in principle. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Twenty-two, three to two, EWS stands."*

### Flashcard SJ-2061 — Comparison: OBC versus EWS
**Front:** How do OBC and EWS reservation differ on basis, article, list, creamy layer and percentage?
**Definition (learn):** OBC is social-educational and list-based; EWS is economic and income-asset-based for non-reserved citizens.
**Breakdown:**
- Basis: OBC social and educational; EWS economic (✅ owner 09).
- Article: OBC 15(4)/16(4); EWS 15(6)/16(6) (✅ owner 09; ✅ Wikipedia).
- Identification: OBC list (Art 342A and State lists); EWS certificate by income and assets (✅ owner 09).
- Creamy layer: OBC yes; EWS uses income and asset limits instead (✅ owner 09).
- Percentage: OBC 27%; EWS up to 10% (✅ owner 09).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 27% OBC; 10% EWS (✅ owner 09).
**Links:** SJ-2051, SJ-2058, SJ-2059
**Trap:** Thinking EWS is an OBC sub-category. It excludes OBCs.
**Mains hook:** The two categories cover different disadvantages; they must be judged separately. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Social list for OBC, income walls for EWS."*

### Flashcard SJ-2062 — Trap: OBC creamy-layer test and EWS test are different
**Front:** Are the OBC creamy-layer ceiling and the EWS income test the same?
**Definition (learn):** No. Both use ₹8 lakh, but the creamy-layer test uses parental status and exclusions, while the EWS test adds asset limits.
**Breakdown:**
- OBC creamy layer: ₹8 lakh, parental status and income exclusions (✅ owner 09).
- EWS: ₹8 lakh plus asset limits (✅ owner 09 advanced).
- The numbers match; the legal tests do not (✅ owner 09).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Both ₹8 lakh; OBC Sept 2017; EWS Jan 2019 (✅ owner 09).
**Links:** SJ-2055, SJ-2059, SJ-2061
**Trap:** The same number invites a false equivalence.
**Mains hook:** Name the test and its legal source before any comparison. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Same eight lakh, different tests."*

### Flashcard SJ-2063 — PM-YASASVI: scholarship umbrella for OBC, EBC and DNT students
**Front:** What is PM-YASASVI and which students does it cover?
**Definition (learn):** An umbrella MoSJE scheme of education support for OBC, EBC and DNT students, with pre-matric, post-matric and top-class routes.
**Breakdown:**
- Target: OBC, EBC and DNT students (✅ owner 09).
- Routes: pre-matric, post-matric, top-class (✅ owner 09 basic).
- Components vary by annual guideline (✅ owner 09).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Launch year not stated; ⚠️ not fetched.
**Links:** SJ-2050, SJ-2064, SJ-2109
**Trap:** EBC is not the constitutional EWS category.
**Mains hook:** Scholarship routes need annual guideline checks before claiming availability. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"OBC, EBC, DNT: scholars on the guideline."*

### Flashcard SJ-2064 — Rohini Commission (2017): sub-categorisation of OBCs
**Front:** What was the Rohini Commission set up to examine?
**Definition (learn):** The 2017 commission examined how to sub-categorise the 27% OBC quota so that benefits reach under-represented OBC sub-groups.
**Breakdown:**
- Year: 2017 (✅ owner 09 advanced).
- Mandate: sub-categorisation within the 27% (✅ owner 09).
- Implementation: pending as of mid-2026 per owner (⚠️ time-varying, owner-stated).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2017; implementation pending (✅ owner 09 advanced; status ⚠️).
**Links:** SJ-2070, SJ-2071
**Trap:** Saying sub-categorisation is implemented nationally. It is not (SJ-2070).
**Mains hook:** Sub-categorisation needs credible data and safeguards against capture. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Seventeen studied the slices; the quota still awaits the cut."*

### Flashcard SJ-2065 — Census 2027 caste enumeration: decision and status
**Front:** What did the Cabinet Committee on Political Affairs decide on caste enumeration, and what is the status?
**Definition (learn):** The CCPA decided on 30 April 2025 to include caste enumeration in Census 2027; houselisting was notified for 1 April to 30 September 2026.
**Breakdown:**
- Decision: CCPA, 30 Apr 2025 (✅ owner 09 current anchor).
- Census 2027 is all-digital (✅ owner 09).
- Houselisting window: 1 Apr to 30 Sep 2026 (✅ owner 09; as at 21 July 2026).
- No caste results yet; not an automatic basis for list revision (✅ owner 09).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 30 Apr 2025; 1 Apr–30 Sep 2026 (✅ owner 09; ⚠️ time-varying, not fetched).
**Links:** SJ-2066, SJ-2070
**Trap:** Treating the caste decision as published data. Results are not out.
**Mains hook:** The 2027 enumeration may close the data gap, but results and validation must come first. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Decided twenty-five, houselisted twenty-six, results not yet."*

### Flashcard SJ-2066 — Caste-data gap: 1931 and SECC 2011
**Front:** What is the caste-data gap and why does it matter for OBC policy?
**Definition (learn):** India has published no full all-caste census since 1931; SECC 2011 collected caste data that was not released or validated for policy use.
**Breakdown:**
- Last all-caste census tables: 1931 (✅ owner 09 advanced).
- SECC 2011 collected caste data; not released or validated (✅ owner 09 advanced).
- Consequence: reservation proportions and sub-categorisation cannot be calibrated (✅ owner 09 advanced analysis).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1931; SECC 2011 (✅ owner 09).
**Links:** SJ-2065, SJ-2064, SJ-2071
**Trap:** Claiming SECC 2011 provides usable OBC numbers. Owner says it does not.
**Mains hook:** Without population data, reservation calibration is guesswork. ⚠️ Analytical.
**Back:** *"Thirty-one was the last count; SECC kept its data shut."*

### Flashcard SJ-2067 — Intergenerational mobility versus first-generation access
**Front:** How does intergenerational mobility differ from first-generation access under reservation?
**Definition (learn):** First-generation access is entry into education or employment; intergenerational mobility is whether the next generation consolidates and advances.
**Breakdown:**
- First-generation: reserved seat or scholarship (✅ owner 09 advanced).
- Second-generation: child reaches higher attainment and occupation (✅ owner 09).
- Stagnation if children re-access reservation without progression (✅ owner 09 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner's conceptual framework (⚠️ inference).
**Links:** SJ-2050, SJ-2065, SJ-2073
**Trap:** Counting slot fill rates as mobility. They are coverage, not mobility.
**Mains hook:** Use second-generation progression as the outcome test. ⚠️ Analytical.
**Back:** *"Entry is one generation; mobility is the next."*

### Flashcard SJ-2068 — Trap: NCBC can directly add communities to the Central List
**Front:** Can NCBC directly add a community to the Central OBC List?
**Definition (learn):** No. NCBC considers requests and advises; Parliament acts to include or exclude.
**Breakdown:**
- NCBC: advisory and consultative (✅ owner 09; ✅ Wikipedia).
- Parliament varies the Central List under Article 342A (✅ owner 09).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Article 342A; Article 338B (✅ owner 09; ✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2052, SJ-2054
**Trap:** The distractor "NCBC adds communities" is wrong.
**Mains hook:** Commission power is advisory; list power is legislative.
**Back:** *"Commission advises, Parliament decides."*

### Flashcard SJ-2069 — Trap: the 102nd Amendment gave states the power to make OBC lists
**Front:** Did the 102nd Amendment give States power to prepare their own OBC lists?
**Definition (learn):** No. The owner says the 102nd Amendment was read as centralising list-making; the 105th Amendment (2021) restored State power.
**Breakdown:**
- 102nd Amendment: NCBC constitutional status (SJ-2054).
- 105th Amendment (2021): restored State power (SJ-2053; ✅ owner 09).
- Maratha ruling of 2021 is ⚠️ owner-cited, not fetched.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2018 and 2021 (✅ owner 09).
**Links:** SJ-2053, SJ-2054
**Trap:** Confusing the 102nd with the 105th.
**Mains hook:** Amendment history shows how a judicial reading can require a corrective amendment.
**Back:** *"One-oh-two built the commission; one-oh-five gave states the pen."*

### Flashcard SJ-2070 — Trap: sub-categorisation of OBCs is implemented nationally
**Front:** Has OBC sub-categorisation been implemented nationally?
**Definition (learn):** No. It is under examination through the Rohini Commission; the owner records implementation as pending as of mid-2026.
**Breakdown:**
- Rohini Commission, 2017 (SJ-2064).
- Status pending (✅ owner 09 advanced; time-varying, ⚠️).
- Caste-data gap constrains any formula (SJ-2066).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Pending status as at mid-2026 (⚠️ owner).
**Links:** SJ-2064, SJ-2065, SJ-2066
**Trap:** The distractor is "implemented" or "notified nationally".
**Mains hook:** Sub-categorisation requires data first.
**Back:** *"Studied, not implemented."*

### Flashcard SJ-2071 — Advanced: the OBC identification paradox
**Front:** What is the identification paradox that the owner describes?
**Definition (learn):** OBC reservation rests on social-educational backwardness, yet the lack of published OBC population data prevents evidence-based calibration of proportions, sub-categorisation and mobility tracking.
**Breakdown:**
- Premise: backwardness justifies reservation (✅ owner 09 advanced).
- Gap: no OBC population data since 1931 (✅ owner 09 advanced).
- Consequence: calibration, sub-categorisation and mobility tracking are blind (⚠️ owner analysis).
- Possible fix: Census 2027 caste enumeration, once results exist (SJ-2065).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1931; Census 2027 (✅ owner 09).
**Links:** SJ-2066, SJ-2065, SJ-2064
**Trap:** Assuming Census 2027 has already solved the problem.
**Mains hook:** Name the paradox, then the data route and its limits. ⚠️ Analytical.
**Back:** *"Backward by evidence, unmeasured by policy."*

### Flashcard SJ-2072 — Advanced: Janhit Abhiyan's basic-structure dissent
**Front:** On what grounds did the Janhit Abhiyan minority say the EWS amendment violated the basic structure?
**Definition (learn):** The minority held that excluding SC/ST/OBC from the EWS quota violated equality, and that Article 16 reservation cannot rest solely on economic criteria.
**Breakdown:**
- Ground 1: excluding SC/ST/OBC/SEBC from the EWS quota violates the equality code (✅ Wikipedia).
- Ground 2: economic-only criteria undercut representation of socially and educationally backward classes (✅ Wikipedia).
- Majority: classification valid; basic structure not violated (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 3:2; 7 Nov 2022 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2060, SJ-2058
**Trap:** Presenting the dissent as the majority holding.
**Mains hook:** Equality and representation tensions frame the EWS debate. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Two dissenters: equality and representation both bleed."*

### Flashcard SJ-2073 — Mains hook: reservation as a means to social mobility
**Front:** How should a candidate answer "reservation is a means to social mobility, not an end in itself"?
**Definition (learn):** Accept that reservation provides access; argue that mobility requires capability, retention, promotion and social acceptance.
**Breakdown:**
- Mobility chain: identification → capabilities → entry → retention and promotion → assets and social acceptance (✅ owner 09 answer architecture).
- Use creamy layer for intra-group equity (SJ-2055).
- Recommend mobility tracking with Census 2027 data once available (⚠️ owner recommendation).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner's 15-mark framing (✅ owner 09).
**Links:** SJ-2050, SJ-2067, SJ-2071
**Trap:** Arguing that reservation alone creates mobility, or that it is pointless.
**Mains hook:** Reasoned verdict: affirmative action needs fair internal distribution and capability investment. ⚠️ Owner verdict.
**Back:** *"Entry opens, capability keeps the door open."*

### GROUP — Minorities: Rights and Welfare
Owners: `basic/10_Minorities-Rights-and-Welfare.md` and `advanced/10_Minorities-Rights-and-Welfare.md`.

### Flashcard SJ-2074 — Minority justice: protection, monitoring, development and outcome
**Front:** What are the four layers of minority rights and welfare in India?
**Definition (learn):** Constitutional protection (Articles 29 and 30), statutory monitoring (NCM), developmental schemes (PM VIKAS, PMJVK), and an outcome test on education, livelihood and inclusion.
**Breakdown:**
- Protective: Articles 29 and 30 (✅ owner 10).
- Monitoring: NCM under the NCM Act, 1992 (✅ owner 10).
- Developmental: PM VIKAS and area-based routes (✅ owner 10).
- Outcome: attainment, livelihood, representation, dignity (✅ owner 10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner's framework (✅ owner 10).
**Links:** SJ-2075, SJ-2076, SJ-2078, SJ-2079, SJ-2081, SJ-2094
**Trap:** Equating scheme coverage with minority rights. Schemes do not define constitutional minority status.
**Mains hook:** Rights and welfare must both work; outcomes decide. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Protect, monitor, develop, then measure."*

### Flashcard SJ-2075 — Six centrally notified minority communities (1993, Jains in 2014)
**Front:** Which communities are centrally notified as minorities, and when were Jains added?
**Definition (learn):** Muslims, Christians, Sikhs, Buddhists, Parsis (Zoroastrians) and Jains are the six centrally notified minority communities.
**Breakdown:**
- Six communities: Muslims, Christians, Sikhs, Buddhists, Parsis, Jains (✅ owner 10; ✅ Wikipedia: National Commission for Minorities).
- Original 1993 notification covered five; Jains added in 2014 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Notification under Section 2(c) of the NCM Act, 1992 (✅ owner 10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Jains 2014 (✅ Wikipedia). Gazette notification dated 23 Oct 1993 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2076, SJ-2080, SJ-2091
**Trap:** Saying only Muslims and Christians are minorities. Jains were added in 2014.
**Mains hook:** Central notification is one level; State-level status is decided differently (SJ-2091). ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Six faiths, Jains last in twenty-fourteen."*

### Flashcard SJ-2076 — National Commission for Minorities (NCM): statutory body
**Front:** What is the NCM's legal basis, composition and function?
**Definition (learn):** A statutory body under the National Commission for Minorities Act, 1992, constituted in 1993, that evaluates development, monitors safeguards, examines complaints and recommends action.
**Breakdown:**
- Basis: NCM Act, 1992 (✅ owner 10; ✅ Wikipedia).
- Constitution: 17 May 1993 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Composition: Chairperson, Vice-Chairperson and five Members (✅ owner 10).
- Status: statutory, not constitutional (✅ owner 10; ✅ Wikipedia).
- Recommendations are advisory and not self-executing (✅ owner 10 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1992 Act; 1993 constitution (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2077, SJ-2075, SJ-2093
**Trap:** Calling NCM constitutional. It is statutory.
**Mains hook:** Advisory monitoring needs government responsiveness to have effect. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Ninety-two Act, ninety-three born, advice not orders."*

### Flashcard SJ-2077 — Trap pair: NCM (statutory) versus NCSC, NCST and NCBC (constitutional)
**Front:** Which minority and backward-class commissions are statutory and which are constitutional?
**Definition (learn):** NCM is statutory under the 1992 Act; NCSC (Art 338), NCST (Art 338A) and NCBC (Art 338B) are constitutional.
**Breakdown:**
- NCM: statutory (✅ owner 10).
- NCSC: Article 338 (✅ owner 07).
- NCST: Article 338A (✅ owner 08).
- NCBC: Article 338B, after 102nd Amendment (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Articles 338, 338A, 338B (✅ owners 07, 08, 09; ✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2076, SJ-2013, SJ-2041, SJ-2054
**Trap:** The distractor is "NCM is one of the constitutional commissions".
**Mains hook:** Constitutional status gives institutional security; NCM's mandate is narrower in security. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"NCM is the statute; three-thirty-eight series is constitutional."*

### Flashcard SJ-2078 — Article 29: cultural and language rights
**Front:** What does Article 29 protect?
**Definition (learn):** The right of any section of citizens with a distinct language, script or culture to conserve it.
**Breakdown:**
- Protects conservation of distinct language, script or culture (✅ owner 10).
- Interpretation belongs to Polity (✅ owner 10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Article 29 (✅ owner 10).
**Links:** SJ-2079, SJ-2074
**Trap:** Article 29 is not limited to religious minorities.
**Mains hook:** Cultural conservation sits beside educational autonomy under Article 30. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Twenty-nine conserves language, script, culture."*

### Flashcard SJ-2079 — Article 30: right to establish and administer educational institutions
**Front:** What does Article 30 guarantee, and to whom?
**Definition (learn):** Minorities, religious or linguistic, have the right to establish and administer educational institutions of their choice.
**Breakdown:**
- Covers religious and linguistic minorities (✅ owner 10).
- Not immunity from reasonable regulation (✅ owner 10 semantic-completeness).
- Doctrine of TMA Pai (SJ-2080).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Article 30 (✅ owner 10).
**Links:** SJ-2078, SJ-2080, SJ-2095
**Trap:** "Art 30 covers religious minorities only" is wrong (SJ-2095).
**Mains hook:** Autonomy is real but regulated; the balance is the central question. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Thirty opens schools, regulation keeps them honest."*

### Flashcard SJ-2080 — TMA Pai Foundation (2002): state-level minority status and regulation
**Front:** What did TMA Pai hold on minority status and on institutional autonomy?
**Definition (learn):** The 2002 judgment held that minority status for Article 30 is determined at the State level, and that minorities may run institutions, including fee-setting and student selection, subject to reasonable regulation.
**Breakdown:**
- Minority status for Article 30 is determined at the State level (✅ owner 10 advanced; case year 2002 ✅ owner 10).
- Right to establish and administer, including fee and admission choices, subject to reasonable regulation to prevent maladministration (✅ owner 10 advanced).
- Cross-referenced to Polity for detailed doctrine (✅ owner 10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2002; State-level determination (✅ owner 10; ⚠️ case not fetched).
**Links:** SJ-2079, SJ-2091, SJ-2093
**Trap:** Saying minority status is national. TMA Pai says State level.
**Mains hook:** A community can be a minority in one State and not another; autonomy follows the unit. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Two-thousand-two: State decides who is a minority."*

### Flashcard SJ-2081 — PM VIKAS: five merged schemes and its routes
**Front:** Which five schemes merged into PM VIKAS, and what routes does it run?
**Definition (learn):** PM VIKAS (Pradhan Mantri Virasat Ka Samvardhan) is a Central Sector umbrella under the Ministry of Minority Affairs.
**Breakdown:**
- Merged: Seekho Aur Kamao, USTTAD, Nai Manzil, Nai Roshni, Hamari Dharohar (✅ owner 10).
- Routes: skilling, women's leadership and entrepreneurship, bridge education, credit facilitation (✅ owner 10).
- Pan-India scheme, not an area-infrastructure scheme (✅ owner 10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Five merged schemes (✅ owner 10).
**Links:** SJ-2082, SJ-2083, SJ-2074
**Trap:** PM VIKAS is not simply a scholarship or infrastructure scheme.
**Mains hook:** Capability routes need outcome tracking, not only enrolment. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Five schemes, one virasat umbrella."*

### Flashcard SJ-2082 — PMJVK versus PM VIKAS: area route and individual route
**Front:** How does PMJVK differ from PM VIKAS?
**Definition (learn):** PMJVK is an area-infrastructure route for minority-concentration areas; PM VIKAS is a pan-India capability umbrella.
**Breakdown:**
- PMJVK: infrastructure in minority-concentration areas (✅ owner 10).
- PM VIKAS: skilling, leadership, bridge education, credit (✅ owner 10).
- Keep the two analytically separate (✅ owner 10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner's description (✅ owner 10).
**Links:** SJ-2081, SJ-2074
**Trap:** Collapsing area infrastructure and individual capability support into one route.
**Mains hook:** A minority district can need both; the answer must name which route delivers what. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Area for roads, PM VIKAS for skills."*

### Flashcard SJ-2083 — Trap: minority scholarships are permanent entitlements
**Front:** Can a candidate assume that a historical minority scholarship is still open?
**Definition (learn):** No. Scholarship names, classes and annual application status change; check the current Ministry or National Scholarship Portal notice.
**Breakdown:**
- Owner: volatile and annual; check the notice (✅ owner 10).
- PM VIKAS bridge support does not prove that older scholarships remain (✅ owner 10).
- No current scholarship list is asserted here (⚠️).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Time-varying (⚠️).
**Links:** SJ-2081, SJ-2082
**Trap:** Quoting a historical scholarship as a current entitlement.
**Mains hook:** Scheme volatility weakens long-run planning for minority students. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Scholarships change; check the notice before claiming."*

### Flashcard SJ-2084 — Waqf: definition and the Waqf Act, 1995
**Front:** What is a waqf, and which Act governs it?
**Definition (learn):** A waqf is the dedication of movable or immovable property for purposes recognised by Muslim law as pious, religious or charitable, governed by the Waqf Act, 1995 and amendments.
**Breakdown:**
- Definition and governing Act (✅ owner 10).
- Administration: State Waqf Boards (statutory) under the Central Waqf Council oversight (✅ owner 10).
- Waqf by deed: formal documented dedication (✅ owner 10 advanced).
- Waqf by user: claim from long uninterrupted religious use without documentation (✅ owner 10 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Waqf Act, 1995 (✅ owner 10).
**Links:** SJ-2085, SJ-2088
**Trap:** Treating waqf-by-user as a formal deed. It is an undocumented claim.
**Mains hook:** Waqf reform turns on how user-based claims are registered and adjudicated. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Deed is written, user is lived."*

### Flashcard SJ-2085 — Waqf (Amendment) Act, 2025: enactment timeline
**Front:** What is the enactment timeline of the Waqf (Amendment) Act, 2025?
**Definition (learn):** Parliament passed the amendment in early April 2025; presidential assent and commencement followed within days.
**Breakdown:**
- Lok Sabha passed: 3 Apr 2025 (✅ Wikipedia: Waqf (Amendment) Act, 2025).
- Rajya Sabha passed: 4 Apr 2025 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Presidential assent: 5 Apr 2025 (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 10).
- Commenced: 8 Apr 2025 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Act number: owner says No. 14 of 2025 (⚠️ Wikipedia gives only Bill No. 109 of 2024; act number unverified).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 3, 4, 5, 8 Apr 2025 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2084, SJ-2086, SJ-2087, SJ-2090
**Trap:** Owner's "No. 14 of 2025" is unverified; do not quote without the Gazette.
**Mains hook:** Rapid enactment and litigation show the tension between regulation and autonomy. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Three, four, five, eight: the April passage."*

### Flashcard SJ-2086 — Supreme Court's 17 April 2025 interim order on waqf property
**Front:** What did the Supreme Court order on 17 April 2025 about waqf property?
**Definition (learn):** The Court ordered that no waqf property, including waqf-by-user, be denotified or altered until the next hearing.
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 17 Apr 2025 (✅ Wikipedia: Waqf (Amendment) Act, 2025).
- Effect: no denotification or alteration pending the next hearing (✅ Wikipedia).
- Centre separately said on 21 Apr 2025 it would hold provisions including waqf-by-user in abeyance (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 17 and 21 Apr 2025 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2085, SJ-2087
**Trap:** The April 2025 steps are not the September 2025 final order.
**Mains hook:** Interim status shows why sub judice provisions must be described as interim. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Seventeen April froze the denotification."*

### Flashcard SJ-2087 — Supreme Court's 15 September 2025 interim order on the Waqf Act
**Front:** What did the 15 September 2025 interim order do?
**Definition (learn):** The Court declined to stay the Act in its entirety and stayed specific provisions on an interim basis, pending final adjudication.
**Breakdown:**
- Not stayed: the Act in its entirety (✅ owner 10).
- Stayed (owner): the five-years-practising-Islam requirement until rules exist; the Collector-type power to determine disputed government-waqf property status (✅ owner 10).
- Not stayed (owner): prospective abolition of waqf-by-user (✅ owner 10; see SJ-2089).
- Wikipedia's fetched page did not state the order's contents; treat specific stays as ⚠️ not verified against the order.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 15 Sept 2025 (✅ owner 10; ⚠️ order contents not fetched).
**Links:** SJ-2086, SJ-2088, SJ-2089, SJ-2090
**Trap:** Presenting the interim order as the final constitutional ruling. It is interim and sub judice.
**Mains hook:** Distinguish enacted law, stayed provisions and pending questions. ⚠️ Owner-based.
**Back:** *"Whole Act stands; some clauses sleep until judgment."*

### Flashcard SJ-2088 — Non-Muslim membership caps on the Central Waqf Council and State Boards
**Front:** What numerical caps on non-Muslim membership did the owner attribute to the September 2025 order?
**Definition (learn):** The owner states a maximum of 4 of 22 on the Central Waqf Council and 3 of 11 on a State Waqf Board, while not staying appointment of non-Muslims as such.
**Breakdown:**
- Owner figures: 4 of 22 (Central Waqf Council); 3 of 11 (State Waqf Board) (✅ owner 10 current anchor).
- Fetched Wikipedia page describes the Act's design with two non-Muslim professionals on boards, not these caps (✅ Wikipedia).
- Caps not verified against the order (⚠️).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 4 of 22; 3 of 11 (⚠️ unverified).
**Links:** SJ-2087, SJ-2085
**Trap:** Quoting the caps as verified. They are an owner-stated figure; check the order.
**Mains hook:** Board composition is a minority-autonomy issue. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Four of twenty-two, three of eleven: check before quoting."*

### Flashcard SJ-2089 — Waqf-by-user: prospective abolition, not retrospective
**Front:** Does the Waqf Act's abolition of waqf-by-user apply to already-registered waqfs?
**Definition (learn):** The owner states that the amendment abolishes waqf-by-user prospectively for future claims and does not retrospectively affect already-registered waqf properties.
**Breakdown:**
- Prospective abolition for future claims (✅ owner 10).
- Not stayed by the September 2025 order (✅ owner 10 advanced).
- Already-registered waqfs not retrospectively affected (✅ owner 10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-stated (✅ owner 10; ⚠️ order text not fetched).
**Links:** SJ-2084, SJ-2087
**Trap:** Saying waqf-by-user is abolished for all properties.
**Mains hook:** A prospective rule protects existing claims; litigation decides scope. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Future claims lose user-status; the past stays."*

### Flashcard SJ-2090 — Trap: the Waqf (Amendment) Act, 2025 has been struck down
**Front:** Has the Supreme Court struck down the Waqf (Amendment) Act, 2025?
**Definition (learn):** No. The Court declined a wholesale stay and stayed specific provisions, while substantive constitutional questions remain pending.
**Breakdown:**
- Act operative except stayed provisions (✅ owner 10).
- Final judgment not delivered (✅ owner 10 advanced).
- Interim orders: 17 Apr 2025 and 15 Sep 2025 (SJ-2086, SJ-2087).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As at owner's date; ⚠️ time-varying.
**Links:** SJ-2085, SJ-2087
**Trap:** "Struck down" versus "partly stayed" is the trap pair.
**Mains hook:** Say "partly stayed and sub judice", never "struck down". ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Partly paused, not struck down."*

### Flashcard SJ-2091 — Trap: minority status for Article 30 is set at the national level
**Front:** Is minority status for Article 30 decided nationally?
**Definition (learn):** No. TMA Pai (2002) holds that status is determined at the State level.
**Breakdown:**
- TMA Pai: State level (✅ owner 10).
- Central notification (six communities) is a separate level (SJ-2075).
- A State may notify a community as a minority for State purposes (✅ owner 10 advanced illustration).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2002 (✅ owner 10).
**Links:** SJ-2080, SJ-2075
**Trap:** Central notification equals Article 30 status everywhere. It does not.
**Mains hook:** State-unit reasoning explains why minority rights vary by State. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Centre notifies six; the State decides Article 30."*

### Flashcard SJ-2092 — Sachar Committee (2006): the named outcome evidence
**Front:** What did the Sachar Committee (2006) document, and what is its limit?
**Definition (learn):** A high-level committee on the socio-economic and educational status of Muslims that documented gaps in education, formal employment, credit and public representation.
**Breakdown:**
- Year: 2006 (✅ owner 10).
- Findings: education, employment, credit, representation gaps (✅ owner 10).
- Limit: findings need updating and vary by region, class and gender (✅ owner 10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2006 (✅ owner 10).
**Links:** SJ-2094, SJ-2074
**Trap:** Using the 2006 figures as current; or universalising them across all minorities.
**Mains hook:** Shift from communal rhetoric to measurable capability gaps. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Two-thousand-six measured the gaps, not the faith."*

### Flashcard SJ-2093 — Advanced: autonomy versus state regulation in Waqf reform
**Front:** What is the contested boundary in Waqf reform?
**Definition (learn):** The balance between state regulation of religious endowments and minority institutional autonomy, which is sub judice.
**Breakdown:**
- Reform aims: registration, dispute resolution, governance (✅ owner 10 advanced).
- Autonomy concern: state intervention in religious endowments (✅ owner 10 advanced).
- Pending: minority autonomy and permissible extent of regulation (✅ owner 10; ⚠️ not fetched).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 Act; sub judice (✅ owner 10).
**Links:** SJ-2084, SJ-2085, SJ-2080
**Trap:** Declaring a verdict on autonomy before the Court rules.
**Mains hook:** Argue both state oversight and autonomy; name the pending issues. ⚠️ Analytical.
**Back:** *"Oversight versus autonomy, decided later."*

### Flashcard SJ-2094 — Advanced: outcome gap and the measurement problem
**Front:** Why must minority welfare be measured by outcomes rather than scheme coverage?
**Definition (learn):** Schemes often report training, bridge-education or infrastructure outputs, not changes in attainment, employment or representation.
**Breakdown:**
- Outcome gap: documented by Sachar (SJ-2092).
- Measurement gap: outputs tracked, outcomes not (✅ owner 10 advanced).
- Test: educational attainment, employment, representation (✅ owner 10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner's framework (⚠️ analytical).
**Links:** SJ-2074, SJ-2092
**Trap:** Counting disbursals as success.
**Mains hook:** Pick an outcome indicator and a baseline before praising a scheme. ⚠️ Analytical.
**Back:** *"Count outcomes, not just outputs."*

### Flashcard SJ-2095 — Trap: Article 30 covers only religious minorities
**Front:** Does Article 30 protect only religious minorities?
**Definition (learn):** No. Article 30 covers both religious and linguistic minorities.
**Breakdown:**
- Covers religious and linguistic minorities (✅ owner 10).
- Applies to establishing and administering educational institutions (SJ-2079).
- Reasonable regulation still applies (SJ-2080).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Article 30 (✅ owner 10).
**Links:** SJ-2079, SJ-2080
**Trap:** The distractor "religious only" is wrong.
**Mains hook:** Linguistic minorities are part of the Article 30 framework.
**Back:** *"Faith or tongue, thirty protects the school."*

### GROUP — Persons with Disabilities
Owners: `basic/11_Persons-with-Disabilities.md` and `advanced/11_Persons-with-Disabilities.md`.

### Flashcard SJ-2096 — Disability justice: from charity to rights
**Front:** What conceptual shift does the RPwD framework embody?
**Definition (learn):** A move from a charity or medical-cure view of disability to a rights-based, social-model view in which barriers, not only impairment, define disadvantage.
**Breakdown:**
- Medical model: disability as individual deficit (⚠️ owner 11 basic).
- Social model: disability as interaction with barriers (✅ owner 11 advanced).
- Rights model: enforceable equality, autonomy, accessibility, reasonable accommodation (✅ owner 11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner's framework (✅ owner 11).
**Links:** SJ-2097, SJ-2100, SJ-2106
**Trap:** The RPwD Act is based on the social model, not the medical model (SJ-2106).
**Mains hook:** Rights need enforceable duties and outcome tracking. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Barriers, not bodies, define the disability."*

### Flashcard SJ-2097 — Rights of Persons with Disabilities Act, 2016: enactment and commencement
**Front:** When was the RPwD Act enacted, assented and commenced, and what did it replace?
**Definition (learn):** The domestic law giving effect to UNCRPD obligations; it replaced the Persons with Disabilities Act, 1995.
**Breakdown:**
- Act No. 49 of 2016; assent 27 Dec 2016 (✅ Wikipedia: Rights of Persons with Disabilities Act, 2016).
- Commenced 15 June 2017 (✅ Wikipedia; not cross-checked against the gazette, ⚠️ for the date of partial commencement).
- Replaced PwD Act, 1995 (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 11).
- Central rules notified under Section 100 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 27 Dec 2016; 15 June 2017 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2098, SJ-2099, SJ-2100, SJ-2106
**Trap:** Dating the Act to 2016 and assuming it was in force that year.
**Mains hook:** Enactment-to-commencement lag matters for implementation questions. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Sixteen enacted, seventeen in force."*

### Flashcard SJ-2098 — The 21 specified disabilities under the RPwD Act, 2016
**Front:** How many disability categories does the RPwD Act specify, and what is the list's status?
**Definition (learn):** The Act specifies 21 disabilities; the government may notify more.
**Breakdown:**
- Count: 21, up from 7 under the 1995 Act (✅ owner 11).
- Includes blindness, low vision, leprosy-cured, hearing impairment, locomotor disability, dwarfism, intellectual disability, mental illness, autism spectrum disorder, cerebral palsy, muscular dystrophy, chronic neurological conditions, specific learning disabilities, multiple sclerosis, speech and language disability, thalassaemia, haemophilia, sickle cell disease, multiple disabilities including deafblindness, acid-attack victims, Parkinson's disease (✅ owner 11).
- Government may notify additional categories (✅ owner 11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 21 categories (✅ owner 11; ⚠️ list not fetched from the Act text).
**Links:** SJ-2097, SJ-2104, SJ-2115
**Trap:** The National Trust covers only four conditions, not the 21 (SJ-2117).
**Mains hook:** Wider categories expand entitlement but need certification capacity. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Seven became twenty-one, the list can grow."*

### Flashcard SJ-2099 — Benchmark disability (40%) and reservation eligibility
**Front:** What is benchmark disability, and what does it trigger?
**Definition (learn):** Disability of 40% or more of a specified type, certified by a medical authority; it triggers reservation and concession eligibility.
**Breakdown:**
- Threshold: 40% or more (✅ owner 11).
- Trigger: statutory reservation and concessions (✅ owner 11).
- Certification: medical authority; UDID (SJ-2102) (✅ owner 11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 40% (✅ owner 11).
**Links:** SJ-2100, SJ-2102, SJ-2118
**Trap:** Benchmark disability is not every disability; lower percentages may be excluded from reservation.
**Mains hook:** A fixed threshold can exclude people with real barriers. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Forty percent unlocks the reserved seat."*

### Flashcard SJ-2100 — Statutory PwD reservation: 4% government and 5% higher education
**Front:** What reservation does the RPwD Act provide, and how is it different from Article 16(4)?
**Definition (learn):** At least 4% in government establishments and 5% in higher-education institutions for persons with benchmark disabilities; it is statutory, not Article 16(4).
**Breakdown:**
- 4% government establishments (✅ owner 11).
- 5% higher-education institutions (✅ owner 11).
- Statutory mechanism, distinct from Art 16(4) for SC/ST/OBC (✅ owner 11).
- Not counted in the 50% constitutional ceiling jurisprudence (✅ owner 11 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 4% and 5% (✅ owner 11; ⚠️ section numbers not fetched).
**Links:** SJ-2099, SJ-2116, SJ-2101
**Trap:** The "Article 16(4)" distractor (SJ-2116).
**Mains hook:** Statutory quotas need identification of suitable posts to fill seats. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Four in government, five in higher ed, by statute."*

### Flashcard SJ-2101 — Reasonable accommodation (Section 2(y)) and denial as discrimination
**Front:** What is reasonable accommodation, and does denial amount to discrimination?
**Definition (learn):** Necessary and appropriate modification, not imposing a disproportionate or undue burden, to ensure equal enjoyment of rights (Section 2(y)).
**Breakdown:**
- Section 2(y) definition (✅ owner 11).
- Denial is recognised as a form of discrimination (✅ owner 11 advanced).
- Scope: physical, procedural, technological and attitudinal modification (✅ owner 11 traps).
- Balancing: necessity against burden, not automatic exemption (✅ owner 11 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Section 2(y) (✅ owner 11).
**Links:** SJ-2100, SJ-2108, SJ-2117
**Trap:** Reasonable accommodation is not only physical access (SJ-2117).
**Mains hook:** Accommodation is substantive equality; universal design reduces the need for individual adjustment. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Adjust without undue burden; refusing is discrimination."*

### Flashcard SJ-2102 — UDID: the Unique Disability ID
**Front:** What is UDID, and what does it do and not do?
**Definition (learn):** A centralised, nationally portable disability certificate and ID platform operated by DEPwD.
**Breakdown:**
- Standardises application, certificate and card (✅ owner 11).
- Portable across States (✅ owner 11).
- Does not itself confer every benefit or guarantee access (✅ owner 11).
- Counts are dynamic; cite the reporting date (✅ owner 11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Operated by DEPwD (✅ owner 11).
**Links:** SJ-2099, SJ-2110
**Trap:** Registration is not inclusion (SJ-2111).
**Mains hook:** Administrative reform cuts documentation burden but needs enrolment and access. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"One ID travels; benefits still need access."*

### Flashcard SJ-2103 — Accessible India (Sugamya Bharat) and SIPDA
**Front:** What are Sugamya Bharat's pillars, and what vehicle carries it since March 2024?
**Definition (learn):** Sugamya Bharat (Accessible India) is a three-pillar accessibility framework for the built environment, transport and ICT, carried from March 2024 through SIPDA.
**Breakdown:**
- Pillars: built environment, transport, ICT (✅ owner 11).
- Vehicle since March 2024: SIPDA (Scheme for Implementation of the RPwD Act) (✅ owner 11).
- Launch year of the campaign: ⚠️ not stated in owner.
- Compliance dashboards: cite reporting date (✅ owner 11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** March 2024 SIPDA (✅ owner 11).
**Links:** SJ-2097, SJ-2101, SJ-2108
**Trap:** Presenting the campaign label as a separate current programme.
**Mains hook:** Compliance on paper vs access on the ground. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Built, moved, connected: three pillars, now under SIPDA."*

### Flashcard SJ-2104 — ADIP scheme: assistive devices
**Front:** What does the ADIP scheme provide?
**Definition (learn):** Assistance to Disabled Persons for purchase or fitting of aids and appliances, providing devices such as wheelchairs, hearing aids and artificial limbs.
**Breakdown:**
- Devices: wheelchairs, tricycles, hearing aids, artificial limbs, braille kits (✅ owner 11).
- Delivery: camp mode and institutional mode by DEPwD (✅ owner 11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** DEPwD (✅ owner 11).
**Links:** SJ-2105, SJ-2096
**Trap:** ADIP provides devices; it is not the guardianship route (National Trust).
**Mains hook:** Device access supports independence; maintenance and fitting matter. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Aids for mobility, camps for reach."*

### Flashcard SJ-2105 — National Trust (1999 Act): only four conditions
**Front:** Which four conditions does the National Trust Act, 1999 cover?
**Definition (learn):** The National Trust for Welfare of Persons with Autism, Cerebral Palsy, Mental Retardation and Multiple Disabilities Act, 1999 covers only those four conditions.
**Breakdown:**
- Autism, cerebral palsy, mental retardation (statutory term), multiple disabilities (✅ owner 11).
- Functions: legal guardianship support; registered-organisation support (✅ owner 11).
- Narrower than the 21 RPwD categories (✅ owner 11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1999 Act (✅ owner 11).
**Links:** SJ-2098, SJ-2117, SJ-2104
**Trap:** The National Trust covering all 21 disabilities (SJ-2117).
**Mains hook:** Scope mismatch shows institutional fragmentation. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Four conditions, ninety-nine statute."*

### Flashcard SJ-2106 — UNCRPD (ratified 2007) and the RPwD Act as domestic law
**Front:** When did India ratify the UNCRPD, and which law gives it effect domestically?
**Definition (learn):** India ratified the UN Convention on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities in 2007; the RPwD Act, 2016 is the domestic law.
**Breakdown:**
- Ratification: 2007 (✅ owner 11; ✅ Wikipedia: RPwD Act page).
- Domestic law: RPwD Act, 2016 (✅ owner 11).
- Social model alignment (✅ owner 11 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2007 (✅ owner 11; ✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2096, SJ-2097
**Trap:** Confusing ratification (2007) with the domestic Act (2016).
**Mains hook:** International obligation needs domestic enforcement. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Oh-seven signed, sixteen made it law."*

### Flashcard SJ-2107 — Medical, social and rights models of disability
**Front:** How do the medical, social and rights models differ?
**Definition (learn):** The medical model locates disability in impairment; the social model locates it in barriers; the rights model adds enforceable duties.
**Breakdown:**
- Medical: cure or rehabilitation of the individual (⚠️ owner 11 basic).
- Social: barriers in environment, institutions and attitudes (✅ owner 11).
- Rights: UNCRPD-based enforceable equality, autonomy, accessibility (✅ owner 11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner's framework.
**Links:** SJ-2096, SJ-2106
**Trap:** The RPwD Act is not built on the medical model (SJ-2106).
**Mains hook:** Use the social model to explain why accessibility, not only cure, matters. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Medical fixes the body, social fixes the barrier, rights enforce both."*

### Flashcard SJ-2108 — Universal design versus retrofitting
**Front:** How does universal design differ from retrofitting?
**Definition (learn):** Universal design builds accessibility into original construction; retrofitting adds features to existing structures.
**Breakdown:**
- Universal design: step-free entrances from the start (✅ owner 11 advanced).
- Retrofitting: ramps and fixes added later (✅ owner 11 advanced).
- Universal design is more effective and cost-efficient; retrofitting is often the reality (✅ owner 11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner's framework.
**Links:** SJ-2103, SJ-2118
**Trap:** Treating universal design and retrofitting as the same concept.
**Mains hook:** Mandate universal design for new construction; plan retrofits for legacy assets. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Build it in, or bolt it on later."*

### Flashcard SJ-2109 — Chief Commissioner and State Commissioners for PwDs
**Front:** What do the Chief Commissioner and State Commissioners do under the RPwD Act?
**Definition (learn):** Statutory authorities that receive complaints, monitor safeguards and recommend remedial measures for persons with disabilities.
**Breakdown:**
- Chief Commissioner at the Centre (✅ owner 11 advanced).
- State Commissioners at the State level (✅ owner 11 advanced).
- Functions: complaints, monitoring, recommendations (✅ owner 11 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Statutory (✅ owner 11; ⚠️ section not fetched).
**Links:** SJ-2097, SJ-2103
**Trap:** The disability development-finance corporation is a credit route, not the regulator (SJ-2113).
**Mains hook:** Grievance institutions need powers and capacity. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Commissioners hear, monitor, recommend."*

### Flashcard SJ-2110 — Census 2011 disability prevalence: 2.2% and undercount
**Front:** What did Census 2011 estimate for disability prevalence, and why may it undercount?
**Definition (learn):** Census 2011 estimated disability at about 2.2% of the population; self-reporting and narrow framing likely undercount actual prevalence.
**Breakdown:**
- Figure: about 2.2% (✅ owner 11 advanced).
- Limits: self-reporting, narrow question framing (✅ owner 11 advanced).
- Census 2027 may update under revised methods (⚠️ owner 11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2.2%, Census 2011 (✅ owner 11).
**Links:** SJ-2102, SJ-2111
**Trap:** Treating the 2.2% as the true prevalence.
**Mains hook:** Data undercounting shapes target setting. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Two-point-two is a floor, not the count."*

### Flashcard SJ-2111 — Coverage versus substantive participation
**Front:** Why is a certificate or scheme count not the same as inclusion?
**Definition (learn):** Coverage counts certificates and benefits; substantive participation measures equal access to education, employment and public life.
**Breakdown:**
- Coverage: certificates issued, devices given (✅ owner 11 advanced).
- Participation: access and dignity in practice (✅ owner 11 advanced).
- Owner: a certificate or portal registration is not substantive inclusion (✅ owner 11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner's concept.
**Links:** SJ-2102, SJ-2110, SJ-2118
**Trap:** Using coverage counts as proof of inclusion.
**Mains hook:** Measure participation, not registration. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Count access, not just cards."*

### Flashcard SJ-2112 — 2022 GS-II Mains: RPwD and sensitisation
**Front:** What did the 2022 GS-II question ask about the RPwD Act, and what route does the owner give?
