**Definition (learn):** The 2022 GS-II question (Q7) asked about the RPwD Act, 2016 and sensitisation; it is a Comment question of 10 marks and 150 words.
**Breakdown:**
- Q7, 2022 GS-II, Comment, 10 marks, 150 words (✅ owner 11 PYQ integration).
- Route: context, core dimensions, evidence, counterpoint, conclusion (✅ owner 11).
- Core route supersedes older Advanced ownership (✅ owner 11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2022 GS-II Q7 (✅ owner 11).
**Links:** SJ-2096, SJ-2097, SJ-2101, SJ-2111
**Trap:** Answering with a list of Act sections without the sensitisation dimension.
**Mains hook:** Sensitisation makes accommodation a social norm, not only a legal one. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Ten marks, one hundred fifty words, rights plus attitude."*

### Flashcard SJ-2113 — 2026 Prelims GS-I Q56: disability law, accessibility mission and development corporation
**Front:** What did the 2026 Prelims Q56 test, and what is the key status?
**Definition (learn):** Q56 tested disability rights law, the accessibility mission and the disability development corporation.
**Breakdown:**
- Q56, 2026 Prelims GS-I (✅ owner 11 PYQ integration).
- Demand: statute, accessibility mission and its vehicle, development-finance corporation as a separate credit route (✅ owner 11).
- Key: provisional; no answer letter recorded or inferred (✅ owner 11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2026 Prelims Q56 (✅ owner 11).
**Links:** SJ-2097, SJ-2103, SJ-2109
**Trap:** Mistaking the development-finance corporation for the regulator.
**Mains hook:** Keep the three institutions distinct. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Law regulates, mission builds, corporation lends."*

### Flashcard SJ-2114 — Case anchors: *Vikash Kumar v. UPSC* and *Jeeja Ghosh*
**Front:** What do *Vikash Kumar v. UPSC* and *Jeeja Ghosh* illustrate in disability rights?
**Definition (learn):** *Vikash Kumar* treats reasonable accommodation as equality, not charity; *Jeeja Ghosh* connects dignity and non-discrimination to air-travel treatment.
**Breakdown:**
- *Vikash Kumar v. UPSC*: accommodation as equality (✅ owner 11 advanced).
- *Jeeja Ghosh*: dignity and non-discrimination in air travel (✅ owner 11 advanced).
- Judgment years not stated in owner (⚠️).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Case years ⚠️ not fetched.
**Links:** SJ-2101, SJ-2112
**Trap:** Citing cases for a holding they did not give.
**Mains hook:** Case law makes accommodation enforceable. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Vikash: equality. Jeeja: dignity in the air."*

### Flashcard SJ-2115 — Trap: the 2016 Act specifies seven disability categories
**Front:** Does the RPwD Act, 2016 specify seven categories?
**Definition (learn):** No. It specifies 21. The seven-category list belonged to the repealed 1995 Act.
**Breakdown:**
- RPwD 2016: 21 (SJ-2098).
- PwD Act 1995: seven (✅ owner 11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 21 vs 7 (✅ owner 11).
**Links:** SJ-2098, SJ-2097
**Trap:** Mixing up the two Acts' counts.
**Mains hook:** The expansion widened the rights base.
**Back:** *"Seven was the old Act; twenty-one is the new."*

### Flashcard SJ-2116 — Trap: PwD reservation is under Article 16(4)
**Front:** Is PwD reservation under Article 16(4)?
**Definition (learn):** No. It is statutory under the RPwD Act; Article 16(4) concerns SC/ST/OBC and the constitutional route.
**Breakdown:**
- Statutory: RPwD (SJ-2100).
- Art 16(4) distinct (✅ owner 11).
- Not subject to Art 16(4) jurisprudence (✅ owner 11 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 4% and 5% (✅ owner 11).
**Links:** SJ-2100, SJ-2051
**Trap:** The distractor is "Article 16(4)".
**Mains hook:** Know the legal source for each reservation.
**Back:** *"Statute, not sixteen-four."*

### Flashcard SJ-2117 — Trap: National Trust covers all 21 RPwD disabilities
**Front:** Does the National Trust Act, 1999 cover all 21 RPwD disabilities?
**Definition (learn):** No. It covers four conditions only.
**Breakdown:**
- Four conditions (SJ-2105).
- 21 RPwD categories (SJ-2098).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1999 vs 2016 (✅ owner 11).
**Links:** SJ-2105, SJ-2098
**Trap:** Assuming one commission covers every disability.
**Mains hook:** Scope mismatch between old and new institutions.
**Back:** *"Four in ninety-nine, twenty-one in sixteen."*

### Flashcard SJ-2118 — Trap: reasonable accommodation means physical access only
**Front:** Is reasonable accommodation limited to physical access?
**Definition (learn):** No. It covers any necessary modification, physical, procedural, technological or attitudinal, that enables equal participation.
**Breakdown:**
- Physical access is one component (✅ owner 11 traps).
- Examples: extra exam time, screen-reader access (✅ owner 11 applications).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Section 2(y) (✅ owner 11).
**Links:** SJ-2101, SJ-2108
**Trap:** Narrowing accommodation to ramps.
**Mains hook:** Procedural and technological accommodation are often cheaper than structural works.
**Back:** *"Ramps are one answer, not the answer."*

### Flashcard SJ-2119 — Advanced: benchmark rigidity and private-sector enforcement
**Front:** What limits does the owner identify in RPwD enforcement?
**Definition (learn):** A fixed 40% threshold may exclude people with lower percentages, and private-sector enforcement is weaker than government enforcement.
**Breakdown:**
- Benchmark rigidity: 40% threshold (✅ owner 11 advanced limitation).
- Private employers: duty applies to all establishments; enforcement weaker (✅ owner 11 advanced).
- Retrofitting costs and extended timelines (✅ owner 11 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner's analysis (⚠️).
**Links:** SJ-2099, SJ-2101, SJ-2108
**Trap:** Assuming the duty applies with equal enforcement to private firms.
**Mains hook:** Recommend universal design, faster retrofits and stronger private-sector enforcement. ⚠️ Owner recommendation.
**Back:** *"Forty is a line; private firms need a watchdog."*

### GROUP — Elderly and Senior Citizens
Owners: `basic/12_Elderly-and-Senior-Citizens.md` and `advanced/12_Elderly-and-Senior-Citizens.md`.

### Flashcard SJ-2120 — Elderly welfare architecture: law, scheme, care and innovation
**Front:** What four layers make up elderly welfare in India?
**Definition (learn):** Statutory maintenance (2007 Act), welfare schemes (AVYAY), geriatric care and elder-abuse protection, and innovation support (SAGE).
**Breakdown:**
- Legal: Maintenance and Welfare of Parents and Senior Citizens Act, 2007 (✅ owner 12).
- Welfare: AVYAY umbrella (IPSC, RVY, Elderline) (✅ owner 12).
- Care: geriatric health and elder-abuse protection (✅ owner 12).
- Innovation: SAGE (✅ owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner's framework (✅ owner 12).
**Links:** SJ-2121, SJ-2122, SJ-2129, SJ-2132, SJ-2135
**Trap:** Treating welfare schemes as a substitute for enforceable maintenance.
**Mains hook:** Integrate law, welfare and geriatric health; a single lever will not do. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Law maintains, schemes support, clinics care, startups innovate."*

### Flashcard SJ-2121 — Senior citizen: 60 years and above
**Front:** What age defines a senior citizen under the 2007 Act?
**Definition (learn):** A citizen of India aged 60 years or above (Section 2(h)).
**Breakdown:**
- Age threshold: 60 (✅ owner 12; ✅ Wikipedia: Maintenance and Welfare of Parents and Senior Citizens Act, 2007).
- A parent qualifies whether or not a senior citizen (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Section 2(h) (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2122, SJ-2138
**Trap:** Using 65 (a common pension or scheme age) for the Act's definition.
**Mains hook:** Scheme ages and Act ages differ; check the source.
**Back:** *"Sixty opens the Act's door."*

### Flashcard SJ-2122 — Maintenance and Welfare of Parents and Senior Citizens Act, 2007: enactment and commencement
**Front:** When was the 2007 Act assented to, and how did commencement work?
**Definition (learn):** A statute giving senior citizens and parents a maintenance route, with commencement left to States and UTs.
**Breakdown:**
- Assent: 29 Dec 2007 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Commencement: State-wise; earliest listed Jharkhand and Karnataka, 1 Apr 2008 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Later States: Uttar Pradesh, 25 Sep 2012 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Jammu and Kashmir and Ladakh: 31 Oct 2019 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 29 Dec 2007 assent (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2123, SJ-2124, SJ-2125, SJ-2141
**Trap:** Assuming a single national commencement date.
**Mains hook:** State-wise commencement explains uneven tribunal coverage. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Oh-seven assented, States switched it on."*

### Flashcard SJ-2123 — Maintenance obligation: children and relatives who are heirs
**Front:** Who is legally obliged to maintain parents and senior citizens under the 2007 Act?
**Definition (learn):** Children and relatives who are or will be heirs of the senior citizen's property.
**Breakdown:**
- Obligation: children and relatives (✅ owner 12).
- Heirs limb: relatives who are or will be legal heirs (✅ owner 12).
- Maintenance covers food, clothing, residence and medical care (✅ owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner's statement (✅ owner 12).
**Links:** SJ-2124, SJ-2126, SJ-2141
**Trap:** Only children are obligated (SJ-2141).
**Mains hook:** Family duty is not a substitute for public responsibility. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Children and heirs owe, under the Act."*

### Flashcard SJ-2124 — Maintenance Tribunal: time limits, interim relief and appeal
**Front:** What procedural rules govern the Maintenance Tribunal?
**Definition (learn):** A summary forum that decides applications within 90 days of notice, can grant interim maintenance and hears appeals.
**Breakdown:**
- Disposal: 90 days of service of notice; extension by up to 30 days in exceptional circumstances with reasons (✅ Wikipedia).
- Interim maintenance (✅ Wikipedia).
- Appeal to the Appellate Tribunal within 60 days, delay excusable for sufficient cause (✅ Wikipedia).
- Warrants not issued if execution sought after 3 months from due date (✅ Wikipedia).
- Tribunal established in every sub-division (✅ owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 90 days; +30 days; appeal 60 days (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2123, SJ-2125, SJ-2127
**Trap:** Tribunals are summary bodies, not unlimited civil courts (SJ-2141).
**Mains hook:** Time limits help only if orders are enforced. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Ninety days to decide, sixty to appeal."*

### Flashcard SJ-2125 — ₹10,000 per month: maximum maintenance allowance
**Front:** What is the maximum monthly maintenance a Tribunal can order under the Act?
**Definition (learn):** The allowance prescribed by the State cannot exceed ₹10,000 per month.
**Breakdown:**
- Ceiling: ₹10,000 per month (✅ Wikipedia: Section 9(2)).
- Owner matches (✅ owner 12).
- Cap may lag inflation (✅ owner 12 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ₹10,000; Section 9(2) (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2124, SJ-2126
**Trap:** Reading the cap as a floor; or citing 2025 Private Members' proposals as having changed it (SJ-2134).
**Mains hook:** A fixed cap may be inadequate in high-cost settings. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Ten thousand a month, Section nine, two."*

### Flashcard SJ-2126 — Section 23: revocation of transfers made on condition of care
**Front:** When can a senior citizen seek a declaration that a property transfer is void?
**Definition (learn):** Where a transfer was made on the condition that the transferee will provide basic amenities and physical needs, and the transferee fails, the Tribunal may declare the transfer void.
**Breakdown:**
- Section 23 declaration (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 12).
- Trigger: failure of the care condition, not any later family dispute (✅ owner 12).
- Transfer by gift or otherwise (✅ owner 12).
- Fraud, coercion and undue influence grounds are also in Section 23 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Section 23 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2123, SJ-2124
**Trap:** Treating revocation as automatic for every family dispute.
**Mains hook:** Property is the lever that makes care enforceable. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Care is the condition; failure voids the gift."*

### Flashcard SJ-2127 — Section 24: penalty for abandonment
**Front:** What is the penalty for abandoning a senior citizen under the Act?
**Definition (learn):** Imprisonment up to three months, or a fine up to ₹5,000, or both.
**Breakdown:**
- Imprisonment up to three months (✅ Wikipedia: Section 24).
- Fine up to ₹5,000 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Owner says enhanced penalties were proposed in 2019 Bill, not enacted (SJ-2134).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Section 24 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2123, SJ-2134
**Trap:** The 2019 Bill's enhanced penalties are not current law.
**Mains hook:** Low penalties weaken deterrence against abandonment. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Three months or five thousand for abandoning a parent."*

### Flashcard SJ-2128 — Old-age homes: one per district, capacity claim unverified
**Front:** What does the Act require for old-age homes?
**Definition (learn):** State governments may establish at least one old-age home in each district for indigent senior citizens.
**Breakdown:**
- At least one per district (✅ Wikipedia).
- Owner's capacity figure: at least 150 indigent seniors (⚠️ owner 12; not verified, Wikipedia gives no capacity).
- Functionality remains an implementation question (✅ owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** One per district (✅ Wikipedia); 150 capacity (⚠️).
**Links:** SJ-2129, SJ-2120
**Trap:** Treating 150 as a statutory capacity. Verify.
**Mains hook:** Institutional care is not the default solution (✅ owner 12). ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"One home per district, capacity to be checked."*

### Flashcard SJ-2129 — AVYAY: the umbrella elderly-welfare scheme
**Front:** What components make up AVYAY?
**Definition (learn):** Atal Vayo Abhyuday Yojana, an umbrella scheme covering IPSC, RVY and Elderline.
**Breakdown:**
- IPSC: grants for old-age homes, day-care and multi-service centres (✅ owner 12).
- RVY: aids and devices (SJ-2130).
- Elderline: national helpline (SJ-2131).
- Launch year not stated in owner (⚠️).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner's components (✅ owner 12).
**Links:** SJ-2128, SJ-2130, SJ-2131
**Trap:** AVYAY is a scheme, not a statute.
**Mains hook:** Scheme coverage is not the same as legal entitlement.
**Back:** *"Three parts: homes, aids, helpline."*

### Flashcard SJ-2130 — RVY: aids and devices through ALIMCO
**Front:** Who is eligible for RVY, and which agency delivers devices?
**Definition (learn):** Rashtriya Vayoshri Yojana provides aids and devices through ALIMCO to eligible persons aged 60+ with age-related infirmity.
**Breakdown:**
- Agency: ALIMCO (✅ owner 12).
- Eligibility: BPL, or family income up to ₹15,000 per month under current guidelines (✅ owner 12; ⚠️ guideline not fetched).
- Devices: spectacles, hearing aids, and similar (✅ owner 12 applications).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ₹15,000 per month (⚠️ guideline).
**Links:** SJ-2129, SJ-2131
**Trap:** The income limit is a guideline figure; check the current guideline.
**Mains hook:** Assistive devices support independence in old age. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Aids through ALIMCO, for the poor and the infirm."*

### Flashcard SJ-2131 — Elderline: the national helpline for senior citizens
**Front:** What is Elderline and what number does it use?
**Definition (learn):** A national helpline for senior citizens providing information, grievance redress and emergency response.
**Breakdown:**
- Helpline: 14567 (✅ owner 12; typical number).
- Functions: information, grievance, emergency (✅ owner 12 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 14567 (✅ owner 12).
**Links:** SJ-2129, SJ-2130
**Trap:** Confusing Elderline with the RVY device route.
**Mains hook:** Helplines help only if linked to response and follow-up. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"One-four-five-six-seven, the elder's call."*

### Flashcard SJ-2132 — SAGE: Seniorcare Ageing Growth Engine
**Front:** What does SAGE do?
**Definition (learn):** An initiative that supports elderly-care start-ups, products and services through a curated marketplace and incubation.
**Breakdown:**
- Curated marketplace and incubation (✅ owner 12).
- Aim: private-sector innovation in eldercare (✅ owner 12 advanced).
- Participant counts not asserted (✅ owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Launch year ⚠️ not stated.
**Links:** SJ-2120, SJ-2129
**Trap:** SAGE is not a pension or care guarantee.
**Mains hook:** Innovation supports supply; it does not replace public care. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Startups for eldercare, not guarantees for elders."*

### Flashcard SJ-2133 — IGNOAPS: central pension floor of ₹200 and ₹500
**Front:** What are the central pension amounts under IGNOAPS for BPL seniors?
**Definition (learn):** Central assistance under the Indira Gandhi National Old Age Pension Scheme: ₹200 monthly for ages 60–79 and ₹500 for 80 and above, with State top-ups.
**Breakdown:**
- Ages 60–79: ₹200 per month (✅ owner 12).
- Ages 80 and above: ₹500 per month (✅ owner 12).
- State top-ups vary (✅ owner 12).
- Adequacy critique: central floor is low (✅ owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ₹200 and ₹500 (✅ owner 12; ⚠️ time-varying, not fetched).
**Links:** SJ-2120, SJ-2121
**Trap:** Quoting a State's higher combined pension as the national amount.
**Mains hook:** Pension adequacy, not coverage alone, decides poverty relief. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Two hundred to seventy-nine, five hundred from eighty."*

### Flashcard SJ-2134 — Amendment bills: 2019 bill lapsed, 2025 Private Members' Bills pending
**Front:** What is the legal status of the 2019 Bill and the 2025 Private Members' Bills?
**Definition (learn):** The 2019 Government Bill lapsed with the dissolution of the Lok Sabha; the 2025 Private Members' Bills are proposals, not enacted law.
**Breakdown:**
- 2019 Bill: introduced in Lok Sabha Dec 2019; lapsed; never enacted (✅ owner 12).
- 2019 Bill proposals: broader dignity definition, enhanced penalties, maintenance deduction from salary (✅ owner 12 advanced).
- 2025 Private Members' Bills: introduced in both Houses; not enacted as at 21 July 2026 (✅ owner 12; ⚠️ time-varying).
- Wikipedia fetched page mentions no 2019 or 2025 bill (✅ Wikipedia; note conflict only in coverage).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Dec 2019 introduction (✅ owner 12; ⚠️ not fetched).
**Links:** SJ-2125, SJ-2127
**Trap:** Calling the 2025 Private Members' Bills an "Amendment Act, 2025".
**Mains hook:** Distinguish enacted law from proposals. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Nineteen lapsed; twenty-five only proposed."*

### Flashcard SJ-2135 — Elder abuse: typology and under-reporting
**Front:** What are the four forms of elder abuse, and why is it under-reported?
**Definition (learn):** Physical abuse, financial or property abuse, emotional or psychological abuse, and neglect, often committed within families and under-reported due to dependence and shame.
**Breakdown:**
- Physical: violence, restraint (✅ owner 12 advanced).
- Financial or property: coerced transfer, theft, misuse of funds (✅ owner 12 advanced).
- Emotional: verbal aggression, isolation, humiliation (✅ owner 12 advanced).
- Neglect: failure to provide care, abandonment (✅ owner 12 advanced).
- Under-reporting: dependence, shame, family loyalty (✅ owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner's typology (⚠️ classification from owner).
**Links:** SJ-2126, SJ-2127, SJ-2136
**Trap:** Elder abuse is not only physical violence (✅ owner 12 traps).
**Mains hook:** Detection needs sensitised systems, not only enforcement. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Body, money, mind, and neglect: four ways to harm."*

### Flashcard SJ-2136 — Active ageing and the Madrid Plan (2002)
**Front:** What is active ageing, and which international plan anchors it?
**Definition (learn):** Active ageing promotes continued participation in social, economic, cultural, spiritual and civic life, not merely absence of disease.
**Breakdown:**
- Concept: WHO and Madrid Plan (✅ owner 12 advanced).
- Plan: Madrid International Plan of Action on Ageing, 2002 (✅ owner 12 advanced).
- Implication: enable productivity and engagement, not only protection (✅ owner 12 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2002 Madrid Plan (✅ owner 12).
**Links:** SJ-2120, SJ-2137, SJ-2139
**Trap:** Active ageing is not forced employment.
**Mains hook:** Use active ageing to move beyond a welfare-only frame. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Madrid two-thousand-two: participate, don't just protect."*

### Flashcard SJ-2137 — LASI: the Longitudinal Ageing Study in India
**Front:** What does LASI provide, and how should it be cited?
**Definition (learn):** A national longitudinal survey on the health, economic and social conditions of India's older population.
**Breakdown:**
- Coverage: health, economic and social conditions (✅ owner 12 advanced).
- Cite wave and year; do not infer causation from association (✅ owner 12).
- Provides some elder-abuse estimates (✅ owner 12 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Wave not stated in owner (⚠️).
**Links:** SJ-2135, SJ-2136, SJ-2139
**Trap:** Quoting LASI without its wave and year.
**Mains hook:** Longitudinal data show change; cross-sectional data show snapshots. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"LASI tracks the ageing, wave by wave."*

### Flashcard SJ-2138 — Old-age dependency ratio and India's 60+ share
**Front:** What is the old-age dependency ratio, and what did Census 2011 show for the 60+ share?
**Definition (learn):** The ratio of the population aged 60+ (or 65+) to the working-age population; a rising ratio increases care and support burdens.
**Breakdown:**
- Definition (✅ owner 12 advanced).
- Census 2011: about 8% aged 60+ (✅ owner 12 advanced).
- Projections rising; exact figures not asserted (✅ owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ~8%, Census 2011 (✅ owner 12).
**Links:** SJ-2120, SJ-2136, SJ-2137
**Trap:** Declining share claim is wrong; the share is rising.
**Mains hook:** Ageing pressure falls on working-age carers, often women. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Sixty-plus over working-age: the burden ratio."*

### Flashcard SJ-2139 — 2020 GS-II Mains: geriatric and maternal care health policies
**Front:** What did the 2020 GS-II question ask, and which part does the elderly-care owner answer?
**Definition (learn):** The question asked about health policies for geriatric and maternal care; this owner answers the geriatric limb.
**Breakdown:**
- Q6, 2020 GS-II, Discuss, 10 marks, 150 words (✅ owner 12 PYQ integration).
- Elderly-care half owned here; maternal-care half owned by health-systems owner (✅ owner 12).
- Route: geriatric-care, income, care economy (✅ owner 12 answer architecture).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2020 GS-II Q6 (✅ owner 12).
**Links:** SJ-2137, SJ-2136, SJ-2138
**Trap:** Claiming the maternal limb as this owner's.
**Mains hook:** Name the care domain and the gap; PM-JAY's 70+ route covers hospitalisation only. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Geriatric care half is ours; maternal care is health's."*

### Flashcard SJ-2140 — Trap: the 2025 amendment to the Act is in force
**Front:** Is a 2025 amendment to the 2007 Act in force?
**Definition (learn):** No. The 2019 Bill lapsed and the 2025 Private Members' Bills are not enacted; the 2007 Act remains current law.
**Breakdown:**
- 2019 Bill: lapsed (SJ-2134).
- 2025 PMBs: not enacted as at owner's date (SJ-2134).
- 2007 Act: current law (✅ owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Status as at 21 July 2026 (✅ owner 12; ⚠️ time-varying).
**Links:** SJ-2134, SJ-2122
**Trap:** The "2025 Amendment Act" distractor.
**Mains hook:** Cite the status date for any amendment claim.
**Back:** *"Two-five is a bill, not an Act."*

### Flashcard SJ-2141 — Trap: only children owe maintenance; tribunals are civil courts
**Front:** Who owes maintenance under the Act, and what kind of body is the Tribunal?
**Definition (learn):** Children and relatives who are or will be heirs owe maintenance; the Tribunal is a summary body with a capped allowance and time-bound adjudication, not an unlimited civil court.
**Breakdown:**
- Obligation: children and relatives (SJ-2123).
- Tribunal: summary and time-bound (SJ-2124).
- Cap: ₹10,000 per month (SJ-2125).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner's traps (✅ owner 12).
**Links:** SJ-2123, SJ-2124, SJ-2125
**Trap:** Two distractors in one card: "only children" and "unlimited civil court".
**Mains hook:** Summary forums give speed, but enforcement must follow.
**Back:** *"Children and heirs owe; the tribunal is quick, not unlimited."*

### GROUP — Transgender Persons and Denotified, Nomadic and Marginalised Communities
Owners: `basic/13_Transgender-Persons-and-Denotified-Nomadic-Marginalised-Communities.md` and `advanced/13_Transgender-Persons-and-Denotified-Nomadic-Marginalised-Communities.md`.

### Flashcard SJ-2142 — Recognitive justice: identity denial in two marginalised groups
**Front:** What unifies transgender persons and DNT/NT/SNT communities in the owner's framework?
**Definition (learn):** Both face recognitive injustice: denial of identity and dignity, which requires statutory recognition, anti-discrimination protection and targeted welfare.
**Breakdown:**
- Transgender: NALSA (2014), 2019 Act, 2026 Amendment, SMILE (✅ owner 13).
- DNT/NT/SNT: Criminal Tribes Act legacy, Idate and Renke Commissions, DWBDNC, SEED (✅ owner 13).
- Common sequence: recognise identity, prohibit discrimination, deliver welfare (✅ owner 13).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner's framework (✅ owner 13).
**Links:** SJ-2143, SJ-2160, SJ-2169, SJ-2171
**Trap:** Merging the two groups into one generic vulnerable category.
**Mains hook:** Recognition without enforcement and documentation remains on paper. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Name, protect, provide: the recognitive chain."*

### Flashcard SJ-2143 — NALSA v. Union of India (2014): self-identified gender
**Front:** What did NALSA (2014) recognise for transgender persons?
**Definition (learn):** The Supreme Court recognised the right to self-identified gender under Articles 14, 15 and 21, using a psychological test for gender identity.
**Breakdown:**
- Articles 14, 15 and 21 (✅ owner 13).
- Psychological test: deeply felt sense of self, not anatomy (✅ owner 13 advanced).
- Directed treating transgender persons as socially and educationally backward for OBC-type benefits (✅ owner 13 advanced).
- Year 2014 (✅ owner 13); exact judgment date ⚠️ not fetched.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2014 (✅ owner 13).
**Links:** SJ-2144, SJ-2157, SJ-2170
**Trap:** NALSA and the 2019 Act are not identical; NALSA is constitutional, the Act is statutory (✅ owner 13 advanced).
**Mains hook:** NALSA's self-identity baseline is the reference point for any 2026 privacy analysis.
**Back:** *"Fourteen declared the self; nineteen wrote it into law."*

### Flashcard SJ-2144 — Transgender person: definition under the 2019 Act
**Front:** How does the 2019 Act define a transgender person?
**Definition (learn):** A person whose gender does not match the gender assigned at birth, including trans-men, trans-women, intersex variations, genderqueer persons and socio-cultural identities such as kinnar, hijra, aravani and jogta.
**Breakdown:**
- Broad 2019 definition (✅ owner 13; ✅ PRS).
- Socio-cultural identities: kinnar, hijra, aravani, jogta (✅ owner 13; ✅ PRS).
- Intersex variations (✅ PRS).
- Note: trans-men, trans-women and genderqueer are removed by the 2026 Amendment (SJ-2152).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Definition under 2019 Act (✅ owner 13).
**Links:** SJ-2145, SJ-2152, SJ-2147
**Trap:** Using the 2026 narrowed definition for the 2019 Act's original scope.
**Mains hook:** Definitions decide who is protected; narrowing changes eligibility. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Gender mismatch, broad circle; the 2026 circle shrinks."*

### Flashcard SJ-2145 — Self-perceived identity: 2019 Section 4(2) and its 2026 status
**Front:** What did Section 4(2) of the 2019 Act provide, and what does the owner say the 2026 Amendment did to it?
**Definition (learn):** Section 4(2) gave a recognised transgender person a right to self-perceived gender identity; the owner says the 2026 Amendment deleted it.
**Breakdown:**
- 2019 Act Section 4(2): right to self-perceived gender identity (✅ owner 13 basic table).
- 2026 Amendment: owner says deleted (✅ owner 13); PRS and Wikipedia do not confirm the deletion by section number (⚠️).
- Medical-board gate replaces self-declaration for certificate (SJ-2153).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Section 4(2) (✅ owner 13); deletion ⚠️ unverified by fetched source.
**Links:** SJ-2144, SJ-2153, SJ-2158
**Trap:** Saying the 2026 Act still guarantees self-perceived identity (✅ owner 13 trap; ⚠️ on deletion detail).
**Mains hook:** Self-identification versus medical verification is the central constitutional question. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Four-two gave the self; the 2026 gate asks a board."*

### Flashcard SJ-2146 — District Magistrate certificate and the revised certificate
**Front:** Who issues the transgender certificate of identity, and when is a revised certificate issued?
**Definition (learn):** The District Magistrate issues the certificate under the 2019 Act; a revised male or female certificate follows gender-change surgery.
**Breakdown:**
- Authority: District Magistrate (✅ owner 13; ✅ Wikipedia).
- Basic certificate: no surgery required (✅ owner 13).
- Revised certificate: after surgery, optional "may" under 2019 Act (✅ owner 13; ✅ PRS).
- Section 7 (owner) for revised certificate (✅ owner 13 advanced; section ⚠️ not fetched).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** DM; optional revision under 2019 (✅ owner 13).
**Links:** SJ-2145, SJ-2153, SJ-2154, SJ-2160
**Trap:** Requiring surgery for the basic certificate.
**Mains hook:** Certificate gates shape access to rights. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"District issues the basic; surgery earns the revised."*

### Flashcard SJ-2147 — Transgender Persons (Protection of Rights) Act, 2019: enactment timeline
**Front:** What is the 2019 Act's passage, assent and commencement timeline?
**Definition (learn):** The statute that gave statutory recognition and anti-discrimination protection to transgender persons.
**Breakdown:**
- Lok Sabha passed 5 Aug 2019 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Rajya Sabha passed 26 Nov 2019, no amendments (✅ Wikipedia).
- Presidential assent 5 Dec 2019; Act No. 40 of 2019 (✅ Wikipedia).
- In force 10 Jan 2020 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Draft rules 18 Apr 2020 (✅ Wikipedia); final Rules year ⚠️ (owner says Rules 2020).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 5 Dec 2019 assent; 10 Jan 2020 in force (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2148, SJ-2149, SJ-2142
**Trap:** Dating the Act to 2020 because it came into force then; it was enacted in 2019.
**Mains hook:** A 2019 law entered force in 2020, then was amended in 2026. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Five December enacted, ten January in force."*

### Flashcard SJ-2148 — 2019 Act: offences and penalties (six months to two years)
**Front:** What penalty applies to the original 2019 Act offences?
**Definition (learn):** Imprisonment of six months to two years plus fine for listed offences such as forced labour, denial of public access, forced eviction and harm.
**Breakdown:**
- Penalty: six months to two years and fine (✅ PRS; ✅ Wikipedia).
- Offences: forced or bonded labour, denial of public places, forced eviction from residence, harm or injury (✅ owner 13).
- Sexual-abuse punishment noted by critics as lower than for women (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Six months to two years (✅ PRS).
**Links:** SJ-2155, SJ-2147
**Trap:** Treating 2019 penalties as the 2026 penalties.
**Mains hook:** Penalty scale signals how seriously the State treats identity-based violence. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Six months to two years, the first penalty tier."*

### Flashcard SJ-2149 — National Council for Transgender Persons
**Front:** What is the National Council for Transgender Persons?
**Definition (learn):** An advisory body created by the 2019 Act, chaired by the Union Minister for Social Justice.
**Breakdown:**
- Created by the 2019 Act (✅ Wikipedia).
- Chaired by the Union Minister for Social Justice (✅ owner 13).
- Advisory; no quasi-judicial powers (✅ owner 13 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Advisory (✅ owner 13 advanced).
**Links:** SJ-2165, SJ-2147
**Trap:** Calling it a statutory commission with enforcement powers.
**Mains hook:** Advisory bodies lack teeth; enforcement needs complaints machinery. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Advisory council, minister in the chair."*

### Flashcard SJ-2150 — 2026 Amendment Act: bill timeline and Presidential assent
**Front:** What is the 2026 Amendment's bill-to-assent timeline?
**Definition (learn):** The Transgender Persons (Protection of Rights) Amendment Act, 2026 passed both Houses in March 2026 and received assent on 30 March 2026.
**Breakdown:**
- Introduced in Lok Sabha: 13 Mar 2026 (✅ PRS; ✅ Wikipedia).
- Lok Sabha passed 24 Mar 2026 by voice vote (✅ PRS; ✅ Wikipedia).
- Rajya Sabha passed 25 Mar 2026 (✅ PRS; ✅ Wikipedia).
- Presidential assent 30 Mar 2026; Act No. 3 of 2026 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 13, 24, 25, 30 Mar 2026 (✅ PRS; ✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2151, SJ-2152, SJ-2156
**Trap:** Treating the bill stage as the enacted law.
**Mains hook:** Rapid passage without Select Committee reference is a process point. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Thirteen, twenty-four, twenty-five, thirty: March two-thousand-twenty-six."*

### Flashcard SJ-2151 — 2026 Amendment: commencement is a source conflict
**Front:** Is the 2026 Amendment operative, and from which date?
**Definition (learn):** The owner says it came into force on 25 May 2026 under S.O. 2620(E) dated 22 May 2026; the fetched Wikipedia page says "not yet in force"; the PRS page gives no commencement information.
**Breakdown:**
- Owner: commencement notification S.O. 2620(E), 22 May 2026; in force 25 May 2026 (✅ owner 13 advanced; gazette not fetched).
- Wikipedia: "Not yet in force" (✅ Wikipedia; page may lag).
- PRS: no assent, Act number or commencement info (✅ PRS).
- Resolution: verify the Gazette before stating operative status (⚠️).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 25 May 2026 (⚠️ owner-only; conflicting fetched source).
**Links:** SJ-2150, SJ-2156
**Trap:** Stating commencement with certainty; the conflict must be shown.
**Mains hook:** Say "enacted; commencement to be verified in the Gazette" if unverified. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Enacted in March; in force, the Gazette must say."*

### Flashcard SJ-2152 — 2026 Amendment: narrowed definition and the eunuch addition
**Front:** How does the 2026 Amendment change the definition of transgender person?
**Definition (learn):** It keeps socio-cultural identity and intersex persons, removes trans-men, trans-women and genderqueer persons, adds "eunuch", and adds persons forced into transgender identity by mutilation or procedures.
**Breakdown:**
- Retained: socio-cultural identities and persons with variations in sex characteristics at birth (✅ PRS).
- Removed: trans-men, trans-women, genderqueer persons (✅ PRS; ✅ owner 13).
- Added: eunuch; person forced into transgender identity by mutilation, emasculation, castration or procedures (✅ PRS).
- Excludes different sexual orientations and self-perceived sexual identities (✅ PRS).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Narrowed definition (✅ PRS).
**Links:** SJ-2144, SJ-2145, SJ-2155
**Trap:** Saying the definition was simply broadened or retained.
**Mains hook:** Narrowing removes self-identifying persons from baseline protection. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Trans-men and trans-women out; eunuch and forced transition in."*

### Flashcard SJ-2153 — 2026 Amendment: the designated medical board gate
**Front:** What changed in certification after the 2026 Amendment?
**Definition (learn):** The District Magistrate issues the certificate only after a designated medical board recommends it; a name change in official documents follows.
**Breakdown:**
- Designated medical board headed by Chief or Deputy Chief Medical Officer (✅ PRS; ✅ Wikipedia).
- DM may consult other medical experts (✅ PRS).
- Earlier: self-perceived identity with no medical test (✅ owner 13; ✅ PRS).
- First-name change on birth certificate and official documents (✅ PRS).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Medical board gate (✅ PRS).
**Links:** SJ-2145, SJ-2146, SJ-2157
**Trap:** Thinking the DM alone decides after a self-declaration.
**Mains hook:** A medical gate contests the NALSA psychological test. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Board first, district second, name last."*

### Flashcard SJ-2154 — 2026 Amendment: mandatory revised certificate and hospital reporting
**Front:** What does the 2026 Amendment require after gender-change surgery?
**Definition (learn):** A revised male or female certificate becomes mandatory, and the treating institution must report surgery details to the DM and medical authority.
**Breakdown:**
- Under 2019: "may" obtain a revised certificate (✅ PRS).
- Under 2026: "shall" obtain it (✅ PRS).
- Hospital reporting to DM and medical board (✅ PRS; ✅ Wikipedia).
- Owner: personal-data reporting obligation (✅ owner 13).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Mandatory (✅ PRS).
**Links:** SJ-2146, SJ-2157
**Trap:** Thinking the revised certificate is still optional.
**Mains hook:** Mandatory reporting creates a new flow of sensitive health data. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Surgery now shall, and the hospital tells."*

### Flashcard SJ-2155 — 2026 Amendment: new offences and penalties
**Front:** What penalties does the 2026 Amendment set for forcing a transgender identity and for related exploitation?
**Definition (learn):** Kidnapping or grievous hurt to force transgender identity is punished by 10 years to life and a minimum ₹2 lakh fine for adults, and by life with a minimum ₹5 lakh fine for children. A second offence set covers begging, servitude or bonded labour.
**Breakdown:**
- Adult victim, kidnapping or grievous hurt: 10 years to life; minimum ₹2 lakh (✅ PRS; ✅ owner 13 basic).
- Child victim: life; minimum ₹5 lakh (✅ PRS).
- Second set, adult victim: 5 to 10 years; minimum ₹1 lakh (✅ PRS; not in owner).
- Second set, child victim: 10 to 14 years; minimum ₹3 lakh (✅ PRS; not in owner).
- Offences described as cognizable and non-bailable (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Penalties are from the bill tracker (✅ PRS); enacted text ⚠️ not verified.
**Links:** SJ-2148, SJ-2151, SJ-2157
**Trap:** The owner's table omits the second offence set; include it for the enacted text after checking the Gazette.
**Mains hook:** Penal strengthening against forced transition is the government's anti-misuse argument. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Ten to life for forcing; five to ten for exploiting."*

### Flashcard SJ-2156 — 2026 Amendment: Supreme Court challenge status
**Front:** What is the status of the constitutional challenge to the 2026 Amendment?
**Definition (learn):** Petitions under Article 32 challenge the amendment; the Court issued notice and refused interim relief for now. The Act's validity is pending, not decided.
**Breakdown:**
- Petitions: nine Article 32 petitions (✅ Wikipedia).
- Notices issued to Centre and States on 4 May 2026 (✅ Wikipedia).
- No interim stay; refused for now (✅ Wikipedia; owner 13).
- Owner: Laxmi Narayan Tripathi petition filed 24 Apr 2026; referred to a three-judge Bench (✅ owner 13; ⚠️ Bench referral not confirmed by Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 4 May 2026 notice (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-2151, SJ-2158, SJ-2159
**Trap:** Saying the amendment was struck down or stayed (SJ-2159).
**Mains hook:** Present both readings and flag sub judice status. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Notice issued, no stay, verdict pending."*

### Flashcard SJ-2157 — Article 21 privacy and the 2026 Amendment: three Puttaswamy facets
**Front:** Which three privacy facets does the owner use to analyse the 2026 Amendment?
**Definition (learn):** Decisional autonomy, informational privacy and bodily autonomy, drawn from *K.S. Puttaswamy* (2017) and applied to the amendment's provisions.
**Breakdown:**
- Decisional autonomy: deletion of self-identity right and medical-board gate (✅ owner 13 advanced).
- Informational privacy: hospital-to-DM reporting of surgery details (✅ owner 13 advanced; ✅ PRS).
- Bodily autonomy: mandatory revised certificate after surgery (✅ owner 13 advanced; ✅ PRS).
- *Puttaswamy* (2017) (✅ owner 13).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Puttaswamy 2017 (✅ owner 13).
**Links:** SJ-2143, SJ-2153, SJ-2154, SJ-2170
**Trap:** Merging informational privacy with the right to self-identify; they are two facets.
**Mains hook:** Engage each facet separately for a higher-mark answer. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Decide, inform, embody: three privacy doors."*

### Flashcard SJ-2158 — 2026 GS-II Q1: the 10-mark answer route
**Front:** How should the 2026 GS-II Q1 (privacy and the 2026 amendment) be answered in 150 words?
**Definition (learn):** State the NALSA and Article 21 baseline, name the changes, show the twin privacy and exclusion effects, note the competing justification, and close with the pending challenge.
**Breakdown:**
- Q1, 2026 GS-II, Examine, 10 marks, 150 words (✅ owner 13 PYQ).
- (1) NALSA baseline; (2) 2026 changes; (3) privacy and exclusion effects; (4) anti-misuse justification; (5) pending challenge (✅ owner 13 answer spine).
- Do not substitute the earlier "no direct PYQ" framing (✅ owner 13).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2026 GS-II Q1 (✅ owner 13; OCR scan not held here).
**Links:** SJ-2143, SJ-2145, SJ-2152, SJ-2153, SJ-2154, SJ-2157
**Trap:** A list of provisions without the privacy analysis.
**Mains hook:** Give both sides and a qualified close. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Baseline, change, twin effect, justification, pending."*

### Flashcard SJ-2159 — Trap: the 2026 Amendment has been struck down or stayed
**Front:** Has the Supreme Court struck down or stayed the 2026 Amendment?
**Definition (learn):** No, as of the last verified hearing. The Court declined interim relief and issued notice; the challenge is pending.
**Breakdown:**
- Interim relief refused for now (✅ Wikipedia).
- Notices issued 4 May 2026 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Pending adjudication (✅ owner 13).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Status as of May 2026 (✅ Wikipedia; ⚠️ time-varying).
**Links:** SJ-2156
**Trap:** "Struck down" and "stayed" are the distractors.
**Mains hook:** Say "sub judice" for a pending challenge.
**Back:** *"Notice, not nullity: the Act stands pending."*

### Flashcard SJ-2160 — Trap: surgery is required for basic gender recognition
**Front:** Does the 2019 Act require surgery for basic recognition?
**Definition (learn):** No. The basic DM certificate rests on self-perceived identity; a revised male or female certificate is a separate post-intervention route.
**Breakdown:**
- Basic certificate: no surgery (✅ owner 13).
- Revised certificate: after medical intervention (✅ PRS; ✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2019 Act (✅ owner 13).
**Links:** SJ-2146
**Trap:** Confusing basic and revised certificates.
**Mains hook:** Keep the two routes distinct.
**Back:** *"Basic is self, revised is surgical."*

### Flashcard SJ-2161 — Criminal Tribes Act, 1871 and the denotified communities (1952)
**Front:** What did the Criminal Tribes Act, 1871 do, and what changed in 1952?
**Definition (learn):** The colonial Act stigmatised entire communities as criminal, subjecting them to registration, surveillance and movement restriction; it was repealed in 1952, creating "denotified" communities.
**Breakdown:**
- 1871 colonial law, "born criminals" framing (✅ owner 13).
- Repealed 1952 (✅ owner 13).
- Stigma survived repeal; documentation and invisibility persisted (✅ owner 13).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1871; 1952 (✅ owner 13).
**Links:** SJ-2162, SJ-2163, SJ-2164, SJ-2168
**Trap:** Thinking denotification declared the communities innocent (SJ-2162).
**Mains hook:** Historical stigma explains current invisibility; trace it before discussing welfare. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Seventy-one branded, fifty-two unbranded but unseen."*

### Flashcard SJ-2162 — Trap: "denotified" versus "decriminalised"
**Front:** Does "denotified" mean the communities were declared innocent?
**Definition (learn):** No. Denotification repealed the statutory label in 1952 but gave no rehabilitation or remedy for wrongful conviction and did not remove stigma.
**Breakdown:**
- Denotified: label removed (✅ owner 13 advanced).
- Decriminalised: would imply wrongful-conviction remedy, which did not occur (✅ owner 13 advanced).
- Stigma persists (✅ owner 13).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1952 (✅ owner 13).
**Links:** SJ-2161
**Trap:** The distractor "declared innocent".
**Mains hook:** Repeal removes law, not stigma.
**Back:** *"Denotified is delabelled, not declared innocent."*

### Flashcard SJ-2163 — Idate Commission (2015): DNT recommendations
**Front:** What did the Idate Commission (2015) recommend for DNT communities?
**Definition (learn):** The National Commission for De-notified, Nomadic and Semi-Nomadic Tribes, chaired by Bhiku Ramji Idate, recommended a permanent statutory commission, a comprehensive central DNT list and targeted welfare.
**Breakdown:**
- Year: 2015 (✅ owner 13).
- Chair: Bhiku Ramji Idate (✅ owner 13).
- Recommendations: statutory commission, central DNT list, welfare schemes (✅ owner 13).
- Statutory commission not established (✅ owner 13 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2015 (✅ owner 13).
**Links:** SJ-2164, SJ-2165, SJ-2168
**Trap:** Saying Idate's commission was established as a statutory body.
**Mains hook:** Recommendations unimplemented are a governance gap. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Idate asked for a statute; the board stayed advisory."*

### Flashcard SJ-2164 — Renke Commission (2008): nomadic and denotified tribes
**Front:** What did the Renke Commission (2008) examine?
**Definition (learn):** A 2008 commission on denotified, nomadic and semi-nomadic communities that documented historical stigma and welfare invisibility.
**Breakdown:**
- Year: 2008 (✅ owner 13).
- Findings: stigma and invisibility; weak enumeration (✅ owner 13).
- Named evidence for DNT answers (✅ owner 13 answer architecture).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2008 (✅ owner 13).
**Links:** SJ-2163, SJ-2168
**Trap:** Confusing Renke (2008) with Idate (2015).
**Mains hook:** Use Renke and Idate as sequential evidence. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Oh-eight Renke saw, fifteen Idate asked."*

### Flashcard SJ-2165 — DWBDNC (21 February 2019): advisory board, not a commission
**Front:** What is the DWBDNC, and why is it not a statutory commission?
**Definition (learn):** The Development and Welfare Board for De-notified, Nomadic and Semi-Nomadic Communities, constituted under MoSJE on 21 February 2019, coordinates welfare, surveys and policy advice.
**Breakdown:**
- Constituted 21 Feb 2019 (✅ owner 13).
- Advisory Board under MoSJE (✅ owner 13).
- Not a statutory commission; no quasi-judicial powers (✅ owner 13 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 21 Feb 2019 (✅ owner 13).
**Links:** SJ-2163, SJ-2166, SJ-2149
**Trap:** DWBDNC as a statutory commission with binding powers.
**Mains hook:** Advisory bodies recommend; the State must act. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Nineteen-February board, advice only."*

### Flashcard SJ-2166 — SEED (16 February 2022): DNT economic empowerment
**Front:** What does SEED provide, and when was it launched?
**Definition (learn):** The Scheme for Economic Empowerment of DNT Communities, launched 16 February 2022, providing coaching, health-insurance facilitation, livelihood and housing or land support.
**Breakdown:**
- Launch: 16 Feb 2022 (✅ owner 13).
- Components: free coaching, health-insurance facilitation, community livelihood, housing and land (✅ owner 13).
- Coverage nascent; not a substitute for documents (✅ owner 13 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 16 Feb 2022 (✅ owner 13).
**Links:** SJ-2165, SJ-2168
**Trap:** SEED as a comprehensive DNT list or census (SJ-2168).
**Mains hook:** Dedicated schemes can create silos; mainstream where feasible. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Twenty-two SEED: coach, insure, livelihood, house."*

### Flashcard SJ-2167 — SMILE: transgender welfare and begging rehabilitation sub-schemes
**Front:** What are the two sub-schemes of SMILE, and what does Garima Greh provide?
**Definition (learn):** SMILE (MoSJE) has a transgender welfare sub-scheme and a begging rehabilitation sub-scheme; Garima Greh shelters provide accommodation, food, medical care and skill training.
**Breakdown:**
- Transgender welfare: Garima Greh, skill development, health and counselling (✅ owner 13).
- Begging rehabilitation: distinct target group (✅ owner 13).
- Garima Greh: shelter with food, medical care, skills, recreation (✅ owner 13).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Two sub-schemes (✅ owner 13).
**Links:** SJ-2149, SJ-2166
**Trap:** SMILE is not only for transgender persons (SJ-2168).
**Mains hook:** Shelters address immediate need; livelihood needs longer linkage. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Garima Greh shelters, two streams: welfare and rehabilitation."*

### Flashcard SJ-2168 — DNT, NT and SNT: definitions and the absent central list
**Front:** How are denotified, nomadic and semi-nomadic tribes defined, and does a central list exist?
**Definition (learn):** Denotified Tribes were freed from the Criminal Tribes label; Nomadic Tribes lack fixed habitation; Semi-Nomadic Tribes are partially settled. No statutory central list exists.
**Breakdown:**
- DNT: denotified after the 1952 repeal (✅ owner 13).
- NT: without fixed habitation, seasonal movement (✅ owner 13).
- SNT: partially settled (✅ owner 13).
- No central statutory list; State lists vary (✅ owner 13; ✅ owner 13 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** No central statutory list (✅ owner 13).
**Links:** SJ-2161, SJ-2163, SJ-2166, SJ-2167
**Trap:** A comprehensive central DNT list exists (✅ owner 13 trap says no).
**Mains hook:** Without a central list, eligibility fragments across States. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Denotified, nomadic, semi-settled: no central list yet."*

### Flashcard SJ-2169 — Census 2011 "other" category as a transgender anchor
**Front:** What does the Census 2011 "other" sex category count, and why is it an incomplete anchor?
**Definition (learn):** Census 2011 recorded 4,87,803 people in the residual "other" sex category, often used as a transgender-population anchor.
**Breakdown:**
- Count: 4,87,803 (✅ owner 13).
- Limits: some transgender persons recorded as male or female; category not identity-perfect (✅ owner 13).
- Use only with caveats (✅ owner 13).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 4,87,803 (✅ owner 13).
**Links:** SJ-2144, SJ-2168
**Trap:** Quoting 4,87,803 as the transgender population.
**Mains hook:** Enumeration gaps limit targeting. ⚠️ Evaluative.
**Back:** *"Four lakh eighty-seven thousand is a floor, not a head count."*

### Flashcard SJ-2170 — Advanced: psychological test versus medical gate-keeping
**Front:** What is the doctrinal reversal test the owner proposes for the 2026 Amendment?
**Definition (learn):** Whether conditioning legal recognition on medical-board approval revives the "biological test" NALSA rejected, or is a permissible verification mechanism.
**Breakdown:**
- NALSA: psychological test (SJ-2143).
- 2019 Act: self-perceived right (SJ-2145).
- 2026 Amendment: medical board gate (SJ-2153).
- Two readings: autonomy violation (petitioners) versus regulatory legitimacy (Union) (✅ owner 13 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner analysis (⚠️ inference).
**Links:** SJ-2143, SJ-2153, SJ-2157
**Trap:** Declaring that the Supreme Court has already ruled on this question.
**Mains hook:** Present both readings; the Court has not decided. ⚠️ Analytical.
**Back:** *"Psychological self or biological board: the Court decides."*

### Flashcard SJ-2171 — Advanced: advisory boards, invisibility and the documentation chain
**Front:** What is the documentation-invisibility chain for DNT communities?
**Definition (learn):** Lack of a central list and fixed domicile blocks ration, Aadhaar-seeding and caste certificates, which excludes families from schemes such as PM-JAY, NFSA and PM-KISAN, while the DWBDNC can only recommend.
**Breakdown:**
- No central statutory list (SJ-2168).
- Domicile barrier: fixed address needed for documents (✅ owner 13 advanced).
- Welfare exclusion: PM-JAY, NFSA, PM-KISAN access blocked (✅ owner 13 advanced).
- DWBDNC is advisory; Idate's statutory commission not enacted (SJ-2165, SJ-2163).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner's chain (⚠️ analysis).
**Links:** SJ-2168, SJ-2165, SJ-2163, SJ-2142
**Trap:** Claiming DNT families are absent from every list; the owner says the position varies by State.
**Mains hook:** Documentation camps and a central list would unlock welfare. ⚠️ Owner recommendation.
**Back:** *"No papers, no list, no scheme, no voice."*

## SOCIAL JUSTICE — Part 3: Sanitation Workers, Labour and Social Security, Urban Poor and Scheme Architecture

### GROUP — Sanitation, Manual Scavenging and Safai Karamcharis (owners 14, basic and advanced)

### Flashcard SJ-3001 — Sanitation labour: the four-lever framework
**Front:** Name the four levers needed to end manual scavenging and sewer deaths, and say which instrument carries each.
**Definition (learn):** Ending manual scavenging needs four complementary levers: prohibition (criminal law), mechanisation (removing human entry), rehabilitation (alternative livelihoods) and compensation (payment after death or injury).
**Breakdown:**
- Prohibition → PEMSR Act, 2013 (offence; identification duty).
- Mechanisation → NAMASTE scheme, 2022-23 (PPE, training, mechanised equipment, capital subsidy).
- Rehabilitation → SRMS legacy and NAMASTE livelihood support.
- Compensation → Supreme Court directions in Dr Balram Singh v UoI (2023), not a statutory schedule.
- Caste-labour nexus makes the issue one of recognitive justice, not only occupational safety (✅ owner 14).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 14 basic, section 1 and Core proposition (✅ owner 14).
**Links:** SJ-3002, SJ-3008, SJ-3007, SJ-3009, SJ-3015.
**Trap:** Treating the four levers as one scheme. NAMASTE does not replace the PEMSR Act; compensation tiers are not in the Act.
**Mains hook:** "Eradicating manual scavenging needs prohibition with enforcement teeth, mechanisation, durable rehabilitation and fair compensation; without the caste dimension, the reform is incomplete." Conclude that the four levers must operate together.
**Back:** *"P-M-R-C: Prohibit, Mechanise, Rehabilitate, Compensate."*

### Flashcard SJ-3002 — Prohibition of Employment as Manual Scavengers and their Rehabilitation Act, 2013 (PEMSR Act)
**Front:** What does the PEMSR Act, 2013 prohibit, and what duties does it place on the State?
**Definition (learn):** Central statute that prohibits employing people as manual scavengers and hazardous manual cleaning of sewers and septic tanks, requires identification and rehabilitation, and creates offences with imprisonment and fines.
**Breakdown:**
- Prohibits employment for manual scavenging and hazardous cleaning.
- Prohibits construction of insanitary latrines (owner 14).
- Requires survey and identification of manual scavengers (District Magistrates and local authorities coordinate).
- Creates criminal offences; state governments implement.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Passed and notified September 2013; Rules (M.S. Rules 2013) formulated December 2013 (✅ Wikipedia: PEMSR Act). Supreme Court disposed of WP (C) 583 of 2003 on 27 March 2014 with directions (✅ Wikipedia). Subsequent-offence imprisonment up to 5 years (✅ owner 14 advanced; fine amounts ⚠️ not verified).
**Links:** SJ-3003, SJ-3004, SJ-3006, SJ-3012, SJ-3014.
**Trap:** Confusing the Act with the NAMASTE scheme or with the Balram Singh compensation directions. The Act is the prohibition statute.
**Mains hook:** Examine the Act's provisions and implementation: identification and rehabilitation duties exist, but deaths in septic-tank cleaning show prosecution and inspection gaps. Qualify with enforcement evidence.
**Back:** *"Ban the job, count the workers, rehab the person."*

### Flashcard SJ-3003 — Manual scavenging (statutory definition)
**Front:** Define manual scavenging under the PEMSR Act, 2013.
**Definition (learn):** Engaging or employing a person to manually clean, carry, dispose of or otherwise handle human excreta in insanitary latrines, open drains, pits or railway tracks before it fully decomposes.
**Breakdown:**
- Covered employer: individual, local authority, agency or contractor (✅ Wikipedia: PEMSR definition).
- Settings: insanitary latrines, open drains, pits, railway tracks (✅ owner 14 and Wikipedia).
- The definition excludes work done with protective gear and equipment notified by the Union government (✅ Wikipedia wording); owner 14 states that protective equipment does not legalise manual scavenging. Reconcile with the statute text before quoting.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Defined in the 2013 Act (✅ Wikipedia). Exact clause number ⚠️ not verified.
**Links:** SJ-3004, SJ-3005, SJ-3002, SJ-3012.
**Trap:** Saying PPE makes manual scavenging lawful. The owner's position is that PPE does not legalise manual scavenging; the notified-gear wording in the definition must be read in full.
**Mains hook:** Use the definition to show why the prohibition is legal, not only social: the trigger is the human handling of excreta in specified settings, with contractors and local authorities covered.
**Back:** *"Hands in the excreta, in the listed settings, is the crime."*

### Flashcard SJ-3004 — Hazardous cleaning (distinct from manual scavenging)
**Front:** How does hazardous cleaning differ from manual scavenging under the PEMSR Act?
**Definition (learn):** Manual cleaning of sewers, septic tanks or specified spaces without the prescribed protective gear, cleaning devices and safety precautions. Both practices are prohibited but defined separately.
**Breakdown:**
- Manual scavenging → excreta in insanitary latrines, drains, pits, tracks.
- Hazardous cleaning → sewers and septic tanks cleaned without statutory safety equipment and precautions.
- Both prohibited; separate definitions (✅ owner 14).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 14 section 2 (✅ owner 14). Statutory wording ⚠️ check against India Code text.
**Links:** SJ-3003, SJ-3005, SJ-3002.
**Trap:** "Manual scavenging and hazardous cleaning are the same." The 2013 Act treats them as two prohibited activities.
**Mains hook:** A question on sewer deaths should be classified first as hazardous cleaning, then traced to the employer or contractor and the safety-equipment gap.
**Back:** *"Manhole = hazard; latrine pit = scavenging."*

### Flashcard SJ-3005 — Insanitary latrine
**Front:** What is an insanitary latrine, and why does its construction matter legally?
**Definition (learn):** A latrine that needs human intervention to remove excreta, as distinct from a water-flush or septic-connected toilet. Construction is prohibited under the 2013 Act.
**Breakdown:**
- Requires manual removal of excreta.
- Water-flush or septic-connected toilets fall outside the definition (✅ owner 14).
- Construction of insanitary latrines is prohibited (✅ owner 14 basic, section 2).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 14 (✅ owner 14). Statutory section ⚠️ not verified.
**Links:** SJ-3003, SJ-3002, SJ-3021.
**Trap:** Thinking that all toilets are insanitary latrines. The category is defined by the manual-removal requirement, not by the toilet's existence.
**Mains hook:** Link toilet construction to safe containment: open-defecation-free status does not guarantee the absence of manual removal.
**Back:** *"Needs a human hand to empty it = insanitary."*

### Flashcard SJ-3006 — Safai Karamchari Andolan v Union of India (2014)
**Front:** Which Supreme Court judgment reinforced identification, rehabilitation and mechanisation, and what compensation baseline did it set?
**Definition (learn):** The Supreme Court's 2014 decision in writ petition (C) 583 of 2003, which gave directions on the identification and rehabilitation of manual scavengers and a compensation baseline for sewer deaths.
**Breakdown:**
- Case: Safai Karamchari Andolan v UoI (✅ Wikipedia: order dated 27 March 2014).
- Gave directions for identification, rehabilitation and mechanisation (✅ owner 14).
- Established the earlier ₹10 lakh compensation baseline (✅ owner 14; amount not separately verified).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Order 27 March 2014 (✅ Wikipedia). ₹10 lakh baseline (✅ owner 14).
**Links:** SJ-3007, SJ-3002.
**Trap:** Crediting the 2014 case with the ₹30 lakh figure. That is the 2023 Balram Singh order.
**Mains hook:** Trace the judicial trajectory: 2014 baseline, then 2023 enhancement, to show courts filling gaps that statute and administration left open.
**Back:** *"Two-thousand-fourteen sets the floor; twenty-twenty-three raises it."*

### Flashcard SJ-3007 — Dr Balram Singh v Union of India (20 October 2023) compensation tiers
**Front:** What compensation tiers did the Supreme Court prescribe for sewer and septic-tank deaths and injuries in 2023?
**Definition (learn):** A Supreme Court direction raising sewer-death compensation to ₹30 lakh, with at least ₹20 lakh for permanent disability and ₹10 lakh for other injury, alongside mechanisation and accountability directions.
**Breakdown:**
- Death during sewer/septic-tank cleaning → ₹30 lakh.
- Permanent disability → at least ₹20 lakh.
- Other injury → ₹10 lakh.
- Judicial directions, not a PEMSR statutory schedule (✅ owner 14).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Judgment date 20 October 2023 (✅ owner 14 basic and advanced; not verified against a fetched source).
**Links:** SJ-3006, SJ-3002, SJ-3001.
**Trap:** "Compensation tiers are in the PEMSR Act." They are court directions; check disbursal and the source separately.
**Mains hook:** Compensation after death does not stop deaths. A sound answer treats the 2023 tiers as a remedy layer that complements prohibition and mechanisation.
**Back:** *"Thirty, twenty, ten: death, disability, injury, by court order."*

### Flashcard SJ-3008 — NAMASTE scheme (2022-23)
**Front:** What is NAMASTE, who runs it, and what components does it cover?
**Definition (learn):** National Action for Mechanised Sanitation Ecosystem: a joint MoSJE and MoHUA scheme for profiling sanitation workers, safety training and equipment, mechanised sanitation access, livelihood support and convergence with social-security schemes.
**Breakdown:**
- Ministries: Ministry of Social Justice and Empowerment (MoSJE) and Ministry of Housing and Urban Affairs (MoHUA).
- Components: worker profiling, PPE and training, mechanised equipment, capital subsidy for worker-entrepreneurs, livelihood convergence (✅ owner 14).
- Builds on and supplements SRMS (✅ owner 14).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Launched 2022-23 (✅ owner 14). Implementing portal ✅ owner 14 (nskfdc.nic.in link cited; not fetched). Dashboard totals are time-varying and not cited here.
**Links:** SJ-3009, SJ-3016, SJ-3001.
**Trap:** NAMASTE replaces the PEMSR Act. It is a scheme for mechanisation and rehabilitation; prohibition stays with the Act.
**Mains hook:** Mechanisation can reduce human entry only if procurement, operator training and maintenance follow. Otherwise machines sit idle and workers still enter sewers.
**Back:** *"NAMASTE equips; PEMSR forbids."*

### Flashcard SJ-3009 — SRMS (Self-Employment Scheme for Rehabilitation of Manual Scavengers)
**Front:** What is SRMS, and how does it relate to NAMASTE?
**Definition (learn):** The earlier rehabilitation scheme for manual scavengers that NAMASTE builds upon, providing one-time cash and livelihood support to identified manual scavengers.
**Breakdown:**
- Predates the 2013 Act (✅ Wikipedia: 2007 Self Employment Scheme).
- Provides livelihood and rehabilitation support; NAMASTE extends to sanitation workers with mechanisation (✅ owner 14).
- A portal listing is not proof that a household received a livelihood alternative (✅ owner 14).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2007 launch (✅ Wikipedia). Components beyond "livelihood support" not verified.
**Links:** SJ-3008, SJ-3002, SJ-3017.
**Trap:** Thinking that SRMS is a 2013-Act scheme. It came first, and the 2013 Act followed.
**Mains hook:** A rehabilitation scheme that pays once without durable alternative work can push workers back into sanitation; show the sequencing gap.
**Back:** *"Oh-seven cash, then NAMASTE builds the machine."*

### Flashcard SJ-3010 — National Commission for Safai Karamcharis (NCSK): status and tenure
**Front:** What is the NCSK's legal basis and current tenure, and what does the source conflict look like?
**Definition (learn):** A commission set up under the NCSK Act, 1993, now non-statutory and continued by government resolution rather than by statute.
**Breakdown:**
- Constituted 12 August 1994 under the NCSK Act, 1993 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Statutory tenure lapsed February 2004 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Revived by executive extensions; owner 14 states a Cabinet/Ministry extension in February 2025 runs through 31 March 2028 (✅ owner 14, not fetched).
- Wikipedia says the most recent extension it cites ended 31 March 2022 (✅ Wikipedia, possibly stale).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 12 August 1994; February 2004; 31 March 2028 (owner); 31 March 2022 (Wikipedia). ⚠️ Conflict: owner tenure date and Wikipedia's last-extension date differ; treat 31 March 2028 as owner-stated and unverified against a primary order.
**Links:** SJ-3011, SJ-3014, SJ-3022.
**Trap:** "NCSK is a permanent statutory body." It is time-bound and non-statutory in current form.
**Mains hook:** The commission's resolution-based life shows institutional under-investment. Qualify the answer with the date of the latest order.
**Back:** *"Statute sunset in 2004; resolution keeps it breathing."*

### Flashcard SJ-3011 — NCSK versus NCSC and NCST (constitutional bodies)
**Front:** Why is NCSK not equal to NCSC or NCST, and which Articles give the latter constitutional status?
**Definition (learn):** NCSC and NCST are constitutional bodies under Articles 338 and 338A; NCSK has no constitutional backing and is a resolution-continued body.
**Breakdown:**
- NCSC → Article 338 (✅ owner 14 advanced).
- NCST → Article 338A (✅ owner 14 advanced).
- NCSK → Act 1993 origin, statutory tenure lapsed, now resolution-continued (✅ owner 14, Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Articles 338, 338A (owner-cited; standard constitutional knowledge ⚠️ not fetched).
**Links:** SJ-3010, SJ-3012.
**Trap:** Option pairs that list NCSK among constitutional commissions. Only NCSC and NCST carry Article-based status.
**Mains hook:** Argue that a constitutional or statutory guarantee would give the sanitation-worker commission independence and enforcement teeth; current form does not.
**Back:** *"Articles 338-338A for SC/ST; NCSK rides a resolution."*

### Flashcard SJ-3012 — Penalty comparison: 1993 Act versus 2013 Act (trap)
**Front:** What was the punishment under the earlier 1993 Act, and how does the 2013 Act tighten it?
**Definition (learn):** The 1993 manual-scavenging law carried weak penalties; the PEMSR Act, 2013 created stronger offences with imprisonment and fines.
**Breakdown:**
- 1993 Act: imprisonment up to one year and/or a fine of ₹2,000 (✅ Wikipedia).
- 2013 Act: imprisonment and fines; subsequent offence imprisonment up to 5 years (✅ owner 14 advanced).
- First-offence 2013 penalty figures ⚠️ not verified.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1993 penalty (✅ Wikipedia). 2013 subsequent-offence term 5 years (✅ owner 14).
**Links:** SJ-3002, SJ-3010, SJ-3013.
**Trap:** Quoting the 1993 penalty for the 2013 Act, or claiming no convictions ever occurred under the 2013 Act. Wikipedia reports no convictions under the 1993 law across 20 years.
**Mains hook:** Harsher penalties are necessary but insufficient; deterrence needs prosecutions. Qualify with rare-FIR evidence (owner 14).
**Back:** *"Ninety-three: one year, two thousand; thirteen: five years for repeat."*

### Flashcard SJ-3013 — Prohibition versus abolition (advanced)
**Front:** Why does prohibition not automatically mean abolition of manual scavenging?
**Definition (learn):** Prohibition criminalises employment; abolition requires that the occupation actually disappears, which needs enforcement, alternative livelihoods and an end to caste-based occupational allocation.
**Breakdown:**
- Prohibition is a legal state.
- Abolition is an outcome.
- Gap bridged by inspection, procurement accountability, mechanisation and durable livelihoods (✅ owner 14 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 14 advanced, section 2 (✅ owner 14).
**Links:** SJ-3002, SJ-3014, SJ-3015.
**Trap:** Saying the law has failed because the Act lacks penalties. The gap is in enforcement and outcomes.
**Mains hook:** "Persistence reflects enforcement and caste-stigma failure, not a legal vacuum." Use the abolition/prohibition distinction as the thesis.
**Back:** *"Ban on paper is not an empty sewer."*

### Flashcard SJ-3014 — Enforcement-failure chain (manual scavenging)
**Front:** What four steps explain why sewer deaths persist despite the 2013 prohibition?
**Definition (learn):** A causal chain from under-counting to diffuse liability, rare prosecution and delayed compensation.
**Breakdown:**
- 1. Survey under-count: local bodies avoid rehabilitation liability.
- 2. Contractor accountability gap: outsourcing diffuses liability; informal workers lack PPE.
- 3. Prosecution rarity: deaths treated as accidents, not prohibited employment.
- 4. Compensation delay: families lack documentation (✅ owner 14 advanced; causal claims ⚠️ analytical).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 14 advanced sections 1 and 3 (✅ owner 14; not an official statistic).
**Links:** SJ-3016, SJ-3018, SJ-3007.
**Trap:** Citing a single death count as proof of enforcement failure. Use dated source counts and distinguish recorded deaths from all unsafe cleaning.
**Mains hook:** Diagnose the specific gap (survey, contractor, prosecution, compensation) rather than "more funds needed".
**Back:** *"Count low, blame diffused, charge rarely, pay late."*

### Flashcard SJ-3015 — Caste-labour persistence chain
**Front:** How does caste-based occupation persist in manual scavenging?
**Definition (learn):** Intergenerational occupation lock: specific Dalit sub-castes are concentrated in sanitation work; stigma and limited alternative skills keep the cycle going.
**Breakdown:**
- Occupation lock across generations.
- One-time rehabilitation cash and short training do not overcome social barriers; workers return.
- Demand-side preference: employers may hire from traditional scavenger castes (✅ owner 14 advanced; causal claims ⚠️ analytical).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 14 basic section 1 caste nexus (✅ owner 14).
**Links:** SJ-3013, SJ-3019, SJ-3009.
**Trap:** Treating manual scavenging only as an occupational-safety problem. The caste-labour nexus makes it a recognitive-justice issue.
**Mains hook:** Caste dignity and livelihood are both needed; a mechanisation-only answer ignores the workers who would need an exit route.
**Back:** *"Born to the broom, kept by stigma, pushed back by one-time cash."*

### Flashcard SJ-3016 — Mechanisation: substitution versus safety (advanced)
**Front:** What is the difference between mechanisation as substitution and mechanisation as safety, and which does NAMASTE emphasise?
**Definition (learn):** Substitution removes human entry (robotic cleaners); safety mechanisation gives PPE and equipment to humans who still enter. NAMASTE currently emphasises the latter (✅ owner 14 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- Substitution → no human entry; capital-intensive and technologically nascent.
- Safety → PPE, training, equipment; humans still enter.
- Limit: adding PPE alone does not eliminate hazard (⚠️ analytical).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 14 advanced sections 2 and 6 (✅ owner 14).
**Links:** SJ-3008, SJ-3014, SJ-3018.
**Trap:** Option pairs saying NAMASTE fully eliminates human entry into sewers. It does not.
**Mains hook:** Qualify mechanisation: it shifts hazard unless it removes human entry; accountability for procurement is what converts machines into safety.
**Back:** *"Safety suits humans in; substitution keeps humans out."*

### Flashcard SJ-3017 — Rehabilitation versus reparation (advanced)
**Front:** Distinguish rehabilitation from reparation in the context of manual scavenging.
**Definition (learn):** Rehabilitation is forward-looking livelihood support; reparation addresses historical, intergenerational caste injustice. Indian policy focuses on rehabilitation, not reparation (✅ owner 14 advanced concept table; analytical ⚠️).
**Breakdown:**
- Rehabilitation → skill, loan, livelihood, scheme convergence.
- Reparation → redress for historical wrong; not current Indian practice.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 14 advanced section 2.
**Links:** SJ-3009, SJ-3015, SJ-3001.
**Trap:** Calling compensation for a death "reparation". Court-directed compensation is remedy for a specific injury, not historical reparation.
**Mains hook:** Argue for a rights-based livelihood transition with a caste lens; reparation is a normative debate, not a current policy instrument.
**Back:** *"Rehab looks forward; reparation looks back."*

### Flashcard SJ-3018 — Contractor and employer boundary (advanced)
**Front:** Why is contractor-run sewer cleaning a liability puzzle under the 2013 Act?
**Definition (learn):** Municipalities outsource cleaning to private contractors who engage informal workers without PPE; the municipality may argue it is not the "employer" (✅ owner 14 advanced boundary case; legal outcome ⚠️).
**Breakdown:**
- Definition of covered employer includes local authority, agency or contractor (✅ Wikipedia: PEMSR definition).
- Outsourcing diffuses liability in practice.
- Liability is fact-specific; trace it, do not assume it away (✅ owner 14 basic section 5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Definition coverage (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-3003, SJ-3014, SJ-3002.
**Trap:** Assuming outsourcing absolves the municipality. The definition itself names contractors and agencies.
**Mains hook:** Accountability must follow the contract chain: procurement conditions, inspection and prosecution of the actual employer.
**Back:** *"Outsourcing moves the crew, not the liability."*

### Flashcard SJ-3019 — Constitutional grounding: Articles 17, 21 and 23
**Front:** Which fundamental-rights Articles ground the sanitation-labour critique?
**Definition (learn):** Article 17 abolishes untouchability; Article 21 protects life and dignity; Article 23 prohibits traffic in human beings and forced labour (✅ owner 14 basic; article content ⚠️ from standard knowledge, not fetched).
**Breakdown:**
- Art. 17 → caste-based stigma of scavenging.
- Art. 21 → dignity and safe working life; sewer deaths.
- Art. 23 → forced or bonded labour framing.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 14 basic section 10 (✅ owner 14).
**Links:** SJ-3015, SJ-3014, SJ-3002.
**Trap:** Citing Article 15 or 16 for the manual-scavenging prohibition. The main grounds are 17, 21 and 23.
**Mains hook:** Use Articles 17, 21 and 23 to show that the harm is a constitutional rights violation, not only a welfare failure.
**Back:** *"Seventeen stigma, twenty-one dignity, twenty-three forced toil."*

### Flashcard SJ-3020 — World Toilet Organization (2024 Prelims GS-I Q24)
**Front:** Which PYQ does the Sanitation owner route to the World Toilet Organization, and what is the answer status?
**Definition (learn):** The 2024 Prelims GS-I question 24 is routed to this owner on the World Toilet Organization; the official Set-A key is held locally, but the answer is not recorded in this repository.
**Breakdown:**
- Source: audited 2024-2025 Prelims routing ledger (✅ owner 14).
- Answer: not recorded or inferred; no option letter is given here.
- Coverage: body's mandate and statement-level distinctions from sanitation justice (✅ owner 14).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024 Prelims GS-I Q24 (✅ owner 14). Body founding details ⚠️ not verified.
**Links:** SJ-3021, SJ-3001.
**Trap:** Marking an answer letter from memory. The owner explicitly declines to supply one.
**Mains hook:** A toilet-focused body is useful only when linked to safe containment and sustained use, not construction numbers alone.
**Back:** *"Toilets for all, but check the key before you claim it."*

### Flashcard SJ-3021 — Sanitation chain: open-defecation-free is not safe sanitation
**Front:** Why is open-defecation-free (ODF) status not the same as safely managed sanitation?
**Definition (learn):** ODF means no open defecation; safely managed sanitation requires containment, safe emptying, transport, treatment and disposal across the chain (✅ owner 14 semantic-completeness section).
**Breakdown:**
- Containment → toilet construction.
- Use → sustained behaviour; construction is not use.
- Emptying and treatment → safe removal; this is where manual scavenging arises.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 14 semantic-completeness section (✅ owner 14).
**Links:** SJ-3005, SJ-3020, SJ-3003.
**Trap:** Equating ODF declaration with elimination of manual handling of excreta.
**Mains hook:** Sanitation justice covers the whole chain; a latrine built without a safe-emptying system can recreate manual scavenging.
**Back:** *"Built is not used; used is not safely emptied."*

### Flashcard SJ-3022 — Recommended NCSK reform (advanced)
**Front:** What reforms does the advanced owner recommend for the NCSK and for mandatory FIRs?
**Definition (learn):** Owner 14 advanced recommends re-enacting NCSK as a permanent statutory commission with quasi-judicial powers, annual reporting to Parliament and binding directions to states; and mandatory FIRs for every sewer death (⚠️ recommendation, not current law).
**Breakdown:**
- Statutory NCSK: quasi-judicial powers, annual reporting, binding directions.
- Mandatory FIR per sewer death as a statutory presumption of prohibited employment.
- Both are proposals (⚠️).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 14 advanced section 4 (✅ owner 14; proposals ⚠️).
**Links:** SJ-3010, SJ-3011, SJ-3014.
**Trap:** Presenting a recommended reform as an existing legal provision.
**Mains hook:** Argue for statutory permanence and presumptions only with evidence that resolution-based bodies failed; state the cost of change.
**Back:** *"Make the commission law, make every death a case."*

### GROUP — Labour Social Security, Unorganised and Gig Workers (owners 15, basic and advanced)

### Flashcard SJ-3023 — Enactment is not commencement: the social-security status ladder
**Front:** What are the rungs of the status ladder that every social-security claim must pass?
**Definition (learn):** Enacted → rules or scheme notified → provision commenced → contribution collected → benefit delivered. A legal text can sit on any lower rung (✅ owner 15 basic section 1).
**Breakdown:**
- Enacted: Act passed with assent.
- Commenced: provision in force by notification.
- Rules/scheme notified: operational machinery.
- Contribution collected and benefit delivered: actual protection.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Code on Social Security commenced 21 November 2025 (✅ Wikipedia); final Central Rules May 2026 (✅ owner 15, not fetched).
**Links:** SJ-3024, SJ-3029, SJ-3036.
**Trap:** Reporting an enacted provision as a delivered entitlement.
**Mains hook:** Lead every implementation claim with the rung it has reached; this answers "is it operational?" before "is it good?".
**Back:** *"Enact, notify, commence, collect, deliver."*

### Flashcard SJ-3024 — Code on Social Security, 2020
**Front:** When was the Code on Social Security, 2020 enacted, assented to, and commenced?
