**Definition (learn):** One of the four labour codes; it merges nine central social-security laws and brings gig and platform workers within social-security schemes.
**Breakdown:**
- Lok Sabha passed 22 September 2020; Rajya Sabha 23 September 2020; assent 28 September 2020; Act No. 36 of 2020 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Section 142 in force 3 May 2021 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Commenced 21 November 2025 for the Code as a whole (✅ Wikipedia; owner 15).
- Merges nine central labour laws including the Unorganised Workers' Social Security Act, 2008 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 22/23/28 September 2020; Act No. 36 of 2020; 3 May 2021; 21 November 2025 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** SJ-3023, SJ-3025, SJ-3026, SJ-3028.
**Trap:** Dating the whole Code to 2020 or 2021. Enactment, partial commencement and full commencement are three dates.
**Mains hook:** Use the three dates to show that legal design predates delivery by years; qualify with rules status.
**Back:** *"Twenty to assent, twenty-five to live."*

### Flashcard SJ-3025 — The four labour codes and the nine merged laws
**Front:** Which four labour codes commenced on 21 November 2025, and what does the Social Security Code absorb?
**Definition (learn):** Labour codes replaced many older central labour statutes; the Social Security Code merges nine social-security laws.
**Breakdown:**
- Four codes: Code on Social Security; Code on Occupational Safety, Health and Working Conditions (OSHWC); Industrial Relations Code; Code on Wages (✅ owner 15 and owner 16; names ⚠️ standard knowledge).
- Social Security Code absorbs nine central laws including the Unorganised Workers' Social Security Act, 2008 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Commencement of all four: 21 November 2025 (✅ owner 15, owner 16).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 21 November 2025 (✅ Wikipedia for SS Code; owner for the other three).
**Links:** SJ-3024, SJ-3051, SJ-3052.
**Trap:** Thinking that the BOCW Act or the EPF Act disappeared with commencement; occupation-specific architecture survives.
**Mains hook:** Labour codes are a consolidation, not an extension; the question is delivery under each code.
**Back:** *"Social, Safety, Industrial, Wages: twenty-five, twenty-one, November."*

### Flashcard SJ-3026 — Gig worker (Code definition)
**Front:** How does the Code on Social Security define a gig worker?
**Definition (learn):** A person who performs work or participates in a work arrangement and earns from activities outside the traditional employer-employee relationship. Statutory recognition, not an automatic benefit (✅ owner 15).
**Breakdown:**
- Work outside the traditional employer-employee relationship.
- Earnings from gig activity.
- Recognition for scheme design, not an entitlement guarantee.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 15 section 2 (✅ owner 15). Exact clause number and text ⚠️ not fetched (Wikipedia page does not define it).
**Links:** SJ-3027, SJ-3028, SJ-3042.
**Trap:** "Gig worker definition gives a right to pension." The definition gives recognition only.
**Mains hook:** Recognition is necessary but not sufficient; the same definition can carry protection, but only with contribution and claim rules.
**Back:** *"Outside the employer line, inside the Code."*

### Flashcard SJ-3027 — Platform worker (Code definition)
**Front:** How does the Code define a platform worker, and how does it differ from a gig worker?
**Definition (learn):** A person engaged in platform work outside the traditional employer-employee relationship, accessing organisations or individuals through an online platform for specific services in exchange for payment (✅ owner 15).
**Breakdown:**
- Online platform as the intermediary.
- Specific services for payment.
- Overlaps with gig work; the platform link is the key differentiator.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 15 section 2 (✅ owner 15); clause text ⚠️ not fetched.
**Links:** SJ-3026, SJ-3028, SJ-3036.
**Trap:** Treating gig and platform as identical in all contexts. Platform work requires the online-platform element.
**Mains hook:** Algorithmic control defines the platform worker's risk; the definition captures the mediated relationship.
**Back:** *"Platform mediates the service, the worker serves the payment."*

### Flashcard SJ-3028 — Aggregator and the Section 114 contribution range
**Front:** What is an aggregator, and what contribution range does Section 114 provide?
**Definition (learn):** An aggregator is a digital intermediary connecting service users with providers through an app or platform. Section 114 provides for a Central-notified contribution of at least 1% and no more than 2% of annual turnover, capped at 5% of amounts paid or payable to gig and platform workers (✅ owner 15).
**Breakdown:**
- Aggregator → digital marketplace intermediary (✅ owner 15).
- Contribution → 1% to 2% of annual turnover (owner-stated).
- Payout cap → 5% of amounts paid or payable to gig and platform workers (owner-stated).
- Section number and exact rate text ⚠️ not verified (Wikipedia gives no figures).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1–2% turnover; 5% cap (✅ owner 15 basic and advanced); ⚠️ not verified against Act text.
**Links:** SJ-3029, SJ-3026, SJ-3027.
**Trap:** Quoting Section 114 as proof of a current levy; the range is statutory design, not a collection ledger.
**Mains hook:** Contribution incidence can be passed to workers or users; transparency and incidence analysis decide whether it is progressive.
**Back:** *"One to two per cent of turnover, five per cent of payouts, notified not yet proven."*

### Flashcard SJ-3029 — Statutory range versus collected contribution (trap)
**Front:** Why is the Section 114 range not evidence of a collected levy or paid benefit?
**Definition (learn):** A statutory range is a legal possibility; collection needs a notified rate, a fund account, claim rules and paid benefits.
**Breakdown:**
- Legal range → Section 114.
- Notified rate → central scheme or rule.
- Collection → Fund ledger.
- Paid claim → individual benefit.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 15 section 2 and advanced section 2 (✅ owner 15).
**Links:** SJ-3028, SJ-3023, SJ-3030.
**Trap:** "Code commenced, so the 1–2% levy is collected and benefits paid." Each rung needs a source.
**Mains hook:** Distinguish the statutory design from its implementation; a levy with no notified rate can give workers false certainty.
**Back:** *"A range on paper is not a rupee in the Fund."*

### Flashcard SJ-3030 — e-Shram (2021)
**Front:** What is e-Shram, who runs it, and what does it give a registrant?
**Definition (learn):** A Ministry of Labour and Employment database and portal for unorganised workers, which issues a UAN-based card (✅ owner 15).
**Breakdown:**
- Launched 2021 (✅ owner 15).
- Registration gives a UAN-based card.
- A registry and convergence tool, not a pension, insurance or statutory gig entitlement.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2021, MoLE (✅ owner 15). Cumulative registration total is time-varying and not cited.
**Links:** SJ-3031, SJ-3033, SJ-3036.
**Trap:** "e-Shram registration automatically gives pension and health insurance." It does not.
**Mains hook:** Registration is necessary but not sufficient for social security: it maps workers, but benefits need eligibility, contribution and claim systems.
**Back:** *"Register to be counted, enrol to be covered."*

### Flashcard SJ-3031 — Building and Other Construction Workers (BOCW) Welfare Boards
**Front:** How are construction workers protected through BOCW welfare boards?
**Definition (learn):** State welfare boards under the BOCW legal framework, funded principally by the construction-welfare cess, that deliver State-notified benefits to registered workers.
**Breakdown:**
- Registration with the State BOCW board.
- Funding: construction welfare cess (✅ owner 15).
- Benefits: accident, education, pension support as notified by the State.
- Framework dated to 1996 (✅ owner 15, "1996 framework"; statute not fetched).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1996 framework (✅ owner 15); cess-funded (✅ owner 15).
**Links:** SJ-3032, SJ-3030, SJ-3025.
**Trap:** "BOCW boards disappeared on commencement." The Code retains an occupation-specific cess and welfare architecture.
**Mains hook:** Occupation-specific welfare shows that a single national scheme is not the only route; State implementation variation decides real protection.
**Back:** *"Cess funds, state boards, registered builders."*

### Flashcard SJ-3032 — EPFO and ESIC as delivery institutions
**Front:** What do EPFO and ESIC deliver, and when do they apply?
**Definition (learn):** Employees' Provident Fund Organisation (provident fund) and Employees' State Insurance Corporation (employee insurance), applicable where their legacy statutes cover the establishment or worker (✅ owner 15).
**Breakdown:**
- EPFO → provident fund and pension for covered employees.
- ESIC → employee insurance (health, sickness, maternity; ⚠️ standard knowledge).
- Coverage depends on establishment and wage thresholds (⚠️ not verified).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 15 section 4 (✅ owner 15).
**Links:** SJ-3031, SJ-3024, SJ-3023.
**Trap:** Assuming EPFO and ESIC cover all gig workers. They protect eligible formal or employed workers under their own statutes.
**Mains hook:** Formal coverage is employment-linked; gig work exposes the gap between employee-linked insurance and platform-based income.
**Back:** *"Provident for employees, insurance for the state-covered."*

### Flashcard SJ-3033 — PM-SYM (Pradhan Mantri Shram Yogi Maan-dhan, 2019)
**Front:** What is PM-SYM, what pension does it promise, and how are contributions matched?
**Definition (learn):** A voluntary contributory pension scheme for eligible unorganised workers, launched 2019, where the worker and the Government contribute matching amounts and a monthly pension of ₹3,000 is payable after age 60, subject to scheme conditions (✅ owner 15).
**Breakdown:**
- Voluntary and contributory.
- Matching contribution by Government.
- Pension ₹3,000 per month after 60 for eligible enrollees.
- Separate enrolment required; e-Shram registration alone is not enough.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2019; ₹3,000 per month after age 60 (✅ owner 15). Wikipedia page not retrieved.
**Links:** SJ-3030, SJ-3034, SJ-3036.
**Trap:** Confusing PM-SYM with the EPFO pension or with a universal old-age pension. PM-SYM is voluntary.
**Mains hook:** Voluntary pensions exclude the irregular earner who cannot keep contributing; this pushes the design toward a universal floor.
**Back:** *"Shram Yogi: join, match, thirty-to-sixty, three thousand."*

### Flashcard SJ-3034 — Social-security benefit categories under the Code
**Front:** Which benefit categories does the Code list for unorganised and gig workers?
**Definition (learn):** The Code envisages life and disability insurance, health and maternity benefits, provident insurance and pension, and skill upgradation, with an open-ended "etc.".
**Breakdown:**
- Life and disability insurance.
- Health and maternity benefits.
- Provident insurance and pension.
- Skill upgradation (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Wikipedia list (✅ Wikipedia). Exact clause ⚠️.
**Links:** SJ-3024, SJ-3033, SJ-3036.
**Trap:** Presenting the list as a list of operational schemes. It lists categories of benefit, not current funded schemes.
**Mains hook:** Map each benefit to its delivery route; a list of categories says nothing about claims, financing or portability.
**Back:** *"Life, health, pension, skill."*

### Flashcard SJ-3035 — Gig-worker scale anchor (NITI Aayog 2022)
**Front:** How many gig workers did NITI Aayog estimate, and what did it project?
**Definition (learn):** NITI Aayog's 2022 report estimated 7.7 million gig workers in 2020-21 and projected 23.5 million by 2029-30 (✅ owner 15).
**Breakdown:**
- 2020-21 estimate: 7.7 million.
- 2029-30 projection: 23.5 million (scenario).
- Not a registry or beneficiary count.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** NITI Aayog report 2022 (✅ owner 15; not fetched). Time-bound estimate.
**Links:** SJ-3026, SJ-3030, SJ-3036.
**Trap:** Using the 2029-30 projection as today's count, or using e-Shram totals as gig-worker totals.
**Mains hook:** Scale justifies policy urgency; a projection is a planning assumption and must be labelled.
**Back:** *"Seven point seven then, twenty-three point five by twenty-nine-thirty."*

### Flashcard SJ-3036 — Registration versus entitlement chain (advanced)
**Front:** Walk through the registration-to-benefit chain and name where it commonly breaks.
**Definition (learn):** Database entry → scheme matching → payment or claim → outcome. Each step can fail even after registration (✅ owner 15 advanced; analytical ⚠️).
**Breakdown:**
- Database entry: e-Shram UAN.
- Scheme matching: age, income, occupation, contribution, documents.
- Claim friction: bank access, documentation, awareness, data quality.
- Outcome risk: high registration with low uptake.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 15 advanced section 3 (✅ owner 15).
**Links:** SJ-3030, SJ-3033, SJ-3023.
**Trap:** Reading a headline registration total as coverage.
**Mains hook:** Measure protection at the claim stage; registration statistics without claim data overstate coverage.
**Back:** *"Entered, matched, claimed, received: four doors."*

### Flashcard SJ-3037 — Platform power and algorithmic management (advanced)
**Front:** How does platform power create insecurity for gig workers?
**Definition (learn):** Algorithmic control (ratings, allocation, incentives, deactivation) shapes working conditions without transparent human management, causing volatility and weak natural justice (✅ owner 15 advanced; analytical ⚠️).
**Breakdown:**
- Algorithmic control → allocation and rating.
- Income volatility → unilateral incentive changes.
- Classification boundary → social-security recognition does not settle employee status.
- Voice gap → app grievance is not collective bargaining.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 15 advanced section 3 (✅ owner 15).
**Links:** SJ-3027, SJ-3038, SJ-3040.
**Trap:** Treating a social-security definition as settling minimum wage or dismissal disputes.
**Mains hook:** Algorithmic deactivation needs an independent grievance route; recognition alone does not fix power imbalance.
**Back:** *"The app rates, allocates, and deactivates; the worker has no bargaining table."*

### Flashcard SJ-3038 — Universal floor versus contributory top-up (advanced)
**Front:** Why does a credible gig-worker model often need both a tax-financed floor and a contributory top-up?
**Definition (learn):** Tax-financed basic protection reduces exclusion of low or irregular earners; contributory protection deepens cover but may exclude volatile earners (✅ owner 15 advanced concept table; analytical ⚠️).
**Breakdown:**
- Universal floor → tax-financed, inclusive of irregular earners.
- Contributory top-up → deeper cover, contribution-linked.
- Combined model is the analytical recommendation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 15 advanced section 2.
**Links:** SJ-3033, SJ-3037, SJ-3039.
**Trap:** Assuming contributory schemes like PM-SYM alone cover irregular earners.
**Mains hook:** The floor-plus-top-up design is the usual answer to "how to cover the gig worker"; qualify with fiscal cost.
**Back:** *"Floor for all, top-up for the steady."*

### Flashcard SJ-3039 — Portability versus interoperability (advanced)
**Front:** Distinguish portability from interoperability in social-security delivery.
**Definition (learn):** Portability means a UAN or card follows the worker; interoperability means records, eligibility rules, claim settlement and grievance redress work across States, platforms and occupations (✅ owner 15 advanced; analytical ⚠️).
**Breakdown:**
- Portable card → movement of identity.
- Interoperable systems → benefits actually move.
- Privacy: cross-platform portability raises surveillance risk (⚠️).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 15 advanced section 2 and section 6.
**Links:** SJ-3030, SJ-3017, SJ-3036.
**Trap:** Calling a portable card "portable benefits".
**Mains hook:** Portability is a data design question; the qualifier is purpose limitation, correction rights and offline routes.
**Back:** *"A card travels; benefits must follow it."*

### Flashcard SJ-3040 — Contribution incidence and levy pass-through (advanced)
**Front:** Why does an aggregator levy's progressivity depend on incidence?
**Definition (learn):** A levy on aggregators can finance protection, but may be passed to workers or consumers; progressivity needs payout transparency and periodic incidence analysis (✅ owner 15 advanced; analysis ⚠️).
**Breakdown:**
- Incidence → who bears the burden.
- Pass-through → to workers (lower pay) or users (higher prices).
- Need: transparent payout records.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 15 advanced section 6.
**Links:** SJ-3028, SJ-3029, SJ-3037.
**Trap:** Assuming an aggregator levy is borne by the platform.
**Mains hook:** A levy financing protection is only progressive if incidence analysis shows the burden does not fall on the lowest earners.
**Back:** *"Who really pays the platform levy?"*

### Flashcard SJ-3041 — 90/120-day eligibility threshold trap
**Front:** Does the Code set a national 90 or 120-day eligibility threshold for gig workers?
**Definition (learn):** No. Owner 15 explicitly states that no official operative rule supplies a 90/120-day threshold; it must not be invented.
**Breakdown:**
- Threshold → only if an official scheme rule sets it.
- Work-day threshold → administrative design option, can exclude casual workers (✅ owner 15 advanced section 6).
- Status: not verified in any fetched official source.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 15 advanced section 6 and section 8 trap (✅ owner 15).
**Links:** SJ-3026, SJ-3028, SJ-3029.
**Trap:** Quoting a 90/120-day rule as a Code provision. Use only if an official scheme rule is cited.
**Mains hook:** Thresholds can exclude casual workers; the design choice needs evidence, not assumption.
**Back:** *"No ninety-one-twenty in the Code without a rule."*

### Flashcard SJ-3042 — Gig worker is not necessarily an employee
**Front:** Why does social-security recognition not settle employee status?
**Definition (learn):** Gig and platform definitions extend social-security design beyond employment; they do not settle every dispute on minimum wages, working time, dismissal or collective bargaining (✅ owner 15 advanced; analytical ⚠️).
**Breakdown:**
- Social-security recognition → a design question.
- Employee status → a labour-standards question.
- The platform label "partner" may not defeat a future scheme definition, but can matter in other disputes (⚠️).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 15 advanced section 5.
**Links:** SJ-3026, SJ-3037, SJ-3023.
**Trap:** Saying the gig definition makes the worker an employee.
**Mains hook:** Separate three questions: who is covered for social security, who is an employee for labour standards, and who can bargain.
**Back:** *"Covered is not employed; employed is not unionised."*

### GROUP — Urban Poor, Homeless and Migrant Workers (owners 16, basic and advanced)

### Flashcard SJ-3043 — Urban vulnerability: the housing-livelihood-portability nexus
**Front:** Name the three dimensions of urban poverty that the owner's core framework links.
**Definition (learn):** Urban poverty is a nexus of housing deprivation (homeless and slum dwelling), livelihood precarity (street vendors, informal work) and entitlement-portability barriers (migrants). Each has a distinct policy instrument (✅ owner 16 basic section 1).
**Breakdown:**
- Housing → DAY-NULM Shelter for Urban Homeless (SUH).
- Livelihood → PM SVANidhi and the Street Vendors Act, 2014.
- Portability → ONORC and the migrant-labour legal route.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 16 basic section 1 (✅ owner 16).
**Links:** SJ-3044, SJ-3047, SJ-3050, SJ-3053.
**Trap:** Answering with only one dimension (for example, only housing). Owner-recommended answers address all three.
**Mains hook:** Urban inclusion needs portable person-based entitlements plus place-based housing and livelihood rights.
**Back:** *"Roof, rupee, ration: three doors to urban dignity."*

### Flashcard SJ-3044 — DAY-NULM (Deendayal Antyodaya Yojana: National Urban Livelihoods Mission)
**Front:** What is DAY-NULM, which ministry runs it, and what are its components?
**Definition (learn):** The urban livelihoods mission of MoHUA, providing self-employment support, skill training, SHG-based livelihoods and the Shelter for Urban Homeless (SUH) component (✅ owner 16).
**Breakdown:**
- Ministry: Ministry of Housing and Urban Affairs (MoHUA) (✅ owner 16).
- Components: livelihood (self-employment, skill, SHG) and SUH shelter.
- Implemented by States/UTs through State Urban Livelihoods Missions (✅ owner 16 advanced).
- Rural counterpart: DAY-NRLM under MoRD (✅ owner 16 trap section).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** MoHUA (✅ owner 16). Launch year ⚠️ not verified.
**Links:** SJ-3045, SJ-3046, SJ-3043.
**Trap:** Calling DAY-NULM a rural scheme. It is urban; DAY-NRLM is the rural one.
**Mains hook:** Urban livelihoods need SHG and credit linkages but also housing and service convergence; a livelihood-only answer is incomplete.
**Back:** *"Urban livelihoods, MoHUA; rural cousin is NRLM."*

### Flashcard SJ-3045 — Shelter for Urban Homeless (SUH)
**Front:** What does the SUH component provide, and what is the shelter-norm?
**Definition (learn):** A DAY-NULM component providing permanent all-weather shelters with 24×7 access, bedding, water and sanitation, and referral linkages to health, skill and livelihood services for urban homeless people (✅ owner 16).
**Breakdown:**
- Permanent, all-weather shelter.
- 24×7 access, bedding, water and sanitation.
- Referral to health, skills and livelihood.
- Norm: one shelter per 100,000 urban population (✅ owner 16; guideline norm not separately fetched).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** One shelter per 100,000 urban population (✅ owner 16).
**Links:** SJ-3044, SJ-3043, SJ-3053.
**Trap:** Counting shelters without service linkage; a shelter without referral does not convert into capability.
**Mains hook:** Shelter coverage is an output; use usage and service linkage as the outcome test.
**Back:** *"One shelter per lakh, with bed, water and a referral."*

### Flashcard SJ-3046 — DAY-NULM funding ratio (advanced)
**Front:** What are the Centre-State cost-sharing ratios for DAY-NULM?
**Definition (learn):** Centrally sponsored, with a 60:40 Centre-State cost share, and 90:10 for North-Eastern and special category States (✅ owner 16 advanced section 4).
**Breakdown:**
- General States → 60:40 Centre:State.
- NE and special states → 90:10.
- Implementation by ULBs; State matching delays slow shelter building (✅ owner 16 advanced section 3).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 60:40; 90:10 (✅ owner 16). Not verified against an official sanction order.
**Links:** SJ-3044, SJ-3045, SJ-3053.
**Trap:** Mixing DAY-NULM's ratio with other centrally sponsored schemes, which have different splits.
**Mains hook:** State matching capacity decides whether the Centre's funds convert into shelters; federal design shapes urban delivery.
**Back:** *"Sixty-forty; ninety-ten for the North-East and specials."*

### Flashcard SJ-3047 — PM SVANidhi (2020)
**Front:** What is PM SVANidhi, and what are its loan tranches and incentives?
**Definition (learn):** PM Street Vendor's AtmaNirbhar Nidhi: a collateral-free working-capital micro-loan for street vendors, launched 2020, with escalating tranches, an interest subsidy and a digital-payment cashback (✅ owner 16).
**Breakdown:**
- Initial loan ₹10,000.
- Second tranche ₹20,000 and third tranche ₹50,000 contingent on timely repayment.
- Interest subsidy (7%) and digital-payment cashback (✅ owner 16 basic section 3).
- Administered through banks; ULBs issue vendor certificates and letters of recommendation (✅ owner 16 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Launched 2020 (✅ owner 16); ₹10,000 / ₹20,000 / ₹50,000 (✅ owner 16). Current disbursal totals are dashboard figures and time-varying; not cited.
**Links:** SJ-3048, SJ-3049, SJ-3043.
**Trap:** Calling PM SVANidhi a grant. It is a micro-loan.
**Mains hook:** Credit eases working capital but not eviction or legal recognition; pair it with a vending-law route.
**Back:** *"Ten, twenty, fifty: loans that reward repayment."*

### Flashcard SJ-3048 — Street Vendors (Protection of Livelihood and Regulation of Street Vending) Act, 2014
**Front:** What does the 2014 Street Vendors Act provide, and what are its key bodies?
**Definition (learn):** The Act recognises vending as a regulated livelihood, creating Town Vending Committees, surveys and certificates of vending, vending plans and safeguards against eviction (✅ owner 16).
**Breakdown:**
- Town Vending Committees (TVC).
- Survey and certificate of vending.
- Vending plans.
- Procedural safeguards against eviction (✅ owner 16 basic section 4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Year 2014 (✅ owner 16 title and grounding; not fetched).
**Links:** SJ-3047, SJ-3049, SJ-3043.
**Trap:** "PM SVANidhi gives the vending right." Credit is separate from the Act's legal protections.
**Mains hook:** Livelihood legitimacy is a legal question as well as a finance question; the Act sets the protections.
**Back:** *"Committees, certificates, plans, no eviction without process."*

### Flashcard SJ-3049 — Credit versus legal right: PM SVANidhi versus the Street Vendors Act (trap)
**Front:** Why is a PM SVANidhi loan not the same as legal vending protection?
**Definition (learn):** A loan provides working capital; the Street Vendors Act provides legal recognition, planning and eviction safeguards. The two work on different axes.
**Breakdown:**
- Credit → PM SVANidhi (MoHUA, banks).
- Legal right → Street Vendors Act, TVCs, certificate and vending plan.
- Both needed; neither replaces the other.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 16 trap section and basic section 4 (✅ owner 16).
**Links:** SJ-3047, SJ-3048, SJ-3050.
**Trap:** Answer options saying the loan scheme "secures vendor rights". It does not.
**Mains hook:** A vendor with a loan can still be evicted in a clearance drive; legal protection must be enforced locally.
**Back:** *"Loan lends, law protects."*

### Flashcard SJ-3050 — ONORC (One Nation One Ration Card)
**Front:** How does ONORC make NFSA entitlements portable, and what infrastructure does it depend on?
**Definition (learn):** A portability mechanism within NFSA that lets an Aadhaar-seeded ration card draw foodgrain at any ePoS-enabled fair price shop across States (✅ owner 16).
**Breakdown:**
- Legal base: NFSA entitlement.
- Technology: Aadhaar seeding and ePoS.
- Not a separate card or separate entitlement (✅ owner 16 trap section).
- Rollout: pan-India, transaction data on Annavitaran portal (✅ owner 16 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Pan-India rollout achieved (✅ owner 16). Launch year ⚠️ not verified.
**Links:** SJ-3051, SJ-3052, SJ-3056.
**Trap:** "ONORC requires a new ration card." It uses the existing NFSA card with Aadhaar seeding.
**Mains hook:** Portability solves the seasonal-migrant food problem only where Aadhaar seeding and ePoS connectivity work; authentication failure excludes.
**Back:** *"One card, any shop, if Aadhaar and ePoS agree."*

### Flashcard SJ-3051 — Occupational Safety, Health and Working Conditions (OSHWC) Code, 2020
**Front:** What does the OSHWC Code do for migrant workers, and when did it commence?
**Definition (learn):** One of the four labour codes; it commenced 21 November 2025, repealed the Inter-State Migrant Workmen Act, 1979, and provides the migrant-worker registration, journey allowance and equal-treatment architecture (✅ owner 16).
**Breakdown:**
- Commenced 21 November 2025 (✅ owner 15 and 16).
- Repealed Inter-State Migrant Workmen Act, 1979 (✅ owner 16).
- Final Central Rules May 2026 (✅ owner 16).
- State rules and enforcement still matter; labour is concurrent (✅ owner 16).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 21 November 2025; May 2026 (owner).
**Links:** SJ-3052, SJ-3025, SJ-3056.
**Trap:** Saying central commencement alone guarantees migrant protection; check State rules and enforcement.
**Mains hook:** Migrant law has moved from a 1979 statute to a consolidated code; the test is enforcement at destination.
**Back:** *"Seventy-nine repealed; twenty-five commenced; States enforce."*

### Flashcard SJ-3052 — Inter-State Migrant Workmen Act, 1979 (repealed)
**Front:** Which earlier law did the OSHWC Code repeal for migrant workers?
**Definition (learn):** The Inter-State Migrant Workmen (Regulation of Employment and Conditions of Service) Act, 1979, which was repealed by the OSHWC Code (✅ owner 16).
**Breakdown:**
- Earlier regime: inter-State migrant workers regulated by a separate 1979 Act.
- Current regime: OSHWC Code, 2020, commenced 21 November 2025.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1979 Act; repeal via OSHWC Code (✅ owner 16).
**Links:** SJ-3051, SJ-3025.
**Trap:** Quoting the 1979 Act as current law after November 2025.
**Mains hook:** Consolidation may rationalise, but it does not automatically extend coverage to informal migrants.
**Back:** *"Seventy-nine Act retired into the Code."*

### Flashcard SJ-3053 — Houseless, slum-dwelling and inadequate housing: a distinct typology
**Front:** How do houseless people, slum dwellers and inadequate-housing households differ?
**Definition (learn):** Houseless or roofless persons have no shelter; slum dwellers have shelter in informal settlements with tenure insecurity and poor services; inadequate housing is kutcha or overcrowded shelter (✅ owner 16 basic section 1, typology; analytical ⚠️).
**Breakdown:**
- Houseless → pavement dwellers; SUH is the response.
- Slum → tenure insecurity; in-situ upgrading or resettlement.
- Inadequate → overcrowded or unsafe; housing scheme response.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 16 basic section 1 typology.
**Links:** SJ-3045, SJ-3054, SJ-3061.
**Trap:** Treating all urban poor as slum dwellers; homelessness is a distinct category, and a migrant may be homeless.
**Mains hook:** Different categories need different responses; a single housing metric hides the homeless.
**Back:** *"No roof, informal walls, unsafe room: three different problems."*

### Flashcard SJ-3054 — Census 2011 slum population anchor
**Front:** What did Census 2011 count as the slum population, and what does that figure exclude?
**Definition (learn):** Census 2011 counted about 65.5 million people in slums, around 17.4% of the urban population; it does not include every informal renter, homeless person or unnotified settlement (✅ owner 16).
**Breakdown:**
- Count: about 65.5 million (✅ owner 16).
- Share: about 17.4% of urban population (✅ owner 16).
- Exclusions: informal renters, homeless, unnotified settlements (✅ owner 16).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Census 2011 (✅ owner 16; not fetched). Old census; newer counts ⚠️ not cited.
**Links:** SJ-3053, SJ-3055, SJ-3043.
**Trap:** Citing 65.5 million as the current slum population. It is a 2011 figure.
**Mains hook:** Use the 2011 anchor with a caveat; the notified-slum definition excludes many vulnerable residents.
**Back:** *"Sixty-five million in 2011, not all of the poor."*

### Flashcard SJ-3055 — PMAY-U 2.0 (2024) and the Interest Subsidy Scheme
**Front:** What are the components of PMAY-U 2.0 and the ISS subsidy rule?
**Definition (learn):** The 2024 urban housing mission with four verticals: Beneficiary-Led Construction, Affordable Housing in Partnership, Affordable Rental Housing and Interest Subsidy Scheme (ISS).
**Breakdown:**
- Verticals: BLC, AHP, ARH, ISS.
- ISS: 4% subsidy on the first ₹8 lakh of a qualifying loan; maximum subsidy ₹1.80 lakh, for EWS, LIG and MIG households subject to conditions (✅ owner 16).
- Sanction is not completed or occupied housing (✅ owner 16).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024; 4%; ₹8 lakh; ₹1.80 lakh (✅ owner 16; not fetched).
**Links:** SJ-3054, SJ-3043, SJ-3053.
**Trap:** Treating sanctioned units as delivered housing; check completion and occupancy.
**Mains hook:** Tenure documents, land and occupancy decide whether a sanction reduces deprivation.
**Back:** *"Four verticals, four per cent on eight lakh."*

### Flashcard SJ-3056 — Circular and seasonal migration
**Front:** What is circular or seasonal migration, and why does it break fixed-entitlement models?
**Definition (learn):** Repeated movement between origin and destination in response to seasonal labour demand. Fixed ration-shop entitlement fails when the worker is elsewhere most of the year (✅ owner 16 basic section 2; analytical ⚠️).
**Breakdown:**
- Repeated, seasonal, between rural origin and urban destination.
- Entitlement needs to follow the person (ONORC).
- Permanent migration is one-way; policy must match the pattern.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 16 basic section 2 and advanced section 2.
**Links:** SJ-3050, SJ-3057, SJ-3063.
**Trap:** Treating all migrants as permanent residents.
**Mains hook:** Match the policy to the pattern: portability for circular, housing for permanent.
**Back:** *"Back and forth with the season; the ration must follow."*

### Flashcard SJ-3057 — 2024 GS-I migration PYQ: routing trap
**Front:** Which owner does the audited ledger route the 2024 GS-I migration question to, and why is it not solved here?
**Definition (learn):** The 2024 GS-I Q5 question on why large cities attract more migrants than towns (Discuss, 10 marks, 150 words) is routed to the Geography migration owner, not this owner (✅ owner 16 PYQ ownership section).
**Breakdown:**
- Routed owner: Geography migration-theories-and-patterns (✅ owner 16).
- This owner: policy-anchor only (ONORC, SUH, PM SVANidhi).
- No answer or marks claimed here beyond the anchor.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024 GS-I Q5; 10 marks; 150 words (✅ owner 16 ledger record).
**Links:** SJ-3056, SJ-3058, SJ-3063.
**Trap:** Claiming this owner "owns" the migration PYQ; the ledger says it does not.
**Mains hook:** Use push-pull and agglomeration from Geography; connect the policy answer to ONORC and shelters from here.
**Back:** *"Five from twenty-four routes to Geography, not here."*

### Flashcard SJ-3058 — 2025 GS-I smart-city question: routing trap
**Front:** Which owner does the ledger route the 2025 smart-city question to, and what is the owner's critique?
**Definition (learn):** The 2025 GS-I Q8 on smart cities addressing urban poverty and distributive justice (Discuss, 10 marks, 150 words) is routed to the Indian Society urbanisation owner (✅ owner 16 ledger).
**Breakdown:**
- Routed owner: Indian Society urbanisation.
- This owner's critique: area-based SPV selection excludes slums and homeless; showcase priority; participation gap (⚠️ analysis).
- Counter: pan-city components and digital service access.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 GS-I Q8; 10 marks; 150 words (✅ owner 16 ledger).
**Links:** SJ-3059, SJ-3060, SJ-3057.
**Trap:** Claiming the smart-city question as a Social Justice demand. The ledger says it is routed elsewhere.
**Mains hook:** Distributive justice in smart cities requires explicit slum and homeless targeting, not just SPV-area development.
**Back:** *"Smart cities must count the slum, not just the zone."*

### Flashcard SJ-3059 — Smart Cities Mission: area-based versus pan-city
**Front:** How does the Smart Cities Mission's area-based model differ from its pan-city component?
**Definition (learn):** Area-based development concentrates investment in selected smart zones through an SPV; pan-city components (transport, water, waste) can benefit wider populations (✅ owner 16 advanced section 2).
**Breakdown:**
- SPV-based area development.
- Pan-city transport, water, waste management.
- Primary focus is ICT-enabled infrastructure, not poverty alleviation (✅ owner 16 trap section).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 16 (✅ owner 16). Mission launch year ⚠️ not cited.
**Links:** SJ-3058, SJ-3060, SJ-3061.
**Trap:** "Smart Cities Mission is primarily poverty alleviation." It is primarily ICT infrastructure.
**Mains hook:** Qualify smart-city equity: inclusion depends on targeting slum, homeless and migrant services inside the plan.
**Back:** *"Zone first, pan-city second, poor last unless named."*

### Flashcard SJ-3060 — Distributive-justice failure chain (smart city, advanced)
**Front:** Trace the three steps by which area-based smart-city design can exclude the poorest.
**Definition (learn):** Area selection favours zones with infrastructure potential; investment flows to visible showcase projects; slum and homeless groups have limited voice in SPV decisions (✅ owner 16 advanced section 3; analytical ⚠️).
**Breakdown:**
- 1. Selection bias → peripheral slums excluded.
- 2. Showcase priority → roads and command centres over basic services.
- 3. Participation gap → SPV governance lacks slum voice.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 16 advanced section 3.
**Links:** SJ-3059, SJ-3058, SJ-3061.
**Trap:** Presenting the critique as a finding of fact; it is analytical.
**Mains hook:** Diagnose each step with evidence and then propose a screening rule for the SPV plan.
**Back:** *"Selected zone, showy road, silent slum."*

### Flashcard SJ-3061 — In-situ slum upgrading versus resettlement (advanced)
**Front:** What is the trade-off between in-situ slum upgrading and resettlement?
**Definition (learn):** In-situ upgrading improves water, sanitation and electricity where the slum stands, preserving livelihoods; resettlement relocates people, often breaking employment and networks (✅ owner 16 advanced section 2; analytical ⚠️).
**Breakdown:**
- In-situ → livelihood continuity; may leave land inefficient.
- Resettlement → frees central land; disrupts jobs and social ties.
- No first-best solution.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 16 advanced section 6.
**Links:** SJ-3053, SJ-3062, SJ-3060.
**Trap:** Assuming resettlement always improves outcomes for the poor.
**Mains hook:** Choose by livelihood proximity and tenure; qualify both options.
**Back:** *"Upgrade where they work; move only with a job."*

### Flashcard SJ-3062 — Tenure security versus title (advanced)
**Front:** How is tenure security different from legal title, and what instruments can provide tenure without title?
**Definition (learn):** Tenure security is assurance against eviction; title is formal ownership. Occupancy certificates or slum notification can provide tenure security without full title (✅ owner 16 advanced section 2; analytical ⚠️).
**Breakdown:**
- Title → formal ownership.
- Tenure security → protection from eviction.
- Intermediate tools → occupancy certificate, slum notification.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 16 advanced section 2.
**Links:** SJ-3061, SJ-3055, SJ-3053.
**Trap:** Equating a title deed with housing security, or the reverse.
**Mains hook:** Tenure security is the immediate need; titling is a longer route.
**Back:** *"Security is not the deed; the deed is one route to it."*

### Flashcard SJ-3063 — Agglomeration economies (advanced)
**Front:** What are agglomeration economies, and how do they explain urban in-migration?
**Definition (learn):** Productivity gains from spatial concentration of firms and workers: knowledge spillovers, labour-market pooling and shared inputs (✅ owner 16 advanced section 2; analytical ⚠️).
**Breakdown:**
- Knowledge spillovers.
- Labour-market pooling.
- Input sharing.
- Explains why cities attract migrants despite housing costs.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 16 advanced section 2.
**Links:** SJ-3057, SJ-3056, SJ-3058.
**Trap:** Confusing agglomeration with urbanisation alone; it is a productivity mechanism.
**Mains hook:** Use agglomeration for the "pull" side of migration; pair it with distress "push" in the Geography answer.
**Back:** *"Crowds make jobs; jobs make cities."*

### GROUP — Scheme Performance, Convergence, Targeting and Data Architecture (owners 17, basic and advanced)

### Flashcard SJ-3064 — The targeting-delivery-outcome-data-correction cycle
**Front:** What five stages make up the owner's default answer cycle for any scheme-evaluation question?
**Definition (learn):** Targeting (who is eligible) → delivery (how benefits reach them) → convergence (multi-scheme coordination at household level) → outcome measurement (what changes) → correction (feedback into redesign). No stage substitutes for another (✅ owner 17 basic section 1).
**Breakdown:**
- Targeting database: SECC 2011, Aadhaar, State lists (✅ owner 17).
- Delivery: DBT, JAM, PFMS, SNA; frontline cadres; ULBs and PRIs.
- Convergence: several ministries, one household.
- Outcome: NFHS, PLFS, NCRB, SDG India Index, MPI, administrative dashboards.
- Correction: DMEO, social audit, grievance redress.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 17 basic section 1 and section 3 (✅ owner 17).
**Links:** SJ-3069, SJ-3077, SJ-3080, SJ-3084.
**Trap:** Treating dashboard coverage as the final test of a scheme; the cycle needs the outcome and correction stages.
**Mains hook:** Use this cycle as the default structure; diagnose which stage fails rather than writing "implementation is weak".
**Back:** *"Target, deliver, converge, measure, correct."*

### Flashcard SJ-3065 — National Social Assistance Programme (NSAP)
**Front:** What is NSAP, which ministry runs it, and how is central money shared with States?
**Definition (learn):** The umbrella non-contributory social-assistance programme: old-age, widow and disability pensions, a family benefit and Annapurna food support (✅ owner 17).
**Breakdown:**
- Ministry: Ministry of Rural Development (MoRD) (✅ owner 17).
- Five components: IGNOAPS, IGNWPS, IGNDPS, NFBS, Annapurna.
- Central contribution is fixed; States may top up (✅ owner 17 advanced section 4).
- Eligibility runs through BPL or State targeting routes (✅ owner 17 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Five components (✅ owner 17). NSAP launch year ⚠️ not verified (Wikipedia page 404).
**Links:** SJ-3066, SJ-3067, SJ-3068, SJ-3069.
**Trap:** "NSAP is a single pension scheme." It is an umbrella of five components.
**Mains hook:** Non-contributory assistance is the floor for older and disabled people; the question is whether targeting leaks or excludes.
**Back:** *"Five pockets under one NSAP umbrella."*

### Flashcard SJ-3066 — IGNOAPS, IGNWPS and IGNDPS (NSAP pensions)
**Front:** What do the three NSAP pensions cover, and what are their full names?
**Definition (learn):** Indira Gandhi National Old Age Pension Scheme (IGNOAPS), Indira Gandhi National Widow Pension Scheme (IGNWPS) and Indira Gandhi National Disability Pension Scheme (IGNDPS) pay recurring non-contributory pensions to eligible old, widowed and disabled people (✅ owner 17).
**Breakdown:**
- IGNOAPS → old age.
- IGNWPS → widows.
- IGNDPS → persons with disabilities.
- Central pension amounts and eligibility age ⚠️ not verified.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 17 section 2 (✅ owner 17).
**Links:** SJ-3065, SJ-3067, SJ-3068.
**Trap:** Confusing IGNDPS with the Rights of Persons with Disabilities framework or with IGNOAPS age rules; check the exact scheme name.
**Mains hook:** Non-contributory pensions reach people outside the formal labour market; the adequacy of the amount is the question.
**Back:** *"Old, widow, disabled: IG pensions."*

### Flashcard SJ-3067 — National Family Benefit Scheme (NFBS)
**Front:** What does NFBS pay, and to whom?
**Definition (learn):** A lump-sum benefit paid on the death of the primary breadwinner within an eligible household (✅ owner 17).
**Breakdown:**
- Trigger: death of the primary breadwinner.
- Form: one-time lump-sum payment.
- Part of NSAP.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 17 section 2 (✅ owner 17). Amount ⚠️ not verified.
**Links:** SJ-3065, SJ-3066, SJ-3068.
**Trap:** Thinking NFBS is a recurring pension. It is a lump sum.
**Mains hook:** A lump-sum family benefit cushions a shock but does not replace lost income over time.
**Back:** *"Breadwinner gone, lump sum given."*

### Flashcard SJ-3068 — Annapurna (NSAP food support for elderly)
**Front:** What does Annapurna provide under NSAP?
**Definition (learn):** Food support for elderly persons who are not covered by the old-age pension (✅ owner 17).
**Breakdown:**
- Target: elderly not receiving pension.
- Form: food support.
- Part of NSAP umbrella.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 17 section 2 (✅ owner 17). Quantities ⚠️ not verified.
**Links:** SJ-3065, SJ-3066, SJ-3067.
**Trap:** Treating Annapurna as a pension top-up for all elderly; it is for those without the pension.
**Mains hook:** Food support for uncovered elderly shows the gap between pension targeting and need.
**Back:** *"No pension, but food on the table: Annapurna."*

### Flashcard SJ-3069 — SECC 2011 (Socio-Economic and Caste Census)
**Front:** What is SECC 2011, what does it target, and what happened to its caste data?
**Definition (learn):** A targeting database, distinct from the decennial Census, built on deprivation indicators (housing, assets, occupation, land) and used for various targeting routes (✅ owner 17).
**Breakdown:**
- Purpose: deprivation-based targeting.
- Core PM-JAY eligibility uses SECC-derived criteria (✅ owner 17).
- Caste data collected but not released or validated for general policy use (✅ owner 17).
- Static: not updated since 2011 (✅ owner 17 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2011 (✅ owner 17). Ministry: MoRD (✅ owner 17).
**Links:** SJ-3070, SJ-3071, SJ-3072, SJ-3086.
**Trap:** SECC 2011 and Census 2011 are the same exercise. They differ in purpose and method.
**Mains hook:** A static database cannot track new poverty; the question is whether the caste data can be used for targeting once released.
**Back:** *"Deprivation list, not head count; caste data kept in drawer."*

### Flashcard SJ-3070 — SECC versus Census (trap)
**Front:** How does SECC 2011 differ from Census 2011?
**Definition (learn):** Census is the decennial population enumeration with universal coverage; SECC is a targeting database using deprivation criteria (✅ owner 17).
**Breakdown:**
- Census → population count, decennial, universal.
- SECC → deprivation scoring for eligibility.
- Caste dimension: SECC collected it; Census has not published caste counts (✅ owner 17; Census caste enumeration decision in SJ-3085).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 17 trap section.
**Links:** SJ-3069, SJ-3085.
**Trap:** Option pairs that call SECC "the census of 2011".
**Mains hook:** Purpose decides data design: enumeration for statistics, deprivation database for eligibility.
**Back:** *"Census counts; SECC sorts."*

### Flashcard SJ-3071 — Inclusion error (Type I)
**Front:** Define inclusion error and state its cost to a scheme.
**Definition (learn):** Ineligible beneficiaries included in a scheme, so resources leak to non-poor people (✅ owner 17).
**Breakdown:**
- Cause: lax eligibility, self-declaration, outdated data.
- Cost: wasted resources; political backlash.
- Opposite: exclusion error (SJ-3072).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 17 section 2 (✅ owner 17).
**Links:** SJ-3072, SJ-3073, SJ-3087.
**Trap:** Confusing Type I with exclusion; Type I = included wrongly.
**Mains hook:** Self-declaration reduces exclusion but raises inclusion error; verification reverses the trade-off.
**Back:** *"Type One: the wrong ones are in."*

### Flashcard SJ-3072 — Exclusion error (Type II)
**Front:** Define exclusion error and give an example from scheme design.
**Definition (learn):** Eligible beneficiaries left out of a scheme, so the truly needy miss benefits (✅ owner 17).
**Breakdown:**
- Cause: documentation barriers, stale data, authentication failure.
- Example: a household excluded from a scheme for missing Aadhaar or caste certificate (✅ owner 17 advanced).
- Opposite: inclusion error.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 17 section 2.
**Links:** SJ-3071, SJ-3073, SJ-3087.
**Trap:** Thinking exclusion is "less bad" than inclusion; in welfare it is often the more harmful error.
**Mains hook:** Exclusion error needs grievance, assisted access and updating, not only tighter verification.
**Back:** *"Type Two: the needy are out."*

### Flashcard SJ-3073 — Universalism versus targeting
**Front:** What are the universalism and targeting options, and what example does the owner give for each?
**Definition (learn):** Universalism covers everyone in a category (minimises exclusion error, raises cost); targeting concentrates resources on the poor (minimises inclusion error, raises exclusion risk) (✅ owner 17 basic section 3; advanced section 2).
**Breakdown:**
- Universalism example: NFSA covers about two-thirds of the population (✅ owner 17).
- Targeting example: PM-JAY core eligibility (SECC-derived).
- No first-best; mix depends on programme type (✅ owner 17).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** NFSA ~67% (✅ owner 17; not fetched).
**Links:** SJ-3071, SJ-3072, SJ-3074.
**Trap:** Saying targeting is always cheaper; the cost is in the exclusion error it creates.
**Mains hook:** Programme type matters: universal for food and health floor, targeted for specialised support; state the trade-off.
**Back:** *"Everyone costs more; the few miss more."*

### Flashcard SJ-3074 — PM-JAY's age-based 70+ route versus the SECC route
**Front:** How does PM-JAY's age-based route for people aged 70 and above differ from its SECC-derived route?
**Definition (learn):** Core PM-JAY eligibility uses SECC-derived rural and urban criteria; a separate expansion covers people aged 70 and above on an age basis, not limited by SECC (✅ owner 17 basic section 2 and advanced; age details ⚠️).
**Breakdown:**
- SECC route → deprivation criteria.
- 70+ route → age-based.
- Different databases and eligibility logic in one scheme (✅ owner 17).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 17 section 2 and section 3.
**Links:** SJ-3069, SJ-3073, SJ-3078.
**Trap:** Assuming every PM-JAY beneficiary is SECC-identified.
**Mains hook:** A single scheme with two eligibility logics is a convergence and data-design issue in itself.
**Back:** *"Poor by SECC, old at seventy: two doors, one scheme."*

### Flashcard SJ-3075 — Aadhaar de-duplication and the Puttaswamy safeguards
**Front:** What must Aadhaar-enabled de-duplication retain to remain lawful and fair under the owner's framing?
**Definition (learn):** Aadhaar can support identity and de-duplication; verification must not become denial of entitlement. Puttaswamy requires legality, purpose, proportionality and safeguards (✅ owner 17 answer architecture; Puttaswamy year ⚠️ standard knowledge).
**Breakdown:**
- Keep assisted and non-digital alternatives.
- Keep a correction route.
- Purpose limitation and proportionality.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 17 basic section 3 and answer architecture.
**Links:** SJ-3064, SJ-3089, SJ-3072.
**Trap:** Treating Aadhaar as a need assessment; it identifies, not identifies poverty.
**Mains hook:** Authentication failure turns identity into exclusion; the safeguard is an alternate route, not a higher threshold.
**Back:** *"Identify without deny: Puttaswamy's four tests."*

### Flashcard SJ-3076 — Horizontal versus vertical convergence (advanced)
**Front:** Distinguish horizontal from vertical convergence.
**Definition (learn):** Horizontal convergence coordinates ministries at the same level; vertical convergence coordinates across Centre, State, district and block. Both are needed for household-level convergence (✅ owner 17 advanced section 2; analytical ⚠️).
**Breakdown:**
- Horizontal → Centre-Centre or State-State.
- Vertical → Centre-State-District-Block.
- Household convergence needs both.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 17 advanced section 2.
**Links:** SJ-3077, SJ-3078, SJ-3079.
**Trap:** Thinking that a national mission ensures convergence without district delivery.
**Mains hook:** District Collectors hold notional authority but lack data and agency; vertical convergence is the gap.
**Back:** *"Across ministries, down the tiers."*

### Flashcard SJ-3077 — Tribal household convergence example
**Front:** Which six schemes could one PVTG household need at once, and which ministries run them?
**Definition (learn):** A PVTG household may need FRA titles, PM-JANMAN infrastructure, PM POSHAN for school-age children, Poshan 2.0 for under-five children, PM-JAY health insurance and NSAP pension for an elderly member (✅ owner 17 basic section 5).
**Breakdown:**
- FRA titles → Ministry of Tribal Affairs and State forest department.
- PM-JANMAN → Ministry of Tribal Affairs.
- PM POSHAN → Ministry of Education.
- Poshan 2.0 → Ministry of Women and Child Development.
- PM-JAY → National Health Authority.
- NSAP pension → MoRD.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 17 section 5 (✅ owner 17). Ministry assignments per owner; NHA attribution ⚠️ standard knowledge.
**Links:** SJ-3076, SJ-3078, SJ-3074.
**Trap:** Assuming one scheme's enrolment covers all six; each has its own eligibility database and frontline worker.
**Mains hook:** The household is the unit of welfare; the scheme architecture is the unit of administration. Convergence bridges them.
**Back:** *"Six doors, one household, no common key."*

### Flashcard SJ-3078 — Convergence is not automatic (trap)
**Front:** Why does convergence not happen just because several schemes exist?
**Definition (learn):** Convergence needs coordinated eligibility databases, shared frontline cadres and district-level management; none occurs by default (✅ owner 17 trap section).
**Breakdown:**
- Silo design: separate eligibility databases.
- Frontline fragmentation: AWW, ASHA, Panchayat secretary, extension workers serve different schemes.
- District coordination gap.
- Timing mismatch of disbursal cycles (✅ owner 17 advanced section 3).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 17 advanced convergence chain.
**Links:** SJ-3076, SJ-3077, SJ-3079.
**Trap:** "Multiple schemes equal convergence." They can produce duplication without coordination.
**Mains hook:** Diagnose convergence failure by naming the database, the cadre and the district owner, then propose a fix for each.
**Back:** *"Many schemes, many databases, no one at the door."*

### Flashcard SJ-3079 — Administrative data, survey data and census data
**Front:** Compare administrative, survey and census data for scheme evaluation.
**Definition (learn):** Administrative data is high-frequency and programme-specific but excludes non-enrolled people; survey data gives comparable indicators with sampling and a reporting lag; census data is universal but infrequent (✅ owner 17 section 2; analytical ⚠️).
**Breakdown:**
- Administrative: dashboards, MIS, portal counts.
- Survey: NFHS, PLFS, NCRB-type records.
- Census: universal, decennial.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 17 section 2.
**Links:** SJ-3080, SJ-3081, SJ-3082.
**Trap:** Quoting dashboard totals as population outcomes.
**Mains hook:** Triangulate: dashboards show reach, surveys show change, census shows the denominator.
**Back:** *"Dashboards count reach; surveys measure change; census frames the crowd."*

### Flashcard SJ-3080 — Output, outcome and impact
**Front:** Distinguish output, outcome and impact with a nutrition example.
**Definition (learn):** Output is the immediate product (meals served); outcome is medium-term change (nutritional status); impact is long-term capability (cognitive development). Administrative data captures outputs, surveys capture outcomes, evaluations estimate impact (✅ owner 17 advanced concept table; analytical ⚠️).
**Breakdown:**
- Output → meals, enrolments, transfers.
- Outcome → stunting, anaemia.
- Impact → learning and capability.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 17 advanced section 2.
**Links:** SJ-3081, SJ-3079, SJ-3084.
**Trap:** Option pairs equating output with outcome.
**Mains hook:** Coverage is an output; the answer must show whether nutrition or capability changed.
**Back:** *"Meals served, stunting down, minds grown."*

### Flashcard SJ-3081 — Coverage is not outcome: NFHS stunting evidence
**Front:** What under-five stunting figures does the owner cite from NFHS-6 and NFHS-5, and what do they show?
**Definition (learn):** The owner cites NFHS-6 (2023-24) under-five stunting of 29.3%, down from NFHS-5's 35.5%, alongside high anganwadi enrolment. This shows that coverage does not guarantee nutrition (✅ owner 17 basic section 5).
**Breakdown:**
- NFHS-6 (2023-24): 29.3% (owner, as-of 21 July 2026).
- NFHS-5 (2019-21): 35.5%.
- Interpretation: outcome gap persists even with high coverage (⚠️ analysis).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 29.3% and 35.5% (✅ owner 17; not fetched, ⚠️ verify against NFHS report before quoting).
**Links:** SJ-3080, SJ-3082, SJ-3088.
**Trap:** Mixing NFHS-5 and NFHS-6 values without labelling the round.
**Mains hook:** Use the two rounds side by side to show progress and remaining gap, not just the latest number.
**Back:** *"Thirty-five point five, then twenty-nine point three: still too many."*

### Flashcard SJ-3082 — NFHS rounds and release status
**Front:** Which NFHS round is the current national round per the owner, and when was it released?
**Definition (learn):** NFHS-6 (2023-24) is the current national round per the owner; release date 29 May 2026. NFHS-5 (2019-21) is the comparator (✅ owner 17 basic section 6 and advanced section 9).
**Breakdown:**
- NFHS-4 → 2015-16 (✅ owner 17 section 4).
- NFHS-5 → 2019-21 (✅ owner 17).
- NFHS-6 → 2023-24, released 29 May 2026 per owner (⚠️ not fetched).
- Always label the round beside every indicator.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 17 sections 4, 6, 9.
**Links:** SJ-3081, SJ-3079.
**Trap:** Citing NFHS-4 values as current. Round identity matters.
**Mains hook:** Survey rounds give change over time; a value without its round cannot show progress.
**Back:** *"Round first, number second."*

### Flashcard SJ-3083 — DMEO (NITI Aayog) and OOMF
**Front:** What do DMEO and OOMF do in the monitoring architecture?
**Definition (learn):** DMEO (Development Monitoring and Evaluation Office, NITI Aayog) coordinates monitoring and third-party evaluations. OOMF (Output-Outcome Monitoring Framework) requires output-outcome statements in Budget documents (✅ owner 17 advanced section 4 and section 7).
**Breakdown:**
- DMEO → NITI Aayog; evaluation and monitoring.
- OOMF → Budget-document requirement introduced by Ministry of Finance (✅ owner 17 advanced).
- Cross-link Governance/15 (✅ owner 17).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 17 advanced section 7 (✅ owner 17). Dates ⚠️ not verified.
**Links:** SJ-3080, SJ-3088, SJ-3089.
**Trap:** Thinking DMEO's evaluations automatically change budget allocations; the owner says they are underused.
**Mains hook:** Budget-linked outcome statements are the lever, but only if evaluation findings feed funding decisions.
**Back:** *"DMEO judges, OOMF asks for outcomes in the Budget."*

### Flashcard SJ-3084 — DBT, PFMS, SNA and JAM delivery machinery
**Front:** How do DBT, PFMS, SNA and JAM work together in benefit delivery?
**Definition (learn):** JAM (Jan Dhan, Aadhaar, Mobile) is the identity-and-payment base; DBT moves money to accounts; PFMS (Public Financial Management System) tracks fund flow; SNA (Single Nodal Account) is a nodal-account arrangement for centrally sponsored scheme fund flows (✅ owner 17 cross-link; expansions and mechanics ⚠️ standard knowledge).
**Breakdown:**
- JAM → bank account, Aadhaar, mobile linkage.
- DBT → direct benefit transfer.
- PFMS and SNA → fund-flow tracking and nodal accounts (⚠️ expansions standard knowledge).
- Frontline cadres mediate last-mile access.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 17 basic section 3 (✅ owner 17). JAM expansion ⚠️ not fetched (Wikipedia page 404).
**Links:** SJ-3064, SJ-3075, SJ-3085.
**Trap:** Treating DBT as proof of delivery; money arriving in an account is not the service or the outcome.
**Mains hook:** Payment rails reduce leakage but exclude people without accounts or Aadhaar linkage; assisted routes matter.
**Back:** *"Jan Dhan, Aadhaar, Mobile carry the cash."*

### Flashcard SJ-3085 — Census 2027 caste enumeration and first-phase houselisting
**Front:** When did CCPA approve caste enumeration in the Census 2027, and what is the first-phase houselisting window?
**Definition (learn):** The Cabinet Committee on Political Affairs (CCPA) approved caste enumeration on 30 April 2025; first-phase houselisting is notified for 1 April to 30 September 2026. Operations have begun, but no results are available (✅ owner 17 section 8, as at 21 July 2026).
**Breakdown:**
- Approval: 30 April 2025 (✅ owner 17).
- Houselisting window: 1 April – 30 September 2026 (✅ owner 17).
- No published caste database as of owner date.
- Full treatment: basic/09 (✅ owner 17).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 30 April 2025; 1 April–30 September 2026 (owner, as at 21 July 2026; not fetched). ⚠️ Today is 1 November 2026; the window status must be rechecked.
**Links:** SJ-3069, SJ-3070, SJ-3089.
**Trap:** Treating houselisting as caste results. Operations are not a published targeting list.
**Mains hook:** The 2027 caste enumeration could partly fill the SECC data gap, but it is not a targeting list until results and rules exist.
**Back:** *"Approved in April twenty-five; houselist in twenty-six; no results yet."*

### Flashcard SJ-3086 — SECC vintage and proxy-means limits (advanced)
**Front:** Why do SECC deprivation criteria both include non-poor and exclude poor households?
**Definition (learn):** SECC is a 2011 snapshot; deprivation criteria such as housing type, land and assets are imperfect proxies for income or consumption poverty, so they can misclassify (✅ owner 17 advanced section 3; analytical ⚠️).
**Breakdown:**
- Vintage → post-2011 poor missed.
- Proxy → assets and housing imperfectly track income.
- Documents → proxy for identity, not need.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 17 advanced section 3.
**Links:** SJ-3069, SJ-3071, SJ-3072.
**Trap:** Saying SECC measures income poverty; it measures deprivation proxies.
**Mains hook:** Targeting errors come from both vintage and proxy design; a better database alone does not fix proxy limits.
**Back:** *"Old photo, poor proxies: the wrong ones counted."*

### Flashcard SJ-3087 — Outcome-learning gap and political incentive (advanced)
**Front:** Why do governments often fail to learn from evaluations and surveys?
**Definition (learn):** Evaluations are underused, and coverage figures are easier to announce than outcome figures, which may be unflattering (✅ owner 17 advanced section 3; analytical ⚠️).
**Breakdown:**
- Dashboard-survey divergence not investigated.
- DMEO and third-party evaluations not fed into redesign.
- Political preference for coverage figures.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 17 advanced section 3.
**Links:** SJ-3080, SJ-3083, SJ-3088.
**Trap:** Claiming evaluations are absent; the owner says they exist but are not used.
**Mains hook:** Fix the feedback loop: legislated outcome review and budget links, not more dashboards.
**Back:** *"Announced coverage, buried outcomes."*

### Flashcard SJ-3088 — Economic Survey 2025-26 on cash transfers (advanced)
**Front:** What does the Economic Survey 2025-26 say about cash transfers, as cited by the owner?
**Definition (learn):** Cash transfers can improve short-term consumption and food security but do not consistently improve child nutrition, education or durable poverty exits without complementary services and work opportunities (✅ owner 17 advanced section 1).
**Breakdown:**
- Short-term: consumption and food security.
- Not automatic: child nutrition, schooling, durable exits.
- Requires complementary services and jobs.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Economic Survey 2025-26 (✅ owner 17; not fetched).
**Links:** SJ-3081, SJ-3087, SJ-3064.
**Trap:** Citing cash transfers as sufficient social-sector success.
**Mains hook:** Transfers are necessary floors; convergence with services and jobs decides durable exits.
**Back:** *"Cash feeds today; services and work make tomorrow."*

### Flashcard SJ-3089 — Targeting-data architecture reform (advanced)
**Front:** What reform does the advanced owner recommend instead of an all-purpose Aadhaar-linked registry?
**Definition (learn):** A federated, purpose-limited interoperability layer with updated beneficiary records, human-assisted correction, audit trails and non-digital access (⚠️ owner recommendation, not current law).
**Breakdown:**
- Federated and purpose-limited.
- Updated records; human correction.
- Audit trails; non-digital access.
- Outcome-linked Budget allocation; District Collector convergence authority (⚠️ recommendations).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 17 advanced section 4 (✅ owner 17 as recommendation).
**Links:** SJ-3075, SJ-3084, SJ-3085.
**Trap:** Presenting recommendations as statutory design.
**Mains hook:** Interoperability raises privacy and profiling risk; purpose limitation and access safeguards are the substance, not add-ons.
**Back:** *"Connect records by purpose, not by default."*

### Flashcard SJ-3090 — 2019 GS-II Q18: awareness and involvement in welfare schemes
**Front:** What is the 2019 GS-II Q18 demand, and how should it be answered?
**Definition (learn):** Welfare schemes for vulnerable sections are not effective because of absent awareness and active involvement of beneficiaries at all stages of the policy process. Directive: Discuss; 15 marks; 250 words (✅ owner 17 ledger).
**Breakdown:**
- Context: vulnerable sections and scheme design.
- Dimensions: awareness, participation at each stage (design to audit).
- Evidence: grievance and social audit routes (✅ owner 17 answer architecture).
- Counterpoint: consultation without decision power can be tokenistic (⚠️).
- Conclude with participation plus correction.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2019, GS-II, Q18, 15 marks, 250 words (✅ owner 17 ledger).
**Links:** SJ-3091, SJ-3087, SJ-3089.
**Trap:** Answering only "awareness campaigns"; the demand asks for involvement at all stages.
**Mains hook:** Awareness and involvement aid uptake and correction; decision power makes involvement real.
**Back:** *"Aware, involved, at every stage: fifteen marks, two-fifty words."*

### Flashcard SJ-3091 — 2023 GS-II Q17: welfare schemes as discriminatory
**Front:** What is the 2023 GS-II Q17 demand, and what is the answer route?
**Definition (learn):** Development and welfare schemes for the vulnerable are discriminatory in approach; the candidate is asked whether they agree and why. Directive: Do you agree and give reasons; 15 marks; 250 words (✅ owner 17 ledger).
**Breakdown:**
- Agree or partly agree with evidence.
- Named evidence: targeting errors, exclusion, documentation barriers (✅ owner 17).
- Counterpoint: welfare design also corrects historical exclusion.
- Conclude with a qualified position.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2023, GS-II, Q17, 15 marks, 250 words (✅ owner 17 ledger).
**Links:** SJ-3090, SJ-3071, SJ-3072, SJ-3073.
**Trap:** A flat yes or no; the demand asks for reasons and a qualified stance.
**Mains hook:** Schemes can be discriminatory in design (documentation, targeting) and still reduce deprivation; the answer must weigh both.
**Back:** *"Agree in part: design can exclude, yet can also repair."*

### Flashcard SJ-3092 — Coverage trap: high enrolment is not nutrition improvement (advanced)
**Front:** Why is high anganwadi or ICDS enrolment not evidence of nutrition improvement?
**Definition (learn):** Enrolment is an output; nutritional status is an outcome. Owner 17 cites high enrolment alongside remaining stunting (✅ owner 17 advanced section 5 and trap).
**Breakdown:**
- Output → enrolment.
- Outcome → stunting, anaemia.
- Gap: service quality, convergence, and timing.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** NFHS round identity per SJ-3082.
**Links:** SJ-3080, SJ-3081, SJ-3078.
**Trap:** "High ICDS enrolment guarantees nutrition." It does not.
**Mains hook:** Use survey outcomes to test dashboard claims; name the convergence failure behind the gap.
**Back:** *"Enrolled is not nourished."*
